# 15 Full Idea Ledger

> The complete flat catalog of atomic product ideas (WD-IDEA ids) distilled from the legacy documentation. One line per idea, fields separated by ` | `: `ID | domain | tags | section | statement (with ‹src› provenance)`. Statements are identical to the thematic documents 01 to 11, which add framing prose and Options blocks. Ids are stable; gaps in numbering are ideas merged into a surviving id during reconciliation (the survivor carries their nuance and sources). Legacy product thinking, not a description of current behavior.

Tags: `impl` legacy docs describe it as built · `planned` intended for the build · `alt` one option among alternatives · `future` deferred / post-MVP / speculative · `rejected` replaced or dropped · `voice` creator stated it directly · `idea` status unclear · `-` none. Domains: PHIL 01 · FLOW 02 · INPUT 02B · CONT 04 · STORY 05 · ASSET 06 · LOC 06B · SHOT 07 · GEN 08 · AV 09 · UX 10 · ALT 11.

Total ideas: 1885. Tag counts: impl 497, planned 395, voice 326, - 288, alt 269, idea 238, future 232, rejected 36.

## 01 Product Model (01-PRODUCT-MODEL.md)

- WD-IDEA-PHIL-001 | PHIL | impl | Object model | Aiuteur is a web app that turns written narratives into AI-generated short films through a deterministic, fixed 12-stage workflow instead of open-ended chat generation. Its two core promises are cost optimization and narrative continuity. The PRD states the value as automating that transformation through a strictly deterministic, cost-efficient, multi-stage workflow.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-003 | PHIL | - | Object model | Prompt templates are the creative backbone of the product. They encode narrative tone, visual language, pacing, and constraints for each stage, and are treated as versioned creative artifacts, not static strings (see 08). ‹src: tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-004 | PHIL | planned | Object model | Keep one stable vocabulary for continuity concepts across all docs and UI: ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis, location-continuity-phase-0-audit, 5-3-location-implementation-plan› / location*: the narrative space or room identity; / location reference*: a baseline image or description of that space; / location view / camera direction*: an advanced-mode viewpoint on the space; / established view*: an approved frame promoted to a continuity anchor; / continuity base*: an approved or generated frame chosen as the reuse/edit base for another shot; / generation package*: the full set of prompt instructions, continuity decisions, fallback notes, ordered references, and risks for one frame generation; / reference manifest* and *attachment role*: the ordered list of references sent with a generation and the role each one plays (see 06B, 08).
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-005 | PHIL | - | Purpose and core problem | Problem framing: current AI video tools are non-deterministic, expensive, and fail at narrative continuity (inconsistent characters, jump cuts, scene-to-scene drift). They behave like a slot machine where the user pays for high volumes of low-quality options.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-006 | PHIL | - | Purpose and core problem | "The Inversion": plan first (lock story structure and style), anchor second (lock each shot's start/end frames with a cheap image model), generate last (spend expensive video generation once per shot, only to bridge pre-approved anchor frames). This order is the source of both consistency and cost control (see 02, 08). The mechanism is WD-IDEA-GEN-002 (frame prompts and video prompts are separate types) and WD-IDEA-GEN-007 (cheap image model for anchor frames; expensive video model only for the final bridge).
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-007 | PHIL | - | Purpose and core problem | Primary utility is "Story Consolidation and Management": the largest effort goes into stability and iterative control of narrative structure and visual style (Stages 1–5). Video is a result of the deterministic process, not the product's core feature. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-008 | PHIL | voice | Purpose and core problem | Core philosophy: take as much input context as the user provides, refine it down to the Master Script as the single source of truth, then expand outward from that script through the production cycle. Freezing the script as the single source of truth happens at approval (WD-IDEA-FLOW-019, WD-IDEA-FLOW-021). ‹src: Tickets, Input-Methods-Task, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-009 | PHIL | - | Purpose and core problem | "Creative compiler": early stages set global intent, middle stages narrow it step by step, late stages produce concrete media. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-012 | PHIL | - | Purpose and core problem | Let users start from imperfect but useful visual truth and iterate from there, in service of moving fast. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-013 | PHIL | voice | Target user | The target user is the "middle 80%": creative people between two extremes. On one side, too-casual users who want AI to generate everything for them (Manus / NotebookLM-style one-click output). On the other, too-technical users who want granular control of every detail (Adobe Creative Suite users). ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-014 | PHIL | voice | Target user | The target user has a story to tell (jokes, ideas, sketches, short films, animations; possibly a not-very-dedicated animator) but lacks the patience or technical depth to learn professional tools. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-015 | PHIL | voice | Target user | The target user is creative but not maximalist, and cares that the output has a human soul instead of being generic, algorithmically produced content. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-016 | PHIL | voice | Target user | Archetype: a ~20-something creative with some free time who mostly writes stories and jokes (meme and story videos; "part writer" types who normally rely on a team of animators). The product aims to be their team of animators. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-017 | PHIL | voice | Target user | Turnaround goal: finish a piece within a day, e.g. see a news event and publish a parody the same day. Reference point: a viral, Sora-made, AI-animated satirical series on YouTube/Twitter. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-018 | PHIL | - | Target user | PRD target user: independent filmmakers, content creators, and marketing teams who need high-quality, stylistically consistent video prototypes or final products quickly and affordably. README tagline: "built for filmmakers who demand both creative control and production efficiency." ‹src: project-overview, README›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-019 | PHIL | - | Target user | Target users also include power users who already have scripts, and "true animators/filmmakers" who bring their own storyboards and reference material and want to guide generation precisely with it. ‹src: Tickets, master-mvp-tasklist, 3.7-maybe-redundant-stage-9-UI›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-020 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Positioning is a "happy middle" between one-shot AI generators and the Adobe suite. It gives creative people enough control to produce something intentional that reflects their vision, without learning professional animation or editing tools (no months of Photoshop). The product bounces between the two domains to serve the middle 80%. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy, voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-021 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Not an Adobe replacement. Competing there needs a much bigger team and toolset and leaves little room to differentiate. The product deliberately gives up highly technical users who want every detail correct ("this is not the tool for them"). A feature that would only appeal to the top ~10% of detail-tweakers is designed incorrectly for this product. Not capturing everyone is intentional. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy, voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-022 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Not a pure AI-video wrapper. Veo/Kling/Seedance-style wrappers ("Manus with a pretty UI") let the model do everything and yield cohesive-but-imprecise slop. The product does not serve the cheap, easy "let the LLM do everything, don't think about it, make something in an hour" audience. ‹src: voice-message-5-2, post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-023 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Not a slop generator. It is not a UGC/TikTok marketing-ad factory pumping out ~20 ads an hour with no human authorship. The creator sees those products as interchangeable, extractive, and "ruining the internet", in the same category as quick-buck B2B leeching apps, and predicts they will not win. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-024 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Not an agent-takeover product. Any feature that deviates from user input, removes creative control the user intends to exercise, or makes decisions the user didn't sanction is out of scope, even if such features are popular or trendy. Full autonomous master mode is questioned rather than ruled out (WD-IDEA-PHIL-026); this rule targets only automation the user did not sanction. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-025 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Contrarian thesis: people making art and short films with new video models, who want work with a human soul, do not actually want the agent to take over. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-026 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | The creator questions a fully autonomous "master mode, let AI cook": if the AI does everything, why not just use a general agent like Manus? Full hands-off generation is not the differentiator, though the note marks it "NOT OUT [OF] SCOPE" (questioned, not ruled out). ‹src: Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-027 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Precision is optional. Users may be more precise if they want, but the main value is saving them time. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-028 | PHIL | voice | Positioning: what it is and is not | Explicit tradeoff: perfect continuity and accuracy are not the goal (that takes professional tools). The goal is quick, funny, true to the creator's intent, and not slop. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-029 | PHIL | - | Positioning: what it is and is not | Visual feel: a cinematic, editorial dark theme inspired by editing suites (Resolve, Premiere). Complex AI film production should feel like elegant professional editing software while staying intuitive (see 10). ‹src: ui-and-theme-rules›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-030 | PHIL | - | Positioning: what it is and is not | The product is desktop-first; mobile responsiveness is low priority. ‹src: missing_todos-not-sure›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-031 | PHIL | future | Positioning: what it is and is not | Long-term ambition: a "world-class AI film generation platform" with professional-grade features comparable to industry tools, ready for beta users and marketing. Read as an output-quality aspiration from the v1.1 roadmap, not a commitment to Adobe-style granular control, which WD-IDEA-PHIL-021 rules out. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist, MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-032 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Making a film should take some time and some effort, but the effort must never feel hampering. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-033 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Fast-flowing and never friction-inducing: users move through ideas quickly, never feel stuck or bogged down, and never hit a moment where they want to stop because a step is too hard or tedious. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-034 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Flexible pacing: users speed through the parts they don't care about and slow down to get precise on the parts they do. Recurring expressions are user-selectable "care" modes, e.g. for locations and for shot/camera complexity (see 06B, 07). Location-specific care modes (speed vs precision, low cost vs budget) are WD-IDEA-LOC-025. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-035 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Agentic features exist to accelerate the low-care tasks the user doesn't care about, never to replace the user's creative judgment. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-036 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Highly technical tools, canvases, and workspaces exist for when the user wants to get specific on details they care about, but are never imposed by default. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-037 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Later stages should be flexible and self-contained. A user reviewing work in a late stage should be able to fix things there instead of navigating back to an earlier stage (see 10). Where the fix belongs to an upstream stage, repair links route the user back to the owning stage (WD-IDEA-FLOW-063) instead of editing that owner from the late stage; fix-in-place applies to fixes the current stage owns. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-3›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-038 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Stage 8 exists to add "final polish" to the assets the user cares about. Assets the user does not care about must never block progress (see 06). ‹src: Tickets, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-040 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Asset-upload end goal: the user ends with an image they like and a description that matches that image (see 06). ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-1-enhanced-upload-modal›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-041 | PHIL | voice | Experience principles | Within-scene changes (a face gets bloodied, someone changes clothes, characters move to another room) must not require the user to hand-supply new asset and background images per shot. That tedium erases the time savings that justify the product (see 04). The location-level instance of this principle is WD-IDEA-CONT-100. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-042 | PHIL | - | Experience principles | First-time user experience is critical for retention and word-of-mouth growth (see 10). ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1, missing_todos-not-sure›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-043 | PHIL | - | Determinism, explicitness, and control | "Determinism over magic": inheritance rules are explicit and inspectable, and prompts never accumulate hidden content. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-044 | PHIL | - | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Small changes in early stages must have a deterministic effect on later stages, while controlled local overrides stay possible. ‹src: inheritance_contract, tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-045 | PHIL | - | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Every AI step receives an explicit, typed, versioned context package assembled deterministically from saved project state. No AI step "remembers" earlier outputs; everything it knows is passed in explicitly (see 08, 04).
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-047 | PHIL | impl | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Immutable by default: a completed stage is never changed in place. Changes create new versions or branches, and every historical state stays queryable.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-048 | PHIL | impl | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Explicit inheritance tracking: every artifact records what it was derived from, so its lineage can always be traced.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-049 | PHIL | impl,planned | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Projects are meant to evolve non-linearly through branching ("Story Timelines") with a full audit trail. Each branch has a name, a parent branch, and a commit-style message. The branch data model exists; the branch UI (WD-IDEA-UX-083) is planned and full git-style branching (WD-IDEA-UX-090) is deferred.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-051 | PHIL | voice | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Creator belief: one-shot/few-shot reference examples have more potential than retrieval (RAG). This is consistent with explicitly chosen style capsules replacing retrieval (see 06). How well models interpret a sample clip and the user's request needs extensive testing. Retrieval was dropped for style (WD-IDEA-ASSET-007) and is deferred for very large lore universes (WD-IDEA-STORY-127). ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-052 | PHIL | - | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Every future technical direction should preserve explicitness, debuggability, and the user's creative control. ‹src: tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-053 | PHIL | future | Determinism, explicitness, and control | Recorded violations of the inheritance contract (user overrides of inherited values) could power analytics on "where users fight the system". ‹src: database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-054 | PHIL | - | Cost and quality | Cost awareness is core: regeneration cost is meant to be computed in advance at every stage transition (see 10). The cost formula is WD-IDEA-UX-071; invalidation warnings that show affected work are WD-IDEA-UX-044 and WD-IDEA-UX-045.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-055 | PHIL | - | Cost and quality | Cost-conscious design: let users cut what they don't need before expensive generation, avoid redundant AI calls, and never spend credits regenerating content that upstream changes have already invalidated.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-056 | PHIL | voice | Cost and quality | The default experience should minimize the number of generations required. ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-057 | PHIL | planned | Cost and quality | Cost management: track the cost of every real AI call, estimate and budget costs, recommend cost optimizations, and alert on excessive usage. Per-operation dashboards, per-user reports, budget alerts, and anomaly detection are future (WD-IDEA-GEN-268). ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-058 | PHIL | - | Cost and quality | Technical accuracy is meant to be verified at every stage before credits are spent on expensive video generation. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-060 | PHIL | - | Cost and quality | Success metrics for AI output: completeness (all required fields filled), accuracy (matches input and project parameters), consistency (character/asset continuity across stages), and technical compliance (correct formatting and model requirements). ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-061 | PHIL | - | Cost and quality | Three canonical "golden" test projects span the product's range: a simple two-person dialogue scene (narrative structure, character continuity), a complex multi-scene action heist (visual complexity, continuity, technical shot breakdown), and a 15-minute podcast-to-video (audio-driven visuals, timestamp sync). ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-062 | PHIL | - | Ownership and trust | Projects, branches, and the global asset library are private to the owning user. Locks record who locked a stage so future multi-user workflows can be supported. ‹src: architecture-and-rules, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-063 | PHIL | idea | Ownership and trust | AI provider keys are held by the platform: they never reach the browser and are never logged. Each provider is isolated so a fault in one generation path cannot leak cost into another. ‹src: security-credit-rate-limiting-strategy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-065 | PHIL | - | Streamline: the second front door | Streamline targets users who do not come for the writing tools and want to reach production faster. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-066 | PHIL | planned | Streamline: the second front door | Product promise: "Drop in what you already have. Aiuteur figures out what it is, asks what it needs, and builds the production-ready project scaffold." Users drop documents, images, and eventually audio; the app identifies each item, asks what is missing, and lands them as far into the pipeline as their material earns them. ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1, streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-067 | PHIL | planned | Streamline: the second front door | Streamline is additive: it feeds the 12-stage pipeline and never replaces it. Pipeline structure, the Phase A/Phase B split, sequential stage locking, and per-stage generation/edit tools stay as they are; Streamline is a faster way to arrive at the right point with more work already done.
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-071 | PHIL | voice | Streamline: the second front door | The creator generated many streamlined-input variations and treats the design as open. They asked for an analysis of which variants are good or misguided, plus adjacent or beyond-scope features. Goals: higher user satisfaction, and users who are more productive and output more. ‹src: claudeprompt-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-072 | PHIL | future | Streamline: the second front door | Personal creative fingerprint / lore library / "second brain": persistent cross-project memory that learns a user's characters, writing rhythm, visual taste, humor, voice, and prior work by ingesting past scripts, sketches, mood boards, voice recordings, and videos. Streamline intake can eventually feed it (see 06). Lore library here means this cross-project personal library; the project-scoped character and world lore directory is a separate idea (WD-IDEA-STORY-126), and intake material may feed either (WD-IDEA-INPUT-137). ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1, streamlined-input-process-consolidated, streamlined-input-analysis-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-073 | PHIL | planned,impl | Product-shape lessons from location continuity | Two-layer system: a lightweight default path for most users and a structured advanced path for users who need tighter control. The default optimizes for speed, clarity, and momentum; the advanced path optimizes for precision and repeatability. The concrete layered model is WD-IDEA-LOC-017 (baseline, advanced, and optional blocking layers); shot-level blocking references are WD-IDEA-SHOT-099. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis, 7-19-location-stabilization-master-plan›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-078 | PHIL | - | Product-shape lessons from location continuity | Treat text as supporting metadata and canonical IDs as the core join key. Moving truth from text heuristics to IDs early is the fastest path to a great tool. ‹src: 4-15-location-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-079 | PHIL | planned | Product-shape lessons from location continuity | Success for the default layer: users do not feel trapped in setup, can make progress with imperfect structure, and see continuity improve through reuse of output, not only through preplanning. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-080 | PHIL | planned | Product-shape lessons from location continuity | Success for the advanced layer: repeated angles and reverse angles become predictable, users can repair or extend coverage with explicit tools, and continuity quality scales with effort instead of collapsing under complexity. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-081 | PHIL | rejected | Product-shape lessons from location continuity › Options: overall shape of the location continuity system | Finish the original advanced location system exactly as first imagined and make it the main experience. Replaced by the two-layer synthesis after a product-shape problem emerged during implementation. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-082 | PHIL | rejected | Product-shape lessons from location continuity › Options: overall shape of the location continuity system | Abandon the structured location system and rely only on prompts for background continuity. Replaced by the two-layer synthesis. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-083 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | North Star test, applied before adding, keeping, or shipping any feature: ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy› / Would the middle-80% user actually want this, or is it for the top-10% hyper-detail-oriented crowd? / Does it add creative speed and flow, or introduce friction, confusion, or decision fatigue? / Does it preserve human authorship, or let the agent overtake the user's creative intent? / Does it focus the product, or add noise that obscures what Aiuteur does?
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-084 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | Too many features, even if they all work well, create confusion and obscure what the product provides. The product cannot be all things to all people; hold back new tickets until they are checked against the mission. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-085 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | Build one product for as many middle-ground users as possible, not different products shaped by individual team members' personal ideals of what is useful to them. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-086 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | The written mission is the source-of-truth anchor for feature decisions. It stops ideation from going down rabbit holes that sound good in theory but stray from what users are provided; new specs are scrutinized against it. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-087 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | Team decision process: each member independently reviews all major open tickets and arrives with a real stance. Independent agreement lets the team move fast and raises confidence; disagreements are debated from knowledge and conviction; nodding along without thought adds no value. ‹src: post-Aiuteur Poduct Mission & Philosophy›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-088 | PHIL | planned | Product discipline and roadmap | Vision narratives must label which scenarios are core and which are future, so aspirational sections are not read as commitments. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-089 | PHIL | - | Product discipline and roadmap | Development philosophy: feature-completion first. Get the full pipeline working end to end with real AI providers, then systematically improve quality, then add advanced features. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-090 | PHIL | - | Product discipline and roadmap | Each stage's quality standard is "do it as well as possible", favoring correct behavior over speed; connectivity across stages still takes priority over perfection. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-091 | PHIL | voice | Product discipline and roadmap | Sequencing: investigate and verify the existing generation and inheritance chains first, then build new features on top of infrastructure that works. The production-level form is WD-IDEA-FLOW-132 (fix asset reference inheritance before amplifying prompts with chaining). ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-0-investigation›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-093 | PHIL | - | Product discipline and roadmap | Legacy docs describe end-to-end user journeys verified with manual test guides, and automated checks after every change to catch regressions. ‹src: unit-test-system›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-094 | PHIL | planned,future | Product discipline and roadmap | Roadmap priority: the core 12-stage pipeline is the critical path for launch. After launch, version control, polish, performance, and monitoring matter most for production stability. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-095 | PHIL | planned | Product discipline and roadmap | The MVP must let users export finished video; without export the product is not usable (see 09). ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-096 | PHIL | planned,future | Product discipline and roadmap | MVP scope-down: UI polish is limited to loading states, error messages, and keyboard shortcuts. Animations, onboarding, help system, advanced editing, and mobile polish are deferred past MVP. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-097 | PHIL | planned,future | Product discipline and roadmap | MVP scope-down: export is MP4-only. NLE export, asset packages, audio stems, and archival are deferred to post-launch. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-PHIL-098 | PHIL | planned,future | Product discipline and roadmap | MVP scope-down: performance work is limited to basic indexing; other optimization is deferred until performance problems appear. Observability is limited to error tracking ("Just add Sentry. Done."); APM and cost analytics are deferred. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›

## 02 End-to-End Creative Workflow (02-CREATIVE-WORKFLOW.md)

- WD-IDEA-FLOW-001 | FLOW | impl | Pipeline shape | Twelve fixed stages in two phases: Phase A, the Global Narrative & Style Engine (Stages 1-5: input, treatment, beat sheet, master script, global assets and style), linear and project-wide; Phase B, the Production Engine (Stage 6 Script Hub plus scene-specific Stages 7-12: shot list, visual definition, prompt segmentation, frames, confirmation, video review), run scene by scene.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-002 | FLOW | idea | Pipeline shape | Phase A in brief: pick a style with the narrative input, generate a treatment, atomize it into beats, format the script, extract assets with visual keys; the style is then locked and every output becomes immutable global context. ‹src: architecture-and-rules›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-004 | FLOW | idea | Pipeline shape | Phase B in brief: break the script into scenes, generate a shot list, inherit assets, merge shot data and assets into provider prompts, generate anchor frames with continuity validation, generate video clips, then the user reviews and approves or iterates.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-005 | FLOW | impl | Pipeline shape | One account of production-stage roles: Stage 9 writes an image prompt and a video prompt per shot, Stage 10 generates frames, Stage 11 generates video, Stage 12 reviews video with batch render and routes issues back upstream. Most accounts instead make Stage 11 a cost checkpoint and put generation plus review in Stage 12 (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-058). ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-006 | FLOW | idea | Pipeline shape | Separation of concerns: "global context" means the project-wide constraints of Stages 1-5, "narrative structure" means beat sheet and script (Stages 3-4), "scene-local context" means shot-specific data (Stages 6-12). ‹src: database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-007 | FLOW | idea | Pipeline shape | Iteration cost rises through Phase A: treatment and beat iteration is expected and lightweight; once a script exists, changes cost more and more downstream.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-008 | FLOW | planned | Pipeline shape | Crisp stage boundaries: each stage owns one kind of decision, and full editing surfaces are not duplicated across stages (e.g. shot list, visual definition and prompts must not all become location editors, or the product feels fragmented). Phase B is controlled translation, not creative exploration, and each stage's agentic tooling is scoped strictly to its one question. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-009 | FLOW | future | Pipeline shape | The pipeline is built to accept added stages, e.g. a Stage 13 "Audio Mixing" (see 09). ‹src: database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-010 | FLOW | alt | Pipeline shape › Options: how to group the stages | Group by authority in four tiers: Global Intent (1-3: narrative intent, tonal constraints, visual philosophy, high-level pacing); Narrative Structure (4-6: master script, beat sheets, act-level pacing); Scene & Shot Specialization (7-10: shot lists, scene visual descriptions, character blocking and emphasis); Media Realization (11-12: image and video prompts, render jobs). Its stage numbers disagree with the canonical list (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-001). ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-011 | FLOW | idea | Entry, navigation and wrap | Entry is a project dashboard: view projects and their progress, resume one, or start a new project through an input mode (intake detail in 02B).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-012 | FLOW | idea | Entry, navigation and wrap | Global tools (style capsule library, asset library, version history, artifact vault) sit in persistent navigation and are never required to move through the pipeline.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-013 | FLOW | impl,planned | Entry, navigation and wrap | Reopening or refreshing a project lands on the exact stage last worked on, never auto-advanced one ahead or behind. This covers partially finished stages, locked stages and production-cycle scenes. The saved position is checked against real status: an unreachable Phase A stage redirects to the furthest allowed stage, a scene stage with no scene falls back to the shot list, and scene progress is rebuilt from actual lock state.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-014 | FLOW | idea | Entry, navigation and wrap | Project wrap/export follows completion; its mechanics are left open on purpose: downloadable video, structured project artifacts, formats for external editing or delivery (see 09). A detailed Project Wrap spec (editor-ready package, NLE timeline descriptors, manifest, checksums, README) is recorded in WD-IDEA-AV-065 to AV-076; this bullet does not decide its status. ‹src: user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-015 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Stage 1 offers four input modes (Expansion, Condensation, Transformation, Script Skip) plus project parameters; detail in 02B. ‹src: user-flow, current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-017 | FLOW | idea | Phase A: handoffs and gates | The user picks one treatment variant and iterates on it; only that variant's prose flows into beat extraction.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-018 | FLOW | idea | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Beat sheet confirmation is the primary structural lock: no script is generated until beats are confirmed.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-019 | FLOW | idea | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Master script approval freezes the narrative as the single source of truth for Phase B and must precede asset extraction.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-021 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Approving Stage 4 is the Phase A to B transition: the script is split into scenes deterministically (no AI), each with a stable identity, and those scenes become Phase B's production units. Each scene keeps its heading and its own script excerpt.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-022 | FLOW | planned | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Scenes are saved at script approval already populated with their expected characters, location and props, so Stage 5 extraction and later scene stages read ready dependencies instead of re-detecting them; manual additions stay possible (see 05, 06). The pre-approval preview of extracted scenes is WD-IDEA-STORY-050.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-023 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Stage 5 opens with a visual style lock (style capsule or manual visual tone); asset extraction and image generation stay unavailable until the style is locked (see 06). The locked style is a global constraint for all later image and video generation; example styles include Neo-Noir, Pixar Animation, and Hyper-realistic VFX.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-024 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Stage 5 gate into Phase B: a visual style is selected, at least one asset is active, and every active (required) master asset has a confirmed image key; confirmation soft-locks assets and locks style for production (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-025).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-025 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Stage 5 ends with a "Finalize Assets" action (not "Lock All Assets and Proceed", so users do not expect rendering to start; see WD-IDEA-ASSET-110) that moves the user on to the Script Hub, with a visible transition state. Stage 5 assets use a soft lock and stay editable (WD-IDEA-UX-036); each project asset can also be locked individually during Stage 5.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-026 | FLOW | impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Location views are built mainly at Stage 5 but are not a gate: the user may define all views, only an establishing shot, or none; Stage 7 infers placeholder directions when none exist and views can be added later from Stage 8, so skipping view setup never dead-ends (see 06B).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-027 | FLOW | idea | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Pipeline-wide quality gates: valid image keys for all assets before Phase B; shot list covers the full scene duration before approval; continuity issues resolved at checkout; final video meets technical specs. This is the strict form: continuity warnings are advisory first, and strict enforcement is optional for advanced continuity or high-value projects (WD-IDEA-LOC-226); a hard checkout gate is only the alternative in WD-IDEA-UX-305. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-028 | FLOW | idea | Phase A: handoffs and gates | Edit scope: Phase A edits trigger global invalidation (every downstream stage is marked outdated, never deleted); a new branch is forced only when the master script is regenerated from edited beats (WD-IDEA-UX-077), while other Phase A edits stay on the current branch with a soft branch suggestion (WD-IDEA-UX-079). Phase B edits invalidate the current scene and, for continuity-breaking changes, later scenes (WD-IDEA-UX-045).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-029 | FLOW | alt,impl | Phase A: handoffs and gates › Options: how strict is stage order | Strict order: a stage cannot start or lock until its predecessor is locked (or invalidated); any reached stage can be revisited, pending (unreached) stages cannot be clicked, and later scene stages stay locked until earlier ones finish.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-030 | FLOW | alt,planned | Phase A: handoffs and gates › Options: how strict is stage order | Flexible order: strict chains ("Stage 2 can't lock until Stage 1 is locked") create dead ends; allow some freedom in lock order, give actionable errors, auto-resolve simple lock chains, and remove the sequential-lock error messages. Conflicts with the sequential-locking clause in WD-IDEA-FLOW-072 (Streamline keeps sequential locking); this alternative stays open.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-031 | FLOW | planned | Phase A: handoffs and gates › Options: how strict is stage order | Whatever the order rule, prerequisites hold for every transition, imported content included: a later stage (e.g. an imported, locked Stage 4) cannot be committed while earlier stages are still pending, so downstream stages never look ready with incomplete inherited context. Enforcement must not depend on client-side routing alone. ‹src: streamline-implementation-findings›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-032 | FLOW | idea,voice | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Phase B is a repeated per-scene cycle entered from the hub and resumable later; when a scene completes, its end state is saved and passed to the next scene as its "previous scene" (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-033 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Scene order is free: work scenes in sequence, skip ahead, return to earlier ones, or branch before a high-impact change.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-034 | FLOW | voice,impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | The Script Hub is a navigation and overview hub with no lock state and no Unlock & Edit; it lists every scene with its status (refreshed periodically) and is the entry into scene production. ‹src: describing-ideal-locking-behavior›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-035 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Hub actions per scene: open its pipeline, jump to a specific stage, defer or restore it, review its finished video.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-036 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Expected hub content: each scene's slug and summary; no dependency warnings on first scenes; continuity warnings on later scenes tied to earlier scenes' end states; for audio-driven projects, each scene's audio timestamps and transcript. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-037 | FLOW | voice,alt | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Revamp the hub, where nothing is edited, with tabs that show the "master ULTRA detailed script". ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-038 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Entering a scene resumes at its first stage that is neither locked nor outdated, or at video review if everything is done.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-039 | FLOW | voice,impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Free re-entry: returning from the hub to a scene finished through Stage 11, the user jumps straight to any completed stage from the stage sidebar (read-only browsing) without re-locking 7, 8, 9 one by one or waiting on no-op saves; nobody is forced to unlock and re-lock just to pass through finished stages. Return resumes at the last position (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-013). ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist, describing-ideal-locking-behavior›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-040 | FLOW | impl,planned | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Scene deferral: a scene can be sidelined in the hub (greyed out, ignored as if deleted) while its data is kept and it can be restored; deleting a scene's last shot offers this instead (see 07).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-041 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Batch operations skip deferred scenes.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-042 | FLOW | voice | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle | Runtime control: the project's target length is respected (a 2-3 minute project producing 6 scenes of 6-10 eight-second shots is unwanted); an on-screen counter shows how far the project is ballooning, and the user may still extend on purpose when the story needs it. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-043 | FLOW | alt,impl | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: tracking scene progress | Explicit scene lifecycle: draft (shot list) → shot list ready (visual definition) → frames locked (frames) → video complete (done), with side states outdated and continuity broken. Scene status labels include "shot list ready" and "shot list locked".
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-044 | FLOW | alt | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: tracking scene progress | Derived progress: infer a scene's position from which artifacts exist (shots exist = shot list stage or later, frames exist = frame stage or later, videos exist = final stage). ‹src: project-cards-in-dashboard-Ui-issue›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-045 | FLOW | voice,planned | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: when a project counts as "in production" | Requirement: a project is "in production" only once Stage 5 has been locked at least once. Existing scenes are not a valid signal, because scenes are created at Stage 4; a project still on Stage 5 shows "Stage 5 of 5". ‹src: project-cards-in-dashboard-Ui-issue›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-046 | FLOW | alt | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: when a project counts as "in production" | Signal (a): Stage 5 has ever been locked (recommended; survives later stages becoming outdated).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-047 | FLOW | alt | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: when a project counts as "in production" | Signal (b): any Stage 6+ activity, or any scene past draft.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-048 | FLOW | alt | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: when a project counts as "in production" | Signal (c): a one-time project-level "entered production at" timestamp set when Stage 5 is first locked.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-049 | FLOW | rejected | Phase B: Script Hub and the scene cycle › Options: when a project counts as "in production" | Requiring all five Phase A stages to be currently locked was dropped: re-editing an upstream stage marks downstream stages outdated, which would hide real production progress.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-050 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: handoffs and gates | Stage 7 for Scene N receives the global context, Scene N's content and the beats it covers, and Scene N-1's end state.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-051 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: handoffs and gates | The shot list is locked before scene asset work (Stage 8) begins.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-052 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: handoffs and gates | Stage 8 never opens on a blocking "No Assets Defined Yet" screen: scene assets auto-populate on entry because dependencies are known from the script and master assets are locked at Stage 5 (Scene 1 bootstraps from locked project assets matched by name). Stage 8 auto-populates once per scene entry with a "Populating assets..." indicator; a genuinely empty scene shows the normal layout with an empty list and add actions.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-053 | FLOW | alt,impl | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | Every scene asset needs a visual reference; the block message says how many are missing.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-054 | FLOW | alt,planned | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | No scene image is required per asset; assets may proceed on their master reference alone.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-055 | FLOW | alt,planned | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | Tiered: primary characters must be generated, secondary props and locations may use master references, non-critical assets get "Generate Later", and the user can override to force generation (fallback order and proceed-anyway handling in 06).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-056 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | The user reviews and edits the full prompt set (frame prompts and video prompts) before any frame is generated; Stage 9 itself generates nothing.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-057 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | Stage 10 gate: every frame approved and no strict continuity check blocking (start-frame-only projects skip end frames; see 08).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-058 | FLOW | impl | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | Stage 11 is a checkpoint, not an iteration stage: the user confirms the scene and its cost before video generation; Stage 12 is where the final video is reviewed. Checkout shows shot cards with frame and prompt status (start and end frame pairs, final prompts), warnings (unapproved frames, mismatch with the prior scene, dependency warnings), and the render model variant with a cost breakdown and total credits; it has no agentic tooling. The user can expand shot details or return to frames before confirming; confirming queues one video job per shot (see WD-IDEA-UX-302).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-059 | FLOW | idea | Phase B: handoffs and gates › Options: what Stage 8 requires before moving on | Stage 12 resolves a scene one of three ways: / Complete Scene is terminal: it marks the scene video-complete, a final status that invalidates nothing, and returns to the hub; if some jobs failed it still completes, with a warning. / Minor visual issue: back to frames, regenerate anchors, re-queue video (credits charged again). / Major narrative or audio issue: back to the shot list, which invalidates that scene's Stage 8-12 work and may cascade through assets, prompts and frames.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-060 | FLOW | idea | Correction loops | Downstream correction: e.g. after spotting dialogue-timing problems in Scene 3's video review, the user returns to that scene's shot list, makes targeted edits and re-runs Stages 8-12; later scenes are flagged as possibly outdated but not blocked. The general non-blocking rule for outdated scenes is WD-IDEA-UX-057. ‹src: user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-061 | FLOW | idea | Correction loops | After a shot-list edit, downstream AI steps re-run in order: asset relevance, then scene visual states (only where descriptions changed), then frame prompts; the user then regenerates frames.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-062 | FLOW | impl | Correction loops | Script-mistake repair: mid-production, the user returns to Phase A, unlocks the master script, sees the downstream warning, edits and re-approves, then repairs affected scene stages from the hub. Re-splitting tries to keep stable scene identities; removed scenes become continuity-broken. Stable scene identity on re-approval and continuity-broken removed scenes are detailed in WD-IDEA-CONT-238. ‹src: current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-063 | FLOW | impl,planned | Correction loops | Repair links route back to the stage that owns the fix: when continuity is weak or missing, Stage 9 offers "Repair in Stage 8" / "Back to Coverage"; shot-list location warnings link to coverage repair, whose repair set is WD-IDEA-LOC-212; frame promotion links back into advanced continuity context.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-064 | FLOW | planned | Continuity ownership across production stages | Stage ownership map: Stage 7 confirms basic shot-to-location truth (lightweight review); Stage 8 diagnoses and repairs advanced spatial coverage; Stage 9 shows which references the generator will use (transparency, not editing); Stage 10 is where continuity is reused, edited and strengthened over time. Heavy editing UI is not duplicated across stages; each stage owns one kind of decision (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-008). Per-stage detail: Stage 7 in WD-IDEA-UX-185, Stage 8 in WD-IDEA-UX-199, Stage 9 in WD-IDEA-UX-217, Stage 10 in WD-IDEA-UX-232 and WD-IDEA-CONT-210.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-065 | FLOW | planned,voice | Continuity ownership across production stages | Stage 10 graduates from a generation endpoint to the main creative control and continuity workspace: earlier stages get things generally right, and in Stage 10 the user approves frames, establishes frames as views, reuses frames from earlier or later shots as edit bases, and curates continuity across shots. Detail: WD-IDEA-UX-232 and WD-IDEA-CONT-210 (continuity-workspace framing). ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink, 5-1-location-system-intent-source›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-067 | FLOW | future | Script revision mid-production | Script revision reconciliation: push a second draft without losing Stage 7-12 work. Diff old and new script, flag changed scenes, keep untouched scenes, and mark only changed scenes for Stage 7 re-entry: a controlled update, not a fork. Open question: WD-IDEA-CONT-243 and WD-IDEA-CONT-244 treat revision reconciliation as branch work (fork versus mutate); WD-IDEA-UX-077 mandates a branch for beat-driven regeneration. ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1, streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-068 | FLOW | future | Script revision mid-production | Revision staleness has a different shape from the usual forward cascade: a scattered, non-contiguous set of scenes across Stages 7-12 goes stale at once. Its deliberate re-sync must be reconciled with backfill's no-re-sync stance (WD-IDEA-FLOW-089).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-069 | FLOW | alt,future | Script revision mid-production › Options: where a script revision lands | Fork a new branch at the revision point: preserves the pre-revision state, but leaves two branches to reconcile. Revision reconciliation is structurally branch work: forking a new branch and mutating the active branch (WD-IDEA-FLOW-070) are the two ways to do it.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-070 | FLOW | alt,future | Script revision mid-production › Options: where a script revision lands | Mutate the active branch in place: cleaner mental model, but needs a rollback story.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-072 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Streamline feeds the pipeline rather than replacing it: the 12 stages, the Phase A/B split, stage locking and per-stage tools stay; it only lets users arrive at the right point with more work done. Stage locking stays sequential; the flexible-order alternative remains open (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-030).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-074 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Land the user as far into the pipeline as their material earns, with everything upstream clearly marked rather than silently absent; detected inputs map to stage artifacts, and upstream stages are skipped or marked when a later artifact is supplied. Upstream stages read as deliberately skipped, never as incomplete or broken (see WD-IDEA-FLOW-078). ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1› / A finished script lands at Stage 4 for review. / A treatment fragment may land at Stage 3 with Stage 2 auto-filled from it.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-075 | FLOW | planned,voice | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Power-user path: a user with a script goes straight to Stage 4 or beyond (not wrongly into Stage 2); importing a script routes to Stage 4 with Stages 2 and 3 marked skipped (not "incomplete"), and context still flows downstream across the skipped stages.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-076 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Fixing skip routing may be merged with structured document identification and routing, since structured upload builds on skip routing (see 02B).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-077 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Only Stages 2, 3 and 4 are skippable. Stage 1 never is, even via Streamline, because it configures the project (mode, rating, genre, target length); Streamline is a Stage 1 input mode, not a replacement for Stage 1. Stage 5 (assets) is never skipped in any scenario. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-078 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | "Skipped" is a real stage state, distinct from not-started, draft and locked: a terminal, intended outcome, not a hole. It counts as passed for gating (a stage may lock if its predecessor is locked or skipped).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-079 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | The user's current stage is the first Phase A stage not yet passed (locked or skipped); when all five are passed they land on the Script Hub.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-080 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip | A stage can become skipped only from not-started; skipping over existing approved content is refused and the user must unlock it explicitly first. Skip is never a way to discard content.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-081 | FLOW | planned,future | Variant: Streamline and script skip | Skip is a Phase A concept only, never applied to scene Stages 7-12; contextual intake straight into scene stages might later need scene-level skipping, which needs its own analysis.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-082 | FLOW | alt,planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how a skip is recorded | Chosen: a skipped stage is a normal stage version with empty content. Nothing downstream may read real data from a skipped stage; it may only read whatever a later backfill replaces it with. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-083 | FLOW | alt,rejected | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how a skip is recorded | Record skipped stages as project/branch metadata: avoids empty-content confusion but forks the progression logic.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-084 | FLOW | alt,voice | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Locked and not enterable, with a "SKIPPED" badge or overlay showing they were bypassed on purpose (the creator's stated choice). ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence, Input-Methods-Task›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-085 | FLOW | alt,voice | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Marked "optional" rather than locked, enterable so the user can add content manually later.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-086 | FLOW | alt,voice | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Auto-populate backwards: reverse-generate treatment and beat sheet from the script (AI back-fill, costs credits) for a complete pipeline the user can review and edit.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-087 | FLOW | alt,planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Enterable in a third view mode, a read-only placeholder ("This stage was skipped") distinct from both the editor and the locked view, offering "Write this myself" (back to an empty editable draft, no credits) or "Generate from script" / "Generate this now" (AI backfill straight to locked, spends credits). The AI backfill result is marked AI-generated (see WD-IDEA-UX-107). ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-088 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Backfill is optional and never automatic: a user who skipped Treatment and Beat Sheet may later ask for them to be reverse-generated from the script (for documentation, later editing, or before branching); nothing runs or spends credits without an explicit request.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-089 | FLOW | planned | Variant: Streamline and script skip › Options: how skipped stages behave | Backfilled content has no live dependency on the script: later script edits never mark it outdated or re-sync it; drift is an accepted limitation because staleness only flows forward. Each backfilled item carries a static note: "Generated from script on [date], won't update automatically if the script changes later" (see WD-IDEA-UX-111). ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-090 | FLOW | voice,future,alt | Variant: storyboard-driven production | The user uploads a storyboard (even rough "shitty-quick" sketches) as a Stage 1 input, plus written material, and the system builds the plot around the storyboard. ‹src: Issues, truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-091 | FLOW | voice,future | Variant: storyboard-driven production | Storyboard panels labeled like 1A, 1B, 1C, 2A are matched to the same labels in the script or outline, aligning each panel with the moment it depicts. ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-092 | FLOW | voice,future | Variant: storyboard-driven production | In this mode Stages 2-4 are bypassed, and scene breakdown and shot list (Stages 6-7) distill and categorize rather than invent.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-094 | FLOW | voice,future | Variant: YOLO / auto-pipeline | YOLO ("speed racer") mode: the user submits their material (script, recorded audio lines, and storyboard and frame stills if available) as in Streamline; the system then moves through the pipeline end to end like an agent from that bundle, keeping characters consistent (fill-in scaling: WD-IDEA-INPUT-041). ‹src: Yolo-mode›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-095 | FLOW | voice,future | Variant: YOLO / auto-pipeline | YOLO accepts whatever partial production material exists (script, recorded audio lines, storyboard, self-generated frame stills, "look of the film" stills, provided sound) and fills in around it. ‹src: Yolo-mode›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-097 | FLOW | future | Variant: YOLO / auto-pipeline | YOLO is a later automation layer on top of Streamline: Streamline builds the scaffold and routes to review checkpoints; YOLO auto-advances through those checkpoints with fewer interruptions, and only after Streamline is proven.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-099 | FLOW | future | Other workflow variants | Quick Mode vs Control Mode: Control Mode is the primary focus; Quick Mode gets later polish (grid view, bulk actions, flow refinements). Related: WD-IDEA-GEN-189 presents Quick Mode as an explicit frame-generation mode choice; status is open. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-100 | FLOW | voice,future | Other workflow variants | Provider-tailored pipeline: when targeting Sora or another video model, the UI, prompting and context adapt to how that model generates video (see 08). The start-only path for Sora-style models is WD-IDEA-SHOT-029. ‹src: Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-101 | FLOW | future | Other workflow variants | Audio-first projects: visuals generated to match a transcript or narration timeline, with the script secondary to audio cadence. This inverts the pipeline and is a fundamentally different product mode (see 09).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-102 | FLOW | idea | Other workflow variants | Scenario playbooks as structural templates: / 30-second brand montage: 5-6 shots, product and talent references, licensed music and VO replacing native audio. / Vertical social teaser: 3-4 shots of 4-6 s, strong subject isolation, punchy actions, text-safe zones for captions. / Short narrative with continuity: 4 shots (establishing, action, reaction, resolve), identical character blocks, first/last-frame handoffs, character and setting references. / ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-103 | FLOW | planned | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow | The studio is optional and non-blocking: users can skip it and go to frame generation as before; when used, it adds a spatial reference layer without replacing existing stage logic. ‹src: prd-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-104 | FLOW | impl | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow | Inside the studio the flow is three linear views, Setup (inputs) → Studio (3D blocking) → Results (review/export), with back-navigation allowed at any point.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-105 | FLOW | alt,planned | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: where the studio sits | A dedicated step between visual definition (Stage 8) and prompts/frames (Stages 9-10), so frame prompts carry spatial references. The dedicated step is entered per location. ‹src: prd-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-106 | FLOW | alt,planned | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: where the studio sits | A full-screen modal launched from frame generation (Stage 10) for the selected shot, refreshing the scene's frames on close. ‹src: prd-revised›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-107 | FLOW | alt | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: when the studio unlocks | Per scene, after Stage 8 completes for that scene. ‹src: prd-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-108 | FLOW | alt | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: when the studio unlocks | Globally, after Stage 5 once asset extraction has run. ‹src: prd-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-109 | FLOW | alt | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: how the studio integrates | Embed it as a separate app that receives credentials and posts its export back. ‹src: CineBlock-system-overview›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-110 | FLOW | alt | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: how the studio integrates | Extract it as modules whose views the host app mounts and navigates. ‹src: CineBlock-system-overview›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-111 | FLOW | alt | Variant: 3D blocking studio (CineBlock) in the flow › Options: how the studio integrates | Treat it as a black box whose structured per-shot export is the contract, with hero frames used as image references for generation (recommended). ‹src: CineBlock-system-overview›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-112 | FLOW | future,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | The Master Document: turn the screenplay into a super-descriptive production document that becomes the source of truth for every later stage, describing every scene and every moment of change (dialogue moments included) with full character, wardrobe, prop and setting descriptions (e.g. Persephone's appearance, glow, smile, tunic, flower crown, berry basket and the forest setting all spelled out). Legacy framing marks it rejected as an MVP path; the chosen path is deferral past MVP (WD-IDEA-FLOW-117), not rejection of the idea. ‹src: Tickets, injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-113 | FLOW | future | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | Its generation: finalize the master script, check inputs for character, asset, location and action descriptions, then generate the master document from the base script. ‹src: Tickets›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-114 | FLOW | alt | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | Option 1: insert it as a new stage between Script (4) and Assets (5) and renumber later stages.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-115 | FLOW | alt | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | Option 2: replace Stage 4 so the script stage outputs a master document instead of a screenplay; no renumbering.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-116 | FLOW | alt | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | Option 3: Stage 4 outputs both a screenplay for the human to read and an enriched master document for the AI to consume downstream.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-117 | FLOW | alt,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | Option 4 (chosen): defer the Master Document past MVP and keep 12 stages, with the master script still the source of truth.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-118 | FLOW | planned | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: closing the "scripts are insufficient for production" gap (Master Document) | What covers the gap meanwhile: structured shot prompts plus per-scene adaptive asset descriptions. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-119 | FLOW | alt,voice,future | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: other reorderings | Merge Treatment (2) and Beat Sheet (3) into one split-screen stage so they inform each other. Concern: a beat edit made without the treatment could confuse script generation. The creator deferred the decision: fix both stages' bugs and don't merge for now.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-120 | FLOW | alt,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: other reorderings | Move the source of truth after asset definition: the user defines all assets first (upload or generate images, edit descriptions, or derive descriptions from images), then the master script is written from those asset descriptions to produce a meticulous master document. Also: a hidden, backend-only master descriptive document generated after assets exist, only if needed. ‹src: post-tickets, new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-121 | FLOW | alt,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: other reorderings | Swap visual definition (8) and prompt segmentation (9) so shot-by-shot prompts exist before scene assets are edited; the creator notes the two depend on each other, which makes the order hard to settle. ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-122 | FLOW | alt,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: which stage decides sub-locations | In the script itself, so later stages inherit sub-location precision (the creator's lean, with Stage 4). Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-069 (06B), which lists script or dependency extraction. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-123 | FLOW | alt,voice | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: which stage decides sub-locations | At dependency extraction on script approval (Stage 4), so Stage 5 knows exactly which sub-location establishing shots and recommended (not required) direction views to make. Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-069 (06B). ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-124 | FLOW | alt | Proposed restructurings of the stage order › Options: which stage decides sub-locations | At shot-list generation (Stage 7), the option selected in the questionnaire; the creator also felt Stage 7 may need to make these distinctions. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-125 | FLOW | planned,voice | Sequencing principles | Remove the biggest friction first, and make each phase deliver something a user can feel on its own. / Overall roadmap: unblock progress (bugs, locking, navigation), then overhaul assets, then smarter production stages, then continuity and versioning, then polish; deferred nice-to-haves last. / Streamline: real script skip, then intake foundation, then smart routing, then Ask Auteur, then reach features. / ‹src: injecting-org-tickets-into-plan, streamline-master-plan-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-126 | FLOW | planned | Sequencing principles | Production roadmap order after the asset system: prompt engineering (Stage 9), frame generation (10), cost management (11), video generation (12), version control/branching UI, then polish, performance and export. ‹src: development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-127 | FLOW | idea | Sequencing principles | Streamline MVP as a pyramid where each level stands alone: (1) structured upload panel, (2) routing to Stages 2-5, (3) script skip that really works, (4) clarifying questions, (5) review before commit.
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-128 | FLOW | alt | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Infrastructure first: intake data model and file storage, extraction/classification, route planner, real script skip, review UI, clarification, image routing, audio, storyboard routing, per-shot assignment from blocking. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-129 | FLOW | alt | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Visible value first: real script skip (plus a prominent Import Script entry on Stage 1 and the dashboard), then upload panel with review, credit estimate and partial success, then classification, routing and image routing, then clarification, conflict UI and streaming, then reach features. ‹src: streamlined-input-analysis-5-10, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-130 | FLOW | future | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Post-MVP Streamline candidates: storyboard-driven mode, audio transcription and dialogue import, voice sample mapping, blocking-package import, per-shot asset assignment from storyboard or blocking, an "organize my notes" assistant, YOLO, lore library, creative DNA, film sample/reference deconstruction, live-action hybrid intake, remix, collaborative intake (see 02B).
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-132 | FLOW | voice,planned | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Fix the chain before amplifying it: asset reference inheritance first (better prompts are pointless if references never reach the generator), then richer shot-list descriptions, then structured prompts and limits; location multi-angle, non-adjacent continuity and shot asset UI follow independently. ‹src: 3.8-threads, 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-133 | FLOW | planned | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Shot chaining amplifies whatever prompt quality exists, so frame prompt quality is improved first. ‹src: 3.7-phase-6-shot-chaining›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-134 | FLOW | voice,impl | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Verify before building: investigate existing generation and inheritance chains first, write up findings and fixes from each verification pass, and only then build features on working infrastructure. ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-0-investigation›
- WD-IDEA-FLOW-135 | FLOW | planned | Sequencing principles › Options: Streamline build order | Ship additive behavior before replacing old behavior, and add telemetry before strict enforcement. ‹src: 5-3-location-implementation-plan›

## 02B Project Intake & Source Material (02B-PROJECT-INTAKE-AND-SOURCE-MATERIAL.md)

- WD-IDEA-INPUT-001 | INPUT | impl | Project creation & dashboard | Creating a project asks only for a title; all creative configuration is deferred to Stage 1 and the user lands straight inside the new project. The dashboard lists projects as cards.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-002 | INPUT | planned | Project creation & dashboard | Dashboard organization: folders, project search/filtering, and cards that show each project's current stage and scene.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-003 | INPUT | future | Project creation & dashboard | Batch operations across projects: batch project creation, bulk regeneration, batch export, batch status tracking, scheduled batch runs. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-005 | INPUT | idea | Project settings (global parameters) | Global project parameters are configured first, before any text generation, and stay constant for the whole pipeline unless a new branch is created; once Stage 2 begins, edits to them are blocked.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-006 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Stage 1 owns project setup and raw intake: project type, target length, rating, genre/tone, writing style capsule, visual style capsule or manual style description, raw source files, file classifications, and clarification answers.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-007 | INPUT | impl | Project settings (global parameters) | Project type defines the product's range: Narrative Short Film (standard pipeline), Commercial/Trailer (high-impact structure), or Video Content for Audio (a.k.a. Audio-Visual). Music video is also named as a target project type in the example-project plans (see WD-IDEA-UX-314). ‹src: current-app-user-flow, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-008 | INPUT | idea | Project settings (global parameters) | Video Content for Audio: background visuals for a podcast or audiobook. The system strongly recommends uploading a timestamped transcript and generates a dialogue-free visual "story" matched chronologically to the audio's action (see 09). Related: WD-IDEA-INPUT-007 (project type) and WD-IDEA-INPUT-009 (transcript intake). ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-009 | INPUT | idea | Project settings (global parameters) | Expected behavior for audio-driven projects: accept the audio file with its transcript, recognize its timestamp/speaker/topic structure, and set target length to the audio's duration. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-010 | INPUT | voice,future | Project settings (global parameters) | "Video Explainers" as a new input mode or project type: the user wants only visuals for a video essay or explainer, not a narrative. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-011 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Target length is a min/max duration range (e.g. 3:00–5:00; set by slider; legacy range 1–15 minutes) that becomes global context, so treatment and beat generation adjust narrative density to fit.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-012 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Content rating (G, PG, PG-13, M) sets baseline constraints on dialogue and action across all stages.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-013 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Genre/tone is a multi-select (e.g. Comedy, Thriller/Suspense, Drama, Romantic).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-014 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Tonal precision: a mandatory free-text note (min 10 characters, live character count) refining rating and genre (e.g. "raunchy, with playful hints at taboo"), saved as a project constraint in global context.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-015 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Aspect ratio is a project-level Stage 1 setting chosen per delivery platform (16:9 for desktop/YouTube, 9:16 for Shorts/Reels/TikTok; fuller list 16:9, 9:16, 1:1, 4:3, 2.35:1 cinematic). Set once and locked, never switched mid-project; applied to asset images, frames and video; shown in the project header.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-016 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Writing style capsule is chosen in Stage 1 and clearly optional (from presets, personal library, or created inline); if skipped, the AI writes neutral descriptive prose.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-017 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | A visual style capsule is also a project-level global choice made alongside the writing capsule (see 06).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-018 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | The manual free-text approach used for tone should also apply to visual tone: a plain-language visual description instead of picking a capsule. ‹src: Tickets›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-019 | INPUT | future | Project settings (global parameters) | "Story Rules" set in Stage 1 persist and apply to all written and visual generation (e.g. "all characters are eggs; people of color are brown eggs; pets are breakfast items", "entire story set in 1600s Europe style"). The creator flags it as maybe unnecessary. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-020 | INPUT | - | Project settings (global parameters) | Expected quality: the system infers from the input how many scenes the story implies (1–2 for a simple dialogue scene; a multi-scene sequence for an action piece). ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-021 | INPUT | voice,planned | Project settings (global parameters) | Allow user-provided content uploads at each of Stages 1–5 (flagged "do soon"). ‹src: ._1-scratchpad›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-022 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | The user picks one of four input modes, which defines the initial state of the story structure.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-023 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | Expansion: a 1–3 paragraph idea kernel (typed or a single file) grows into a full three-act treatment; solves the blank page (e.g. "A detective investigates a mysterious murder...").
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-024 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | Condensation: large text (100+ pages: a novel, a long script) is summarized and trimmed to a core cinematic narrative for adaptation.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-025 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | Transformation: source text plus a "twist" parameter rewrites the narrative (e.g. "Hamlet in Space") for stylistic adaptation or parody; optional secondary files (parody subject material, research notes) inform the twist.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-026 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | Script Skip: a formatted screenplay is parsed directly into the master-script format (Stage 4), skipping treatment and beat generation for prepared users.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-027 | INPUT | - | Classic input modes | Script Skip may include a structured character/setting definition sheet (fixed template) that pre-defines assets so the AI need not infer them. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-028 | INPUT | impl | Classic input modes | Known gap: Script Skip existed but did nothing; every mode still ran Stage 1→2→3→4→5, so uploading a script saved zero work. This was the most-complained-about gap, and fixing it was seen as the highest-leverage, lowest-effort first win. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence, streamline-master-plan-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-029 | INPUT | voice | Classic input modes | An uploaded script file (e.g. PDF) must actually be readable; failing with "primary file content could not be read" breaks the skip path. ‹src: Input-Methods-Task›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-030 | INPUT | - | File staging (classic modes) | File staging area: drag-and-drop multiple files in any mode; the user designates one Primary Input and tags the rest as Context Files. The AI builds the story from the primary input and uses context files for tone, detail and supplementary information.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-031 | INPUT | impl | File staging (classic modes) | Staging details: size and count limits; first file auto-marked primary, any file can be starred primary; context tags are Character Notes, World Building, Parody Source, Visual Reference (Text), Research Notes, Style Guide, Other.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-032 | INPUT | impl | File staging (classic modes) | In Expansion the typed idea is the primary content and an optional supporting-file area holds context only; the other modes use the multi-file staging area with the primary file as primary.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-033 | INPUT | impl | File staging (classic modes) | Full text of primary and context files is passed downstream without chunking or summarizing. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-034 | INPUT | impl | Stage 1 gating & completion | Stage 1 cannot continue until a mode and project type are chosen, tonal precision has ≥10 characters, and there is either an uploaded file or, in Expansion, ≥20 characters of idea text.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-035 | INPUT | impl | Stage 1 gating & completion | Stage 1 autosaves drafts; on completion it processes the input into a normalized form for later stages and locks itself.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-037 | INPUT | planned | Streamline: purpose & framing | Streamline is a second "front door" for users who already have material (a finished screenplay, script plus character sketches, voice notes, half a treatment). They drop unstructured or semi-structured material into one intake space and the system organizes it into the structured artifacts the pipeline needs. Prepared users should not be forced through stages their material already covers, since the step-by-step path is tedious for them on a first pass; it stays for users starting from nothing (see 01). Related: WD-IDEA-INPUT-040.
