- WD-IDEA-INPUT-038 | INPUT | voice | Streamline: purpose & framing | Expert users need a much more structured input option, channeled through the pipeline, that lets them skip the step-by-step flow and "really fly through". ‹src: Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-039 | INPUT | planned | Streamline: purpose & framing | Build Streamline as a general intake pipeline whose first, best-supported case is scripts, not as a script-only shortcut. Later routing of treatments, beat sheets, images and eventually audio then extends the same system.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-040 | INPUT | - | Streamline: purpose & framing | Mixed bundles are the highest-value path: script + character images, script + recorded lines, script + storyboard frames, treatment + sketches, voice notes + PDFs + mood board, half-finished script + lore notes.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-041 | INPUT | voice,future | Streamline: purpose & framing | Infer how much creative liberty to take from how much was supplied. With only a script and one character image the system fills in more; with script, character sheets, style frames, audio and storyboard stills it follows inputs tightly and only fills gaps. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated, Yolo-mode›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-042 | INPUT | - | Streamline: purpose & framing | "Really disorganized thoughts" mode: the system acts less like a form parser and more like an assistant that organizes chaos into pipeline-ready structure.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-043 | INPUT | voice | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | The creator is undecided on where the Structured Inputs / Streamline UI lives ("IDK, need more time to think"). ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-044 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Option 1, replace Script Skip: the fourth Stage 1 mode becomes "Structured Inputs"/"Streamline" and opens multi-file upload with per-document type dropdowns. Pros: smallest change, clear MVP surface, reuses the "one mode for prepared users" idea. Cons: Streamline is broader than script skip and may hide behind one of several cards.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-045 | INPUT | planned | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Locked-in plan decision: Streamline becomes the fourth mode, replacing the non-functional Script Skip. Expansion, Condensation and Transformation are untouched, and the four-mode selector stays the primary Stage 1 mental model for now.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-046 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Option 2, separate panel: keep the four modes and add a Streamline button/section that opens a dedicated, richer upload panel (treatment, beat sheet, script, images), usable after selecting any mode. Cons: another entry choice, so it needs a careful UI hierarchy.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-047 | INPUT | alt,future | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Option 3, Streamline as the default Stage 1: one unified upload-and-prompt area where every file gets a type label, with the old modes demoted to secondary presets. It is the best long-term "drop in whatever you have" UX for laymen, but a large redesign that may weaken the modes' structured clarity. Framed as a possible future redesign if Streamline proves more natural for most users.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-048 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Recommended near-term direction: a dedicated Stage 1 panel or Script Skip replacement, modeled internally as a general intake pipeline (documents, images, eventually audio); promote it to primary Stage 1 only if it proves more natural.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-049 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Counter-position: the four-mode selector gives blank-slate creatives a "what do I have to start with?" mental model, and a universal drop zone would be a regression for them; Option 3 should be off the table for at least 12 months. ‹src: streamlined-input-analysis-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-050 | INPUT | voice,alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Project-type-first variant: move "Project Type" to the top of Stage 1. If Narrative, keep the four modes but rename Script Skip to "Structured-Input" or "Streamline", subtitled "Enter Structured Docs and Fly Through the Pipeline" (other project types imply different mode sets). ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-051 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Prominent one-shot "I just have a script" path: a large dedicated "Import Script" drop zone above the general intake area. About three clicks: drop file, confirm "this is your script", choose skip vs backfill of treatment/beats, land at Stage 4 review. The streamlined three-click target for a finished script is WD-IDEA-UX-115.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-052 | INPUT | future,alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: where Streamline lives (entry point) | Dashboard "Start from Files": opens Streamline directly from the dashboard and auto-names the project from the imported material (document title or filename stem) instead of asking for a title before the user can give content they already have.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-053 | INPUT | voice | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: shape of the intake UI | Desired Streamline UI: press "Streamline", get rows of [Enter file] [Identify-doc dropdown] [if script: "Skip to Script" Y/N toggle], with a "+ Add More +" button to add more documents in one go. ‹src: Input-Methods-Task›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-054 | INPUT | voice,future | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: shape of the intake UI | "Super Spec'd Out" mode for users who already have character descriptions, a detailed plot summary, a treatment, etc.: a form where each attached file gets a label (Character Descriptions, Treatment, Script, Beat Sheet, Shot Description, Visual Style Description). ‹src: Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-055 | INPUT | planned | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: shape of the intake UI | Structured upload form (ticket version): upload files or paste text; each submitted item gets a type dropdown so the user can correct the auto-detected type.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-056 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: shape of the intake UI | Linear intake flow: open Streamline → drop files, paste text or record audio → classification review table → correct types and primary/context roles → a few clarifying questions → route-plan preview → confirm → pipeline opens at the most relevant review stage. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-057 | INPUT | alt | Streamline: purpose & framing › Options: shape of the intake UI | Spatial intake canvas instead of a linear wizard: files appear as cards, classification and routing annotations appear beside each card in real time, and the user adjusts and confirms in place, "like organizing a desk rather than filling out a form" (see 10). ‹src: streamlined-input-analysis-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-058 | INPUT | - | Intake UI principles | Progressive disclosure: intake starts as "drop what you have here"; the type dropdown appears only when confidence is below threshold, primary/context designation only when there are multiple files; most power-user options start hidden.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-059 | INPUT | - | Intake UI principles | Plain-language validation copy that offers a way out, e.g. "We couldn't find any scenes in your script. Script scenes usually start with something like INT. COFFEE SHOP - DAY... Want to add those, or should we treat this as unstructured notes instead?"
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-060 | INPUT | planned | Upload & text extraction | Structured upload panel: multi-file drop zone plus a paste-text option.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-061 | INPUT | planned | Upload & text extraction | Supported text formats: TXT, MD, PDF, DOCX, RTF. PDF text extraction must actually work (PDFs were previously stored with no text).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-062 | INPUT | planned | Upload & text extraction | Legacy binary .doc is rejected outright with "please save as .docx or .pdf" rather than attempted and silently failed.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-063 | INPUT | impl | Upload & text extraction | Files are extracted one per request, so each file in a batch can be retried or dropped independently.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-064 | INPUT | planned | Upload & text extraction | Partial success: if some files fail to parse, the user proceeds with the rest; failed files can be retried, reclassified or dropped without losing the batch. No all-or-nothing batch failure.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-065 | INPUT | - | Upload & text extraction | File-level partial success is distinct from the stage commit, which must be all-or-nothing (see 02).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-066 | INPUT | - | Upload & text extraction | Store the raw source material and its extracted text for traceability.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-067 | INPUT | - | Upload & text extraction | Unclassified files are kept as context, never discarded.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-068 | INPUT | alt | Upload & text extraction › Options: where text extraction runs | In-browser parsing: avoids a server step, but carries bundle-size cost and uneven quality on scanned PDFs and complex DOCX. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-069 | INPUT | alt | Upload & text extraction › Options: where text extraction runs | Server-side extraction (legacy choice): keeps the client lean and quality predictable; classification happens in the same pass, so text never round-trips to the browser. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-070 | INPUT | - | Accepted source material | Text/document inputs: idea fragments, rough notes, treatments, beat sheets, outlines, half-finished scripts, complete formatted screenplays, novels/large documents, character descriptions, setting/location notes, world-building notes, research notes, style guides, parody or transformation source material.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-071 | INPUT | - | Accepted source material | Visual inputs: character, prop and location/environment images, style references, mood boards, storyboard frames, rough sketches/doodles, existing frame stills, CineBlock or other blocking captures, photos with multiple people to be mapped to characters.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-072 | INPUT | - | Accepted source material | Uploaded images are analyzed; users can name the people or objects in them; image-derived descriptions are reconciled with existing written descriptions. Character/prop/location images route to Stage 5; scene-specific or storyboard images route to Stage 8, 9 or 10 by usage (see 06).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-073 | INPUT | future | Accepted source material | Multi-person photo intake: upload a photo of several people, name each, specify how each should be visually changed, attach or record voice samples, and the system creates character assets from those mappings. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-074 | INPUT | voice,future | Accepted source material | Film-sampling input: upload the actual .mp4, or more conveniently just describe the clip in text (see 07). ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-075 | INPUT | voice,future | Accepted source material | Users can submit audio recordings of their ideas as input. ‹src: Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-076 | INPUT | future | Accepted source material | Audio inputs: voice notes or rambling project notes, recorded dialogue lines, voice-acting performances, audio-first material (podcasts, audiobooks, lectures/transcripts), character voice samples; users can also record directly into intake (see 09).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-077 | INPUT | future | Accepted source material | Audio notes are transcribed, with detection of interruptions, filler and unrelated fragments, and extraction of story intent, character ideas, tone and unresolved questions. ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-078 | INPUT | future | Accepted source material | Near-term audio interpretation: transcribe, classify as notes / dialogue / narration / voice sample, then route notes to treatment/script context, dialogue and narration to frame/video prompting and final-assembly audio context, and voice samples to character metadata. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-079 | INPUT | future | Accepted source material | When audio returns, scope it to "transcribe and route to context", not the full audio-first product mode. ‹src: streamlined-input-analysis-5-10, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-080 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | Each input's document or asset type is auto-detected (script, treatment, beat sheet, character notes, unclassified) with a manual override dropdown.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-081 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | Detection confidence is always shown, never hidden: "95% confident: Screenplay" earns trust; "62% confident: Treatment or Outline — what is this?" turns a silent misclassification into a collaborative question.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-082 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | Streaming feedback: per-file classification cards appear as each file resolves instead of one spinner, so the user reviews and corrects early files while later ones process.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-083 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | One file can be marked primary and the rest are supporting context; the primary flag also feeds conflict-authority ranking.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-084 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | Script detection by structural line classification (scene headings, transitions, dialogue, action), with tunable thresholds: / High confidence: ≥3 scene headings and ≥1 dialogue line, so skip to Stage 4 is offered. / Ambiguous: 1–2 headings, or headings without dialogue. No auto-skip; show an "is this right?" confidence prompt. / Prose: zero headings, treated as treatment/notes, with no skip offered.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-085 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | The same screenplay parse serves detection and produces Stage 4's formatted script, so detection and import share one pass.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-086 | INPUT | rejected | Document type detection & classification | A throwaway "looks like a script if it has INT./EXT." heuristic, to be replaced later by full classification. Replaced by reusing the existing screenplay parser as the detector. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-087 | INPUT | - | Document type detection & classification | A confidently detected script gets a one-line summary: "This looks like a complete screenplay. We'll skip Treatment and Beat Sheet and take you straight to Stage 4 for review."
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-088 | INPUT | planned | Import behavior (as-is vs AI-format) | "As-is" means "no structural reformatting pass", not "byte-for-byte untouched". A file imported as-is can still receive small surgical patches from clarification answers, so the two are independent axes, and the review must show such edits (e.g. "3 edits applied from your answers").
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-089 | INPUT | voice,alt | Import behavior (as-is vs AI-format) › Options: how an imported document populates its stage | Insert as-is (the creator's pick, marked "Recommended"): the exact text becomes the stage's starting content, with no AI and no credits; the user refines with existing regenerate/edit tools. Best for clean treatments, scripts and beat sheets already in the expected structure. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-090 | INPUT | voice,alt | Import behavior (as-is vs AI-format) › Options: how an imported document populates its stage | AI-format: a lightweight AI pass restructures the upload into the stage's expected format (e.g. extract individual beats from a prose beat sheet). Costs credits but guarantees structural compatibility; best for prose beat sheets, messy notes, PDFs/long docs, half-finished scripts, mixed bundles.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-091 | INPUT | voice,alt | Import behavior (as-is vs AI-format) › Options: how an imported document populates its stage | User chooses per file: each upload gets a "Use as-is" / "AI-format" toggle, so power users can mix clean sources with messy notes. Later decided as the behavior: always per file, never a single global toggle.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-092 | INPUT | planned | Import behavior (as-is vs AI-format) › Options: how an imported document populates its stage | Adopted default: the system pre-selects as-is vs AI-format per file from its detection confidence (clean, well-structured docs inserted at no AI cost; messy docs restructured into the target stage's format), always with a visible per-file override. Generated stage content is always previewed before commit.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-093 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | Streamline maps detected inputs to stage artifacts and fills the pipeline as far as the material justifies, skipping or marking upstream stages when a later-stage artifact is already supplied (see 02 for skipped-stage states and backfill).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-094 | INPUT | planned | How intake output maps onto later stages | Structured upload (ticket version, the only new MVP input mode): detect each document's type and route it to the right stage instead of regenerating from scratch. Treatment → Stage 2; beat sheet → converted to structured beats and pushed into Stage 3; script → Stage 4; character descriptions → stored for Stage 5 extraction.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-095 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | Stage 2: a supplied treatment is imported directly (as-is or AI-formatted); notes or long prose generate a treatment normally; a finished script lets Stage 2 be skipped or optionally backfilled. Stage 2 originally had no direct import path.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-096 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | Stage 3: a supplied beat sheet is parsed into structured beats; a treatment generates beats normally; a finished script lets Stage 3 be skipped or optionally reverse-generated.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-097 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | Stage 4: a formatted screenplay imports directly, with scenes parsed deterministically; this is where skip logic and general intake meet. Otherwise the script generates from treatment/beats. Stage 4 should also offer one-click file import, not paste-only.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-098 | INPUT | planned | How intake output maps onto later stages | The skip path must route users into Stage 4 or later, never wrongly into Stage 2. ‹src: injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-099 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | The landing stage depends on what the material earned: a finished script lands in Stage 4 for review; a treatment fragment may land the user at Stage 3 with Stage 2 auto-filled from it.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-100 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | Stage 5 asset creation accepts four input routes together: script-derived extraction; user-supplied character, setting and prop sheets; uploaded images converted into assets with description reconciliation; and manual creation without generation (see 06). ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-101 | INPUT | planned | How intake output maps onto later stages | Two asset mechanisms. Script import reuses the normal Stage 4 lock path: free, deterministic scene splitting and per-scene dependency extraction, with the only credit cost being the AI visual distillation of user-selected assets. A standalone character/location/prop notes file (no scene to attach to) gets its own small AI read that pre-fills one Stage 5 asset draft (name, type, description) for confirmation; it is costed, not free. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-102 | INPUT | - | How intake output maps onto later stages | A script import counts as fully successful only if scene extraction succeeds. If it fails or yields zero scenes, the UI must not claim success: it shows a visible recovery action and records the failure, since Stage 5 depends on those scenes. ‹src: streamline-implementation-findings›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-103 | INPUT | voice,alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: where image uploads happen | All in Stage 1 (the creator's pick): character images and storyboard frames go through the same Structured Inputs flow, tagged (character images for Stage 5, storyboard frames for Stage 10), and the system routes them. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-104 | INPUT | voice,alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: where image uploads happen | Split by stage: text in Stage 1, character images in Stage 5, storyboard frames in Stage 10, each stage with its own upload. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-105 | INPUT | voice,alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: where image uploads happen | Stage 1 + Stage 10: text and character images in Stage 1 (characters are needed early); storyboard frames handled separately in Stage 10 because frame-to-shot mapping is complex. ‹src: planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-106 | INPUT | planned | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: where image uploads happen | Adopted default: all character/location/prop reference images are accepted in the Stage 1 Streamline intake and routed to Stage 5 on commit, reviewed together in one batch grid rather than one dialog per image (see 06). Each batch card shows the detected entity type and name, with an editable type or name override and one editable description; a filename or note naming a script character (e.g. "Marcus") fuzzy-matches that character. Accept or skip applies per image, and a conflicting description is pre-filled by the authority ranking in WD-IDEA-INPUT-124. Heavy per-image actions (edit image, apply visual style, remove background, regenerate) are excluded from intake and handled after commit (see WD-IDEA-ASSET-138). ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-107 | INPUT | future,alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: storyboard material as input | Storyboard and sketch frame routing into Stage 10 is out of Streamline's MVP; it is treated as a Stage 10 feature that intersects Streamline.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-108 | INPUT | alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: storyboard material as input | Storyboard frames can be part of the Stage 1 bundle: classified and stored. If the user maps them to scenes/shots they route to the shot list and frame stages; if unmapped they wait in an input bin for later assignment. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-109 | INPUT | future,alt | Images and storyboards in intake › Options: storyboard material as input | Storyboard upload as a Stage 1 input for quick-storyboard enthusiasts: the system reads labels such as 1A, 1B, 2A and matches them to the written outline/script. This enables a mode that skips Stages 2–4, where the shot list stage distills and categorizes strong storyboard data instead of inventing shots (see 07). Related: WD-IDEA-FLOW-091 and WD-IDEA-FLOW-092.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-111 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | After analyzing submitted material, an "Ask Auteur" session asks a short, targeted set of questions about what the system could not resolve, modeled on an agent's "ask clarifying questions instead of guessing" pattern. It turns Streamline from a file parser into an assistant; answers persist as project context for later generation (see 04). Persistence as a branch-scoped ledger of established facts is WD-IDEA-CONT-037.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-112 | INPUT | - | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | What the clarification layer does: resolve contradictions, clarify vague character motivations, ask about missing plot direction, flag irrelevant transcript fragments, turn disorganized notes into a coherent treatment, and let users choose a branch when several interpretations are plausible.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-113 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Question triggers fall into five categories (contradictions, vague traits, ambiguous structure, missing information, conflict resolution). Contradictions between documents: two names for one character, two implied endings.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-114 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Trigger: vague or unfinished traits the user clearly meant to define (an accent mentioned once, a personality note that trails off).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-115 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Trigger: ambiguous structure, such as a paragraph that reads like a discarded aside ("kept idea or drop it?") or text that seems cut off ("treat what follows as a new scene, or drop it?").
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-116 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Trigger: missing information the pipeline needs that nothing supplied (e.g. no visual style anywhere: infer one, or ask the user to define it?).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-117 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Conflict resolution: when inputs disagree (the script says a character is in his 40s, the uploaded image shows someone younger), the system never silently picks one; it surfaces the conflict as a question.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-118 | INPUT | - | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Example questions: "You mention two possible endings. Which should be the main branch?"; "Is this paragraph a discarded idea, or part of the story?"; "You supplied a script but no visual style. Infer one or define it?"; "This storyboard frame appears to match Scene 2B. Correct?"; "Two different names for the antagonist, which is correct?"
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-119 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Question format: multiple choice with a final "type your own answer" option, never an open text box as the primary interface; few, targeted questions, not a long form; voice answers eventually.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-120 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Opt-in every time and never blocks commit: zero triggers means no session is offered; declining lets the AI infer every open answer, recorded the same way as user answers.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-121 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Questions open only after the whole batch is classified (per-file cards may stream, questions never open early), so a later file cannot contradict an answer just given.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-122 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Answers surgically edit the user's own submitted documents with variable scope: word patch (naming fix, find/replace across documents, near-free), paragraph rewrite (a trait clarification inserted into one description), or structural cascade (a plot answer rewrites or re-splits a section of both beat sheet and script) (see 05). Reconciliation runs once per clarification session across all submitted documents and the full Q&A transcript; each affected document gets a one-line diff summary (see WD-IDEA-STORY-083 and WD-IDEA-STORY-084).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-123 | INPUT | planned | Clarification session ("Ask Auteur") | Re-ask rule: when new material (a later upload or mid-production intake) disagrees with a previously answered fact about the same named entity, the system asks again ("You previously said the antagonist is Marcus. This new file calls him Marc — keep Marcus?") rather than silently keeping or overwriting. History is kept by adding a new answer, never by changing the old one. The established-facts ledger that holds these answers is WD-IDEA-CONT-037. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-124 | INPUT | planned | Source-of-truth when inputs conflict | Default authority ranking: explicit user answer to a clarification question > uploaded primary document > supporting context documents > AI inference (fills gaps only). This ranking applies when inputs conflict and no other rule resolves the conflict. ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1, streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamlined-input-analysis-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-125 | INPUT | planned | Source-of-truth when inputs conflict | Below the question threshold, conflicts may be auto-resolved silently by the same ranking, with the winning value tagged imported (a document won) or AI-generated (inference won).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-126 | INPUT | planned | Review & approval before commit | Review-before-commit is a hard requirement from the first version. Before anything is written, the user sees what was detected and what will happen: which stages are filled with their material as-is, which are skipped, which are AI-generated to fill gaps. They can correct classifications and primary/context roles, and nothing commits until they approve, because a system that silently decides what to do with a user's material won't be trusted (see 10 for cost estimate and route-plan preview).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-127 | INPUT | planned | Review & approval before commit | "Messy pile" walkthrough: a rough half-script, notes for some characters, a treatment that trails off and reference images are dropped together. Cards classify each with confidence; the user fixes one misclassification by dropdown, answers 4–5 Ask Auteur questions, sees which stages are as-is vs AI-completed and what it costs, then lands at the stage the material supports.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-128 | INPUT | planned | Review & approval before commit | In the initial build nothing from an intake session (files, Q&A, reconciled text) is saved until the user confirms; closing before confirming loses the session. On confirm, facts, edits and stage content are written together, so a confirm applies all or nothing (see WD-IDEA-UX-118).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-129 | INPUT | future | Other structured-input variants | Guided "ideal input format" template with sections: Script (Y/N), Plot, Dialogue Rules & Examples, Character Descriptions, General Vibe, Shot Styles, Story Rules.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-130 | INPUT | planned,future | Other structured-input variants | MVP scope: Structured Upload is the only new Stage 1 input mode; storyboard upload and the input template are deferred to post-MVP. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-131 | INPUT | future | Other structured-input variants | "Just talk to me" conversational intake for users with no files, only ideas: they describe the project by voice or text and the system builds the scaffold through iterative questions. Distinct from Ask Auteur (which runs only after material is submitted), though the two should eventually converge.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-132 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Contextual intake from inside production: a user in Stage 7/8 (e.g. Scene 6) opens Streamline to drop newly received material, such as a collaborator's reference photos for a final-act character. The system recognizes where the user is and offers scoped routing to Stage 5 and optionally the current scene ("You're in Scene 3, Stage 8. These look like scene asset references. Add them to Scene 3?") without leaving their place.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-133 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Asset-only intake sub-mode: add one new character or location mid-production (a late-appearing character, a location discovered during production), routed to Stage 5 and optionally the current scene's Stage 8, with no full extraction pass or pipeline restart.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-134 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Intake session persistence: a draft "project staging area" holds submitted material across tab close and multiple sittings until the user explicitly commits it.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-135 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Import templates for series/episodic creators ("start this project like my previous one"): save a finished project's configuration (global assets, style capsule, project type, tone, scene structure) and apply it to a new episode, so only the new script is needed.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-136 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Collaborative intake: director, writer and production designer each upload to the same intake session, each file attributed to its contributor, with the system reconciling overlapping or conflicting inputs.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-137 | INPUT | future | Intake beyond project start | Intake material can later power the lore library: users tag each intake item "project only" or "personal library" (reusable in future projects), built on the global asset library rather than a complex memory system (see 06).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-138 | INPUT | impl | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Location input modes for 3D world generation: Guided (four directional slots), Free (2–8 images in any arrangement, spatial layout inferred), Text (scene description only), Video (one video file), Single Image (one reference, optionally flagged panoramic) (see 06B). Text-only, single-video and single-image modes also appear in WD-IDEA-LOC-265.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-139 | INPUT | impl | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Guided mode has four azimuth slots (Front 0°, Right 90°, Back 180°, Left 270°). Each is a drop zone with preview, a remove button, and helper text suggesting content: eye-level hero; side/perpendicular; opposite wall/reverse; other side/window wall. Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-263 (same four-slot Guided mode with azimuth labels).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-140 | INPUT | alt | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Earlier variant: a plain drag-and-drop multi-image uploader with helper text recommending an establishing wide/aerial, an eye-level hero, and an opposite-wall/reverse angle; thumbnails removable individually. Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-266. ‹src: prd-2-tight, prd-final-enough›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-141 | INPUT | planned | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Once integrated, the directional slots map onto the location asset's existing directional views (establishing = front; other directions = right/back/left).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-142 | INPUT | impl | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Every image/video mode also accepts an optional text description for hybrid generation, and a scene prompt can be composed from the shot list to guide world generation.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-143 | INPUT | impl | Location image input for world generation (CineBlock) | Each uploaded location image shows a resolution badge: green (≥1080p), yellow (≥720p), red (<720p).
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-144 | INPUT | idea | Open questions | Acceptable file-size and token limits for large PDFs and novels.
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-145 | INPUT | idea | Open questions | How much AI parsing should happen automatically before the user confirms anything? ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-INPUT-146 | INPUT | idea | Open questions | Should an imported script stay the source of truth, or may treatment/beat-sheet backfills feed changes back into it? ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›

## 04 Creative State & Continuity (04-CREATIVE-STATE-AND-CONTINUITY.md)

- WD-IDEA-CONT-001 | CONT | impl | Persistent context: global vs local | Context is split in two. Global context is always available and holds project-wide truth: target length, genre, tone, chosen writing and visual style capsules, the beat sheet, the master script summary, and locked master assets with their descriptions. Local context exists only while a scene is active: that scene's script excerpt, its shot list, its scene asset instances, and the previous scene's end state (asset states and final frame). Narrative structure (beat sheet and script, Stages 3-4) sits between the two. Stages 1-5 establish the global truth as immutable by default, not absolutely: a locked visual style can still change later with a warning about downstream impact, and a late change in production creates a branch (WD-IDEA-ASSET-026, WD-IDEA-CONT-034). Stages 6-12 add only scene-local specificity on top of it, so global changes can invalidate local work. Related: the invalidation scopes that realize this inheritance are WD-IDEA-UX-044 (the layer where a change happens) and WD-IDEA-UX-045 (global, local scene, and continuity break). ‹src: project-overview, development-progress, AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-002 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local | No Phase B AI call includes the script of non-active scenes. This prevents drift, hallucination, and context overflow. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-003 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local | When master assets are locked at the end of Stage 5, the system assembles a global context package. It contains the beat sheet; the master script broken into scenes (each with slug, content and estimated duration); the visual style lock (which capsule, and when it was locked); every master asset (name, type, master description and reference image); the project parameters (target length range, content rating, genres, tonal guidance); and branch metadata (branch name, parent branch, and the stage it branched at). ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-004 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local | Every Phase B AI step receives the same global context package, which stays unchanged throughout Phase B production. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-005 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local | The global context package names its branch, so every AI step knows which creative timeline it is working in (see 10). ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-006 | CONT | planned,impl | Persistent context: global vs local | Global context should carry the full asset manifest, visual style locks and constraints, and project-level continuity rules. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1, global-vs-local-context-BIG, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-007 | CONT | planned,impl | Persistent context: global vs local | Local scene context should come from the stored scene-asset map rather than real-time extraction. It holds the scene's dependencies, the relevant asset inheritance chains, and the prior scene's end state. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1, global-vs-local-context-BIG, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-008 | CONT | impl,planned | Persistent context: global vs local | Each scene records which characters, locations, and props it depends on. The scene dependency maps drive the scene hub's scene info (Stage 6) and the Stage 8 auto-suggestions. AI relevance detection remains available as a separate Detect with AI action (WD-IDEA-ASSET-159); whether suggestions rely on the map alone is an open alternative (WD-IDEA-ASSET-162, WD-IDEA-ASSET-163). Extraction is conceived to record these dependencies automatically. Missing assets can still be added manually. ‹src: development-progress, master-mvp-tasklist, injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-009 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local | Dependency rule: speaking characters must be present in the scene. Off-screen (O.S.) characters are also present in the scene, but not on camera. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-010 | CONT | impl,planned | Persistent context: global vs local | When context must be trimmed, recent asset states take priority over distant history, and context size is monitored. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist, MVP-tasklist-v1.1, missing_todos-not-sure›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-011 | CONT | idea,impl | Persistent context: global vs local | Context assembly is one centralized system, not logic scattered across stages. It automatically assembles and injects the right global or local context before every AI call, so the user never has to paste context by hand. This is the implementation side of the explicit, typed, versioned per-step context package in WD-IDEA-PHIL-045 and of the unchanged global package in WD-IDEA-CONT-004. ‹src: missing_todos-not-sure, development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-012 | CONT | impl | Persistent context: global vs local | When several assets get story-informed suggestions at once, the scene's story context is assembled once and shared across all selected assets, so their suggestions stay consistent with each other. Bulk inference is cost-aware: story context is fetched once per scene and reused, and small sets of assets are batched into a single AI call. ‹src: Infer-from-Story, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-013 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | Beat extraction sees the selected treatment, the target length, the genre, the tonal guidance and the writing style capsule. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-014 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | Script generation sees the beat sheet, content rating, genre, tonal guidance and writing style capsule. It does not see the treatment prose directly. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-015 | CONT | impl | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | Phase B runs per scene. When the user selects Scene N in Stage 6, shot list generation receives the global context, Scene N's script content (and the beats it covers), and Scene N-1's end state as local context. The previous-scene end-state input is the mechanism in WD-IDEA-CONT-073, where shot-list generation uses the end frame and character positions so the next scene's first shot connects. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture, system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-016 | CONT | impl | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | Scene asset relevance acts as an "asset continuity manager." It sees the global context, the current shot list, and the previous scene's asset instances (their scene-specific description overrides, status tags and end-state summary), and decides which master assets appear in the scene. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture, system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-017 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | The scene visual-state step sees the master description, the previous scene's description (if any), the user's current-scene modifications, the global context, and the visual style capsule. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-018 | CONT | idea | Persistent context: global vs local › What each AI step sees | The frame prompt step sees the global context, the shot, the scene's asset instances (with their overrides and status tags), the frame type (start or end), a reference to the previous frame, and the visual style capsule. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-019 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Context always flows downstream. A downstream stage never changes an upstream stage, and a stage can never inherit from itself or from a later stage. ‹src: inheritance_contract, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-020 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Context sent to the model is built from layers ranked by priority, highest to lowest: local overrides; scene or character context; narrative structure context; global intent context; system constraints (hard limits). ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-021 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Prompts are assembled in a fixed order: system constraints, global intent, narrative context, scene and character context, local overrides, then selected style capsules. The result is frozen as an immutable snapshot. ‹src: inheritance_contract, tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-022 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Every override must be explicit, limited to specific fields, and logged. An override never silently erases global intent. ‹src: inheritance_contract, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-023 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Allowed local overrides: visual emphasis, camera framing, momentary pacing, and shot-level verbosity. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-024 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Forbidden overrides: genre, core tone, narrative thesis, and style locks. Attempts are rejected or flagged. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-025 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Narrative-structure stages inherit all global intent. They may refine the global tone but never contradict it. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-026 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Scene and shot stages inherit strongly from the global and narrative layers. They may override only local emphasis, never tone, genre, or style locks. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-027 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Media-realization stages have no creative authority and are purely consumptive. A defect in the media is traced upstream and fixed there, never patched at the media stage. ‹src: inheritance_contract, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-028 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Attempted violations (forbidden override, authority escalation, style-lock violation, tone violation) are kept as permanent records of creative drift. Recording them does not block the work. ‹src: database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-029 | CONT | impl | Inheritance contract | Auditability: every generation records what it inherited, what it overrode (as diffs), which style capsules and versions it applied, its parent stages, its prompt versions, which retrieved reference documents it drew on (beats, script, image keys, frame prompts, scene asset states), and an observability trace, so generations can be audited and compared. Every artifact records what it was derived from, so its full lineage can always be traced. ‹src: inheritance_contract, database-schema-state-transition-matrix, development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-030 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Diagnosis path when output breaks a constraint: identify the violating field, trace the inheritance chain upstream, inspect context relevance, then adjust the prompt or the override rules. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-031 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | The inheritance rules stay fixed even when the orchestration engine, media providers, or style-management system change. ‹src: inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-032 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract | Asset context flows global to local and then to scene instances: master assets first, then per-scene asset instances, then continuity tags. ‹src: architecture-and-rules, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-033 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract › Style inheritance | Each style capsule application is explicitly scoped: global-only, narrative-only, scene-only, asset-specific, or combined with inheritance. ‹src: inheritance_contract, architecture-and-rules, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-034 | CONT | impl | Inheritance contract › Style inheritance | Changing a locked visual style shows a warning and offers a choice: mark existing asset images as outdated, or clear them. Marking them outdated sets a "style outdated" flag on each asset, and the user then triggers regeneration to match the new style. ‹src: current-app-user-flow, planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-035 | CONT | idea | Inheritance contract › Style inheritance | Lighting continuity: standardize 2–3 lighting and color descriptors across all frame prompts in a sequence so lighting does not shift between clips. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-037 | CONT | planned | Earlier decisions as durable context | Conceived: clarification answers become durable "established facts" rather than one-off inputs. A branch-scoped ledger records each question, the options offered, the chosen answer, whether the user or the AI resolved it, the subject entity (character, location, prop, style or structure), and which sources conflicted. AI-inferred resolutions are recorded the same way for audit. Recorded answers are re-read on every call and injected into later generations as established facts (WD-IDEA-CONT-038). The ledger's record of conflicting sources and AI-inferred resolutions is the intake-side behaviour in WD-IDEA-INPUT-123 to WD-IDEA-INPUT-125. ‹src: streamline-master-plan-8-1, streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamlined-input-process-consolidated, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-038 | CONT | planned | Earlier decisions as durable context | Conceived: established facts are re-injected into every later Stage 2-5 generation as "ESTABLISHED FACTS (do not contradict)" and re-read on every call. As a result, even a from-scratch regeneration months later cannot drift from an earlier answer. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-042 | CONT | alt,impl | Asset state: separate layers | Canonical identity: each character has a consistency block (name/role, age, ethnicity, gender, hair, eye color, facial features, build, clothing, accessories, skin details) reused word for word in every frame prompt where the character appears. It is copied verbatim from the approved asset description, which is its single source; free-form per-shot wording was considered and rejected because it causes identity drift. Define-once-reference-many by name or alias is the considered alternative (WD-IDEA-ASSET-218). ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-043 | CONT | idea | Asset state: separate layers | A frame captures a frame-specific emotional expression and a static posture "frozen in this moment," kept separate from the character's canonical identity description. The frame-level expression and posture rule is written as a prompt standard in WD-IDEA-GEN-042 (posture, gesture, specific facial expression, gaze, physical state markers). ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-044 | CONT | idea | Asset state: separate layers | Wardrobe lock: a character's clothing and accessories stay identical across shots unless the story motivates a change. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-045 | CONT | idea,impl | Asset state: separate layers | Scene appearance override: a scene-specific edit to an inherited asset (mud on the coat, a new hairstyle) applies to that scene only and saves a new scene instance (e.g. "John Doe (Scene N+1 - Muddy)") with an audit trail. The master stays untouched. The instance's image is regenerated only if its description changed; otherwise it inherits the project version. ‹src: project-overview, architecture-and-rules, development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-046 | CONT | impl | Asset state: separate layers | Effective description precedence: the scene override comes first, then the description inherited from the prior scene, then the master description. Legacy docs describe location views as project-level only, shared by every scene instance of the location (WD-IDEA-LOC-245); per-scene location variants remain alternatives (WD-IDEA-LOC-248 to WD-IDEA-LOC-251). ‹src: 3.7-phase-0-investigation-findings, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, jump-a-gun-discrepancies, architecture-and-rules›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-047 | CONT | idea,impl | Asset state: separate layers | Condition (status) tags record temporary visible physical state of an asset in a scene, such as wet, bloody, muddy, torn wardrobe, torn shirt, or locked. They come from shot outcomes during production, or the user picks them from a keyboard-navigable dropdown. Each asset instance has a carry-forward toggle, on by default, that decides whether its tags persist into the next scene. A scene visual state therefore yields both a description override and status tags. ‹src: project-overview, architecture-and-rules, AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-048 | CONT | idea | Asset state: separate layers | Status tags can be applied only once the scene's shot list is ready, after Stage 7. ‹src: database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-049 | CONT | impl,planned,voice | Asset state: separate layers | Conditions must visibly reach the images. Status tags are appended as visual modifiers when scene-instance images are generated, and frame prompts describe the visual conditions of characters, props, and settings in detail, including damage or dirt. Frame prompt subject appearance comes from each asset's scene-specific effective description rather than only the master description, with status tags as condition modifiers. The scene-instance image recipe is: master reference as identity anchor, effective description as the prompt subject, status tags as visual modifiers that must visibly register, and the visual style capsule for aesthetic. ‹src: 3.7-phase-0-investigation, 3.8-phase3-structured-prompts-and-limits, 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-050 | CONT | voice | Asset state: separate layers | A scene-instance image is the selected master reference regenerated with the scene's visual description applied, and status tags applied in that same regeneration. For example, a normal SpongeBob master, plus "wears a suit" for this scene and a "soiled" tag because he fell in mud, yields SpongeBob in a muddy suit. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-051 | CONT | idea | Asset state: separate layers | Expected state behavior: characters change across an action sequence, becoming sweaty, injured, or determined. Props show wear or activation, such as tools getting dirty or security systems activating. Tags such as "damaged", "activated", and "compromised" apply to these states. A simple scene should need no changes beyond the inherited master descriptions. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-052 | CONT | impl | Asset state: separate layers | Assets can evolve across scenes in both directions, such as muddy to clean or injured to healed, while their inheritance chain stays intact. ‹src: development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-053 | CONT | impl | Asset state: separate layers | Only real changes in visual appearance count as transformations. Emotional shifts and dialogue changes do not count. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-054 | CONT | idea | Asset state: separate layers | Parentheticals such as "(crying)" or "(bloodied)" inform how a character should look in that scene's visual state. Parenthetical delivery notes also feed voice generation as delivery context (WD-IDEA-STORY-062). ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-055 | CONT | impl | Asset state: separate layers | Every change to a scene asset instance is tracked: a modification count, which field changed last, and an optional reason the user can supply for the change. ‹src: development-progress, planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-056 | CONT | planned | Asset state: separate layers | An asset change log records what changed, when, and why. Users can roll an asset back to a previous state, separately from branch-level rollback. ‹src: missing_todos-not-sure›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-057 | CONT | voice,alt | Asset state: separate layers › Options: representing major story-state changes of a character | Option A: a character can have several distinct assets, each pulled in for a scene range, to represent major story-state changes. For example, scenes 1-3 use the hero, scenes 3-6 use a transgender hero, and scenes 7-9 use a trans, wheelchair-using hero. ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-058 | CONT | voice,alt | Asset state: separate layers › Options: representing major story-state changes of a character | Option B: define only starting-state assets, and let the scene asset definition carry changed states as the story progresses. Changed states reference images from prior scene instances. ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-059 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Assets, including characters, props, and wardrobe, keep their state across scenes. When entering scene N+1, each asset defaults to its final state from scene N. ‹src: project-overview, README›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-060 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | State-inheritance rule: an asset seen in the previous scene starts this scene in its end state from that scene. An asset new to this scene starts from its master definition. Assets needing explicit state changes are flagged. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-061 | CONT | impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | From Scene 2 onward, assets are populated in a set order. Assets first inherit from the prior scene, carrying forward their status tags, scene description overrides, and images. Gaps are then filled from the scene's expected dependencies for assets newly appearing in this scene. An instance can also be created from a project asset, detected from shot or script dependencies, or added manually. "Inherit from prior scene" copies the previous scene's asset states into the current scene as a starting point.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-062 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Scene asset instances are pulled automatically into the local context of the current scene. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-063 | CONT | voice | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Legacy docs describe per-frame attachments, including characters, major props, and location direction shots, as already in the asset library from earlier stages and pulled in automatically. The blocking is the only extra piece added to that per-frame bundle. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-064 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Each scene instance records the scene it inherited from, so an asset's state history can be traced back through earlier scenes. ‹src: architecture-and-rules, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-065 | CONT | idea,impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Each scene instance has a carry-forward toggle, on by default, that decides whether its status tags persist into the next scene. Status tags capture temporary physical state such as "muddy", "torn shirt", "bloody", or "wet", generated during production from shot outcomes. So a character who gets muddy stays muddy, and a broken prop stays broken until something fixes it.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-066 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Entering scene N+1 after a scene that set carry-forward tags triggers a mandatory question. The system looks up carry-forward tags from Scene N and asks the user, for example: "Character 'John Doe' has status 'muddy' from Scene N. Carry forward to Scene N+1?" ‹src: project-overview, database-schema-state-transition-matrix›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-067 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | State ownership: the scene asset stage (Stage 8) defines only each asset's starting visual state at its first appearance in the scene. Frame generation (Stage 10) owns visual evolution within the scene. No other stage changes asset appearance. ‹src: project-overview, user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-068 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Mid-scene visual changes are not pre-defined upstream. Later frame prompts derive them from the action context and the prior frame. Over-specifying visual evolution upstream is explicitly discouraged as brittle. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-069 | CONT | idea,impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Each scene stores a prose summary of its final moment and a reference to its final frame, with a thumbnail, so the next scene can pick up visually where it left off. ‹src: architecture-and-rules, database-schema-state-transition-matrix, development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-070 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | A scene end state contains: the final shot's action and dialogue; the last approved end frame; every asset instance's state (description override, status tags, and carry-forward setting); character positions (e.g., "standing by window") and states (e.g., "facing door"); and the final setting. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-071 | CONT | planned,voice | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | After Stage 10, an AI writes a natural-language end-state summary of how the scene concludes. It covers each character's appearance, position, and emotional state, and what changed for props and locations. It draws on the final shot's action and dialogue, final asset states, and status tags. The summary replaces placeholder data in the rearview mirror and feeds continuity validation, which compares the prior scene's end state with the current scene.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-072 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | When a scene completes, its end state is saved and the pipeline loops back to the scene hub (Stage 6) for Scene N+1. Scene N's end state is passed in as the "previous scene." ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-073 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Three steps consume the scene end state. Shot list generation uses the end frame and character positions so the next scene's first shot connects. Asset relevance uses the asset states to decide whether each asset inherits or reverts to its master. Frame prompting uses the previous scene's end frame as the visual reference for the next scene's first start frame. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-074 | CONT | idea,impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | A scene's first shot must visually connect to the previous scene's end state. Character positions and states must match how the prior scene ended. The setting must either match or transition explicitly. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture, system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-075 | CONT | idea | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Each scene's shot list includes continuity notes explaining how the scene connects to the prior and next scenes. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-076 | CONT | impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state | Across a scene boundary, the previous scene's end frame and the next scene's start frame are available to each other as adjacent continuity references. ‹src: 3.8-phase5-non-adjacent-continuity›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-078 | CONT | planned,impl | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | Historical reference carousel: a scene asset's reference defaults to the selected image from the most recent prior scene, so scene 4 defaults to scene 3. The user can cycle through the master asset, then the Scene 1 and Scene 2 instances, and so on, and confirm with "Use This Reference."
- WD-IDEA-CONT-079 | CONT | planned | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | Chain edge cases: the first scene defaults to the master asset, and scenes without a generated instance are skipped. Scene deletion and reordering must be handled. When the previous scene has no instance for an asset, Stage 8 still shows that asset's latest images from earlier scenes as a carousel of past modifications. Any of them can be pulled in as the new reference. The fallback order is scene instance, then master, then placeholder. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1, Tickets, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-080 | CONT | impl,planned,voice | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | An asset's state is an inheritance chain from the master (Stage 5), through the scene instance (Stage 8), prompts (Stage 9), and frames (Stage 10). Final frames should reflect master and scene assets rather than generic output; frames that ignore them read as generic slop and defeat the purpose, and the requirement covers master and scene-instance assets alike. The chain must be verified end to end. Planned tooling includes an asset timeline view showing state evolution, plus inheritance validation and repair tools. A reported bug, where master assets did not influence scene and frame generation, must be fixed. ‹src: Tickets, master-mvp-tasklist, 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-081 | CONT | voice,planned | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | Frame generation must receive the scene-specific instance image, for example the gnome variant, not the original master reference. The creator observed frames whose characters and composition looked completely different from the scene instances, with no master asset influence. Assets must actually be fed into frame generation, because shot-to-shot continuity comes from using asset images to generate subsequent frames. Scene-specific images from Stage 8 must flow through to final frame generation in Stage 10, so frames resemble the scene instances rather than the masters.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-082 | CONT | planned | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | Asset reference images are resolved fresh at frame-generation time, rather than trusted from a list cached at prompt time. A cached list can go stale or expire. ‹src: 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix, 3.8-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-083 | CONT | planned,future | Scene-to-scene handoff: end state becomes next start state › Reference chain across scenes | Each scene instance inherits from the prior scene's instance, forming a chain. An asset timeline view shows the asset's visual state evolving across scenes, with tools to validate and repair the chain. Later, a visual tree of the chain is planned to show an asset's inheritance across scenes.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-084 | CONT | voice | Story-informed scene state | Problem: inherited asset descriptions go stale as the story evolves. They describe the asset generally, not the scene conditions (soaked, torn dress, exhausted) that the script, treatment, and beat sheet already imply. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-085 | CONT | voice,impl | Story-informed scene state | A per-asset "Update from Story Context" button, also labeled "Infer from Story," sits in the Stage 8 main panel. The AI reads the scene's script excerpt, treatment, beat sheet, and shot-list actions and dialogue, plus the asset's master and current descriptions. It proposes an updated scene description and suggested status tags, with its reasoning. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-threads, Infer-from-Story›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-086 | CONT | voice,impl | Story-informed scene state | A bulk "Update from Context (N)" button in the Stage 8 sidebar runs the same inference for all checkbox-selected assets. It is enabled when assets are selected. The user reviews the proposals and applies only the accepted ones. ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-087 | CONT | idea | Story-informed scene state | The inferred scene description covers what the asset is wearing, its physical state, its emotional state, and any change from its master. It applies to characters, props, and locations. ‹src: 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-088 | CONT | voice,impl | Story-informed scene state | Bulk results open in a review modal. The user can accept or reject each asset, and sees current and suggested descriptions side by side, with tag differences marked "+new." Reasoning is collapsible. The user can select or deselect all and apply the selected changes. Assets with no suggested changes are shown as such. ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-089 | CONT | impl | Story-informed scene state | The single-asset suggestion preview offers Accept Description, Accept Tags, Accept Both, or Dismiss. It appears inline beside the current description, with newly suggested tags marked and the reasoning collapsible. ‹src: 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-090 | CONT | impl | Story-informed scene state | Every suggestion carries a short reasoning that names the story context that informed it, helping the user decide whether to accept. ‹src: 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-091 | CONT | impl | Story-informed scene state | Suggested status tags are additive. They are merged and deduplicated with existing tags, never replacing them, and the review interface makes this clear. ‹src: 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-092 | CONT | voice,impl | Story-informed scene state | An accepted story-informed description becomes a new save state in version history, never a replacement; accepting any AI suggestion goes through normal editing and likewise creates a version-history entry. All versions stay cycleable: original, edited, story-updated, and further tweaked. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-093 | CONT | voice | Story-informed scene state | Regenerating the scene-instance image after a description update is the user's choice. The user may dislike the update and keep the earlier scene image, whether from the master reference or a prior generation. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-094 | CONT | idea | Story-informed scene state | Condition inference should be tuned to avoid over-eager changes when nothing meaningful happened in the scene, since those would annoy users. ‹src: 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-095 | CONT | voice | Story-informed scene state | AI detection of new assets and inference of conditions for existing assets are separate concerns with separate buttons. They should not be conflated. ‹src: 3.7-threads, 3.7-phase-2-story-context-updates›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-096 | CONT | voice,rejected | Story-informed scene state | Earlier idea, since replaced: the existing AI asset-detection button would also infer asset conditions, adding status tags and description updates. It was replaced by separate per-asset and bulk inference buttons. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-097 | CONT | voice,planned,alt | Story-informed scene state | Older spec and an automatic alternative to the user-triggered review flow (WD-IDEA-CONT-085 to WD-IDEA-CONT-088), which stays separate. It is not the settled direction. Descriptions adapt to scene context without being asked. The hero's workout outfit becomes a suit at the Scene 4 wedding, and Persephone shifts from a blonde nature princess to a goth alt-girl in the underworld. The scene variant is inferred from the script, the scene, and the shot list. Per-scene variants merge the master description with scene context, are stored alongside the master, and stay reviewable and editable by the user.
- WD-IDEA-CONT-098 | CONT | voice | Story-informed scene state | The creator suspected a basic, unrobust version of context-aware descriptions already existed, and wanted it investigated before building. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-099 | CONT | idea | Story-informed scene state | Intake can supply scene-specific visual state to the scene asset stage (Stage 8). This includes scene-specific asset images, state changes (outfit, injury, condition, props in hand), user-supplied scene reference images, and manual additions and deletions. Assets auto-populate by default, with user correction. Intake routing of scene-specific images to Stage 8 (WD-IDEA-INPUT-072) is the intake side of this scene-state supply. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-100 | CONT | voice | Mid-scene change and transformations | Mid-scene change problem: characters get bloodied, change clothes, move to a different room, or see a room damaged within a scene. Supplying new precise asset and location images for every such variation per shot is tedious and erases the time savings. Each location change would otherwise multiply per-shot location variants. The system should handle these variations largely on its own. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-101 | CONT | voice | Mid-scene change and transformations | Preferred direction for mid-scene variation: the shot's text prompt drives changes such as blood, wardrobe, or room, rather than requiring users to manually update asset images for each shot. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-102 | CONT | planned | Mid-scene change and transformations | Asset state changes are detected from shot action descriptions while the shot list is being built, and the visual-evolution context is recorded so frame generation can use it. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist, MVP-tasklist-v1.1, missing_todos-not-sure›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-103 | CONT | impl,planned | Mid-scene change and transformations | Shot extraction detects visual transformations in the script and shot list, such as costume changes, physical transformations, injuries, disguises, and magical changes, and flags the shot where each one happens. Transformation points for assets, such as costume changes and injuries, are detected automatically. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory, 3.7-tasks-part-1, master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-104 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | There are three transformation types: instant (the change happens on a cut between shots), within-shot (the change happens on camera), and gradual (the change spans several shots, starting at a trigger shot and completing at a completion shot). ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-105 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | An asset can be turned into a "transformation" with transformation events that must be confirmed and completed, such as a character changing mid-scene. Unconfirmed or incomplete transformations block progress. ‹src: current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-106 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | After a transformation, the AI writes a short post-transformation description of 500 characters or fewer, based on the asset's appearance before the change and what happens during the transformation. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-107 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | Given only the trigger shot, the AI can infer both the post-transformation appearance and a short account of what happens visually during the change. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-108 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | Each transformation event has its own post-transformation image. In Stage 8, that image can be generated for the event and regenerated within the scene. ‹src: 3.7-Z-toggle-&-tooltip, pw-toggle-tooltip-results, EnhancedUploadModal-1›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-109 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | The post-transformation image is built from the post-transformation description and status tags, with the pre-transformation image as the identity reference. A "Use as ref" toggle replaces that default reference with the user's own current post-transformation image. ‹src: 3.7-phase-0-investigation, 3.7-phase-0-investigation-findings, 3.7-Z-toggle-&-tooltip›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-110 | CONT | voice | Mid-scene change and transformations | Transformation events should get the same ease of use as scene-instance regeneration, including the upload and edit flows. The creator wanted to confirm whether this exists. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-111 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations | Stage 9 shows a transformation state badge for each shot (Pre-Transform, Transforming, or Post-Transform), with the chain context below it, and both are visible together. ‹src: 3.7-phase-6-shot-chaining, 3.7(extra-doc-covered-in-6)WT-E-task›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-112 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | Per-shot asset presence decides frame references. Assets that are "throughout" the shot appear in both the start and end frames, "enters" assets appear only in the end frame, "exits" assets only in the start frame, and "passes through" assets appear in the video only, with no frame references. Each asset's presence is classified this way, and the classifications drive separate start-frame and end-frame reference sets. ‹src: 3.7-phase-0-investigation, 3.7-phase-0-investigation-findings, 3.7-phase-4-frame-prompt-quality›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-113 | CONT | voice | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | Assets that only pass through a shot, appearing in neither the start nor the end frame, must still appear in the shot accurate to their design. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-114 | CONT | voice,planned | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | End frames often need different scene-instance assets than the start frame, whether through a transformation or because characters enter or exit. End-frame asset references therefore must not simply copy the start frame's linked assets. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-115 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | End-frame references: an explicit end-frame list wins when one exists. Otherwise the end frame starts from the start-frame references, with post-transformation images swapped in for assets transforming within the shot, keeping each reference's role. These swaps are computed automatically from transformation events. The start frame is also an identity reference for the end frame, and without transformations the end-frame references equal the start-frame references. In start-frame-only mode (WD-IDEA-GEN-186, WD-IDEA-SHOT-029) no end frame exists, so these end-frame reference rules do not apply. ‹src: 3.7-phase-0-investigation, 3.7-phase-0-investigation-findings, 3.7-phase-4-frame-prompt-quality›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-116 | CONT | impl,planned | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | Location references appear in both the start-frame and end-frame reference lists, so the background stays anchored across an in-shot transformation. The end frame keeps the start frame's location unless the shot action explicitly changes it. Within-shot transformations swap transformed asset references without dropping the location references. ‹src: location-continuity-progress-audit, 5-3-location-implementation-plan›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-117 | CONT | planned | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | Users can see the end-frame reference set, showing which assets and which images, with the pre-to-post transformation image swap highlighted. Users can also manually override the end-frame asset images. ‹src: 3.8-phase6-shot-asset-assignment-ui›
- WD-IDEA-CONT-118 | CONT | impl | Mid-scene change and transformations › Start frame vs end frame state | The end frame shows the result of the shot's action: same camera position, framing, and characters, with only poses, expressions, positions, and visible effects of the action changed. It is built from the start-frame prompt, the shot action, the shot duration, and the prior frame. Where the action does not state end positions or end states, the end frame infers reasonable ones. Appearance changes only if a transformation completes within the shot, in which case a transformation rule lets the character's appearance change; otherwise appearance stays fixed. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory, project-overview›
