- WD-IDEA-STORY-123 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Alternative ending generator.
- WD-IDEA-STORY-124 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Narrative timeline continuity: story-logic checks, character-motivation consistency, plot-point validation.
- WD-IDEA-STORY-125 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Creative insights on the user's own work: genre/tone analysis, character development tracking, structure visualization, pacing analysis, dialogue analytics.
- WD-IDEA-STORY-126 | STORY | voice,future | Future story tools | "Lore Library" ("Lorerary"): a project-scoped directory of persistent context (character lore, world-building, settings, relationships, history, and world rules) that grows with the project, especially for animated series. Cross-project personal memory is WD-IDEA-PHIL-072; intake that feeds it is WD-IDEA-INPUT-137. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-STORY-127 | STORY | voice,future | Future story tools | Retrieval over lore for premium users with massive reusable story universes (retrieval was cut from MVP); not for style or tone, which simpler methods handle; a well-structured non-vector database could do the same. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-STORY-128 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Content translation: translate generated content, bilingual script editing, translation memory, language-specific style references.
- WD-IDEA-STORY-129 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Multi-language generation: language detection, language-specific prompting, cross-language consistency checks, language-specific quality metrics.
- WD-IDEA-STORY-130 | STORY | future | Future story tools | Script reader view with audio and text-to-speech for scripts. ‹src: Post-MVP-tasklist›

## 06 Visual Development & Assets (06-VISUAL-DEVELOPMENT-AND-ASSETS.md)

- WD-IDEA-ASSET-001 | ASSET | impl | Object model | Three asset tiers: a per-user global library reused across projects, project (master) assets defined in Stage 5, and scene asset instances defined in Stage 8. Asset state flows down the tiers, then into continuity tags (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-002 | ASSET | impl | Object model | Asset types: character, prop, location (see 06B), and extra archetype. Each asset has a master description and a reference image ("image key").
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-003 | ASSET | impl | Object model | A project asset's origin is tracked: extracted from the script, created manually, cloned from the global library, or created from a Streamline image import. ‹src: streamline-plan-critique-8-1, streamline-implementation-spec-8-3›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-005 | ASSET | planned | Object model | The inheritance chain from master to scene instance to later scenes is tracked strongly enough to validate end to end (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-006 | ASSET | impl | Visual style capsules | Style is applied deterministically: the user picks a capsule and the system injects it as text plus metadata into AI instructions, with no similarity retrieval. Reproduction is exact and predictable. The cost: capsules are curated by hand, with no automatic discovery. ‹src: tech-stack, architecture-and-rules›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-007 | ASSET | rejected | Visual style capsules | A retrieval/embedding (RAG) approach to style consistency was dropped and replaced by deterministic capsules. ‹src: development-progress›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-008 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | Two capsule kinds, writing and visual, kept strictly separate.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-009 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | A writing capsule holds example excerpts, high-level descriptors, and negative constraints (what not to imitate). It can be injected into the treatment, beat sheet, and script steps (see 05).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-010 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | A visual capsule holds reference images, descriptors ("minimalist", "ornate"), negative constraints, and design pillars: structured aesthetic definitions covering color palette, mood, medium, lighting, and camera language. It can also hold locked example frames.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-011 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | The visual capsule acts as a "visual style lock" applied to master reference images, scene asset states, and frame prompts (see 08).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-012 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | Styles set early must be injected into later outputs (shot lists, image prompts, video prompts). This matters most between the master script (Stage 4) and the shot list (Stage 7), and across visual refinement (Stages 8–10). ‹src: tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-013 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | Capsules are versioned together with prompts and assets. ‹src: tech-stack›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-015 | ASSET | impl | Visual style capsules | Style audit trail: every capsule application is logged with the capsule version, the stage output and context it was applied to, who applied it, and why.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-016 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules | Quality expectation: writing and visual capsules fit the genre. Examples: conversational/character-driven for dialogue drama, technical/action-oriented for a heist, documentary/educational and clean/professional for a podcast. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-017 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules › Capsule library | User-level style capsule library for creating and managing writing and visual capsules. Preset capsules can be duplicated and customized.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-018 | ASSET | impl | Visual style capsules › Capsule library | Library browsing: separate Writing Styles and Visual Styles tabs, search, a type filter, an all/presets/custom filter, and grid/list views.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-019 | ASSET | impl | Visual style capsules › Capsule library | Preset capsules are read-only; to customize one, the user duplicates it and edits the copy. Custom capsules can be created, edited, favorited, and deleted (with confirmation).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-020 | ASSET | - | Visual style capsules › Capsule library | Legacy workaround: capsule reference images uploaded to a personal space were made publicly viewable to fix a storage-permission error. That is not the intended access model; the required model is private, authenticated access to user-generated media (WD-IDEA-ALT-013). ‹src: storage-rls-error-work-around›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-021 | ASSET | alt | Visual style capsules › Options: grouping capsules into libraries | Capsules are organized into named, user-owned libraries (user-made or preset collections). A library can be marked public for sharing beyond its owner.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-022 | ASSET | rejected,voice | Visual style capsules › Options: grouping capsules into libraries | Auto-created libraries, so users wouldn't have to think about them. The creator chose no libraries: simple capsule creation with just a name and content, distinguished only by "Preset" vs "Custom" badges. ‹src: libraries-for-style-capsules›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-024 | ASSET | planned,voice | Project visual style (Stage 5) | Manual visual tone as an alternative to choosing a capsule. It offers presets (3D animation, hyperrealistic, noir, 2D animation, etc.) plus a custom plain-English description, richer than a single text box, mirroring the Stage 1 manual writing tone. The creator's note stresses that choosing a style guide is not mandatory: plain-English style or tone text alone is enough ("you don't have to choose a style guide"), which sits against Stage 5 treating a visual style lock as a gate (see 13). ‹src: new-tasks› The user never has to pick a capsule. ‹src: Tickets, new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-025 | ASSET | planned | Project visual style (Stage 5) | A custom visual-tone description can be promoted into the starting point of a new visual style capsule. ‹src: Tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-026 | ASSET | planned,voice | Project visual style (Stage 5) | The style is not immutably locked. It can be changed after selection with a warning about downstream impact. Any change made after unlocking the style guide triggers a branch, whether early or late in production. Related: WD-IDEA-UX-029, WD-IDEA-UX-035. ‹src: Tickets, new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-028 | ASSET | impl | Project visual style (Stage 5) | Each asset records which visual capsule its image was generated under.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-029 | ASSET | - | Project visual style (Stage 5) | The user confirms the visual style for each scene in Stage 8. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-030 | ASSET | impl,planned | Style vs identity: influence balance | Influence hierarchy: a master (or scene-instance) reference image has the highest priority. The visual capsule shapes the look but never overrides identity. Identity references are placed before style references in the image order. The capsule must still visibly affect asset images; it once had no visible effect. When style reference images overwhelm identity references, style images are limited while asset references are present. ‹src: Tickets, MVP-tasklist-v1.1, 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-031 | ASSET | voice | Style vs identity: influence balance | Top complaint: capsules overrode master reference images. The creator wanted a better default balance between capsule and reference, fixed early. ‹src: injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-032 | ASSET | planned | Style vs identity: influence balance | Capsule influence slider (0–100%) in Stages 5, 8, and 10. 0% = master reference only; 100% = full capsule style override. Defaults to a balanced value, saved per project, possibly with real-time preview.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-035 | ASSET | planned | Asset extraction | Re-extraction triggers: a master-script change, mandatory branching, or a user "Re-extract Assets" action. It regenerates the asset list and scene map, preserves manually added assets, and flags downstream stages for review (see 10).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-040 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Anyone who speaks is a character. The character list and per-scene presence come from dialogue lines.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-041 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Counting each character's dialogue lines separates leads from one-line extras. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-042 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Non-speaking characters are detected by scanning action lines for already-known names, so silent characters are still mapped to the scene.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-043 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Props come from the screenplay convention of capitalized nouns in action lines (BRIEFCASE, REVOLVER). Screenplay terms (INT, DAY, CUT, POV, SFX…) and known character names are ignored.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-044 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Props not written in caps are found by a lightweight AI pass over action text only, not the whole script, to save cost. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-045 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Scene-level dependency extraction records names exactly as written ("THE GUARD", "his watch"); matching them to existing assets is a separate step. A scene's location comes from its heading but can be refined when the action names a more specific place (see 06B). Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-129 (heading-based location) and WD-IDEA-LOC-132 (shot-level resolver). ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-046 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Deterministic identification rules | Aliases and name variants merge into one asset ("John" = "John Doe" = "Mr. Doe"; "John's kitchen" = "Kitchen").
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-047 | ASSET | alt | Asset extraction › Options: who identifies entities | AI catalog pass over the master script and its per-scene mention lists. Characters are any named person or creature with dialogue or significant action. Props are objects mentioned repeatedly, plot-critical, or visually distinctive. Settings are every place a scene occurs. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-048 | ASSET | alt | Asset extraction › Options: who identifies entities | The AI extracts everything without filtering by importance, flags each asset Key (3+ mentions) or Supporting (1–2), and gives it a prominence score. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-049 | ASSET | alt,impl | Asset extraction › Options: who identifies entities | Discovery keeps only tangible, visual assets, excluding abstractions (atmosphere, tension, mood), and is limited to main characters, important props, and primary locations. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-050 | ASSET | rejected | Asset extraction › Options: who identifies entities | AI-based entity identification as the first pass is replaced by deterministic extraction. The AI only writes visual descriptions for entities already identified. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-051 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Two-pass extraction and filtering | Two-pass extraction. Pass 1 is a cheap or free deterministic preview of entity names, types, and scene mentions, grouped as Characters, Locations, and Props. The user filters, then pass 2 writes descriptions and image prompts/images only for the selected assets.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-052 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Two-pass extraction and filtering | The filter is a compact checklist (name and category only, no descriptions or images) grouped by category. Every asset starts selected, with Select All/None per category, an asset count, and "Confirm Selection (X assets)". Its job is to drop assets not worth pre-generating.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-053 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Two-pass extraction and filtering | The AI proposes candidates and the user confirms which to keep. When nothing is found, the user adds assets manually.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-054 | ASSET | voice | Asset extraction › Two-pass extraction and filtering | Stage 5 should offer bulk delete, and all assets collapsed by default so the user can strip unwanted ones fast. ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-055 | ASSET | alt | Asset extraction › Options: filter actions | Keep/Select-only checklist (above), where removal always sidelines rather than deletes.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-056 | ASSET | alt,voice | Asset extraction › Options: filter actions | Three columns per asset: Keep (default), Defer (real, but not now), and Delete (wrong extraction or too minor). A small section lists deferred assets with their scenes. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-057 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Each asset's master description is synthesized from every script mention, cleaned and merged into one. The exact per-scene excerpts it came from are kept.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-058 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Descriptions | The description is fully editable and supports targeted rewrites ("change eye color to green"). ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-059 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Descriptions are short (3–5 sentences), physical-only, and made of concrete details an image model can render.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-060 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Descriptions | When the script describes an asset in conflicting ways, the description points out the conflict instead of silently resolving it. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-061 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Descriptions are written to fit the chosen visual style. The AI also judges whether each asset is a main character/key prop or a background element.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-062 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Quality expectation: descriptions cover age, appearance, and clothing. Image keys fit the genre (dynamic poses and equipment for action; speaking poses and presentation materials for podcasts). The asset set is complete but not inflated. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-063 | ASSET | - | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Audio-driven projects: hosts look the way the audio describes them, visual aids (charts, graphics) are defined as assets, and asset states change with topic transitions. Related: WD-IDEA-STORY-093, WD-IDEA-STORY-094. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-064 | ASSET | voice | Asset extraction › Descriptions | Once assets are defined, optionally build a hidden "master descriptive document" from them. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-065 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Manual and deferred assets | Users manually add assets extraction missed (name, type, description, optional image prompt) in Stage 5 or Stage 8, without triggering re-extraction. Manual assets join the scene dependency map, can be assigned to scenes by hand, and survive re-extraction.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-066 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Manual and deferred assets | Removing an asset in Stage 5 defers (sidelines) it instead of deleting it. It is hidden from Stage 5 and the pipeline but keeps its scene information, and it can be restored. It stays available to Stages 8 and 10, where AI can generate it on the fly from script context.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-067 | ASSET | voice | Asset extraction › Manual and deferred assets | Deferred assets appear in Stage 8. In their first scene they have no master reference; in later scenes they show the scene-instance images generated or uploaded earlier. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-068 | ASSET | impl | Asset extraction › Manual and deferred assets | Deferred assets have no action menu, so per-asset tools (e.g. location camera directions, see 06B) work only on active assets. ‹src: 3.7-location-rd-1-testing-guides›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-069 | ASSET | voice | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | Extra archetypes are a user-authored creative addition to the world ("my world has this type of background figure"), not a reclassification of characters found in the script. Extraction never finds them, because they are never named. ‹src: extra-archtype-info›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-070 | ASSET | planned,voice | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | One prototype entry stands for a whole class of background characters with little or no dialogue (e.g. "Impoverished Plebeian" in plague-era France), so no extra needs individual persistent identity. Included in MVP. ‹src: Tickets, injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-071 | ASSET | - | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | A character asset means "exactly this person, always". An extra archetype is a template from which frame/video generation spawns background figures recognizably of that type, with natural variation. ‹src: extra-archtype-info›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-072 | ASSET | - | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | Stage 5 has a dedicated "Add Extra Archetype" action: name it ("Zorblaxian Alien", "Medieval Peasant"), describe its visual traits, and optionally generate or upload a reference.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-073 | ASSET | - | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | Archetypes form a fourth group in the Stage 5 asset grid, alongside characters, locations, and props.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-074 | ASSET | - | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | The archetype reference image is its key prototype but optional. Text-only archetypes are allowed, and an image can be added later without blocking Stage 5, since some users won't assign extras until Stage 8. ‹src: extra-archtype-info›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-075 | ASSET | - | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | Archetypes are created globally in Stage 5 (optionally also at scene level) and assigned to scenes in Stage 8 ("this scene is on the alien planet, include Zorblaxian Aliens"). They count as mise-en-scène in scene dependencies and serve as references for frame generation.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-076 | ASSET | rejected | Characters, props, and extras › Extra archetypes | Earlier extras options, including reclassifying an extracted character as an extra, did not fit. They were replaced by the user-created archetype type. ‹src: extra-archtype-info›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-078 | ASSET | voice | Characters, props, and extras › Options: characters whose state changes across the story | For that option, extraction gets a rule allowing several "changed-state assets" per character. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-057 (the multiple-assets option). ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-080 | ASSET | alt,voice | Characters, props, and extras › Options: characters whose state changes across the story | A section in scene asset definition describes how a character changes shot by shot within the scene (e.g. in shot 7F the space suit cracks, he bleeds, his face reddens). The creator suspected this is a bad idea. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-068 (mid-scene changes are not predefined upstream). ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-081 | ASSET | future,voice | Characters, props, and extras › Options: characters whose state changes across the story | Per-shot refinement without image generation. The asset is set at scene start; while cycling through shots, the user adds descriptive tags for what changed, which enrich that shot's frame and video prompts. ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-082 | ASSET | voice | Characters, props, and extras › Options: characters whose state changes across the story | Open uncertainty: the scene instance may need to break down further into shot-by-shot asset instances, though the creator hopes not. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-083 | ASSET | voice,idea | Characters, props, and extras › Options: characters whose state changes across the story | Because shot prompts and scene asset states are mutually dependent, the creator considered swapping Stages 8 and 9 so shot-by-shot prompts exist before scene assets are edited. ‹src: post-tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-084 | ASSET | - | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Each asset's image key is generated from its edited description plus the locked visual style. The user iterates with a guidance box ("make the hair redder") until satisfied, then locks the master asset.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-085 | ASSET | - | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | The image key prompt opens with the asset's type and name. It includes every physical characteristic from the master description, specifies reference-sheet lighting, angle, and composition, applies capsule cues, and respects content rating and genre. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-086 | ASSET | - | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Master reference images are references, not scenes: no action or narrative elements.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-087 | ASSET | planned,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Characters, props, and extras are generated isolated on a clean background (transparent, white, or black). The instruction is injected invisibly at generation time, so frame generation isn't confused by background noise or other characters. Locations never get isolation (see 06B). Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-011, WD-IDEA-ASSET-120.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-088 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Two ways to get an image key: upload one (PNG/JPEG/WebP, up to 10 MB) or generate it. "Generate all missing" fills every gap at once.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-089 | ASSET | voice | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Stage 5 master assets get the same multi-image carousel as Stage 8. Users generate and upload several images (about 4), all are stored, the user picks the active one, and users are encouraged to delete off-target ones. This matters most for assets that change dramatically over the story. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist, planning-wif-lawrence›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-090 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Users can delete any uploaded or predetermined image, returning the asset to description-only so its look is no longer fixed.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-091 | ASSET | voice | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Image keys | Regenerating a Stage 5 asset whose image the user uploaded must balance faithfulness to the upload against the requested changes. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-092 | ASSET | alt | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: background isolation method | Prompt-only isolation.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-093 | ASSET | alt | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: background isolation method | Background-removal post-processing (removes halos and solid backgrounds) as a safety net, A/B tested against prompt-only isolation.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-094 | ASSET | planned | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: are images required? | Images are optional for all assets. Users generate or upload only for assets they care about. An imageless asset shows a placeholder ("Upload or Generate"), still carries its description into production, and doesn't block leaving Stage 5.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-095 | ASSET | alt,planned | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: are images required? | Limit the image-optional rule to props in Stage 5, since minor props are the clearest case where images are unnecessary. ‹src: Tickets›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-097 | ASSET | planned,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Angle variants | Multi-angle assets: front, side, three-quarter, and back views of characters (and props) on clean backgrounds, like engineering drawings, each generated independently. Frame generation receives the view matching each shot's camera direction. Included in MVP. Locations use a different view model (see 06B).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-098 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Per-asset actions: edit visual description, delete, defer, restore, and change type (character / prop / location / extra archetype).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-099 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Merge several selected assets into one (e.g. duplicates from extraction).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-100 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Split an asset into scene-specific variants. Select mode offers both Merge and Split.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-101 | ASSET | voice,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Merge works on image-approved assets: the survivor keeps its image, and the user is warned that the absorbed assets' images will be lost. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-102 | ASSET | voice,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Change Type stays available after an asset's image is approved. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-103 | ASSET | - | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Legacy edge case: there is no "deselect image" action, so any lock tied to image selection traps the asset (no type change, no merge). ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-104 | ASSET | voice,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | Truncated descriptions in the grid get an inline "Show more/less" toggle, so users can compare descriptions (e.g. before merging) without opening an editor. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-105 | ASSET | voice | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Asset management | The asset editor modal must scroll, and props must be deletable. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-106 | ASSET | alt | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: what Change Type does to an approved image | Clear the selected image and prompt regeneration. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-107 | ASSET | alt | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: what Change Type does to an approved image | Keep the image as is. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-108 | ASSET | impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: locking master assets | Each project asset can be locked individually during Stage 5. Stage 5 ends with a visible, working lock-all-and-proceed action.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-110 | ASSET | voice,impl | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Options: locking master assets | The completion button reads "Finalize Assets", not "Lock All Assets & Begin Production", so users don't expect rendering to start. ‹src: ui-fixes›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-111 | ASSET | future | Master asset definition (Stage 5) › Voice profiles | A master character can be linked to a voice profile (e.g. an ElevenLabs voice) in the asset editor during global asset definition, with a voice preview. The profile acts as a "voice seed" in every video call so the character sounds identical across clips. Native video audio does not keep a character's voice consistent across shots, which this fills (see 09).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-112 | ASSET | voice | Uploads and image reconciliation | Problem: AI extraction won't always match the user's vision, and uploaded custom images can differ meaningfully from the auto-generated description. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-113 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Uploading an asset image triggers AI analysis that proposes a description from the image, so the stored description matches the custom design. Goal: the user ends with an image they like and a description that matches it.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-114 | ASSET | - | Uploads and image reconciliation | A layman's real photo, rough sketch, or imperfect generated image should become a pipeline-ready asset without a separate design workflow. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-115 | ASSET | - | Uploads and image reconciliation | Never blindly accept an uploaded image or blindly overwrite existing text.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-116 | ASSET | impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Review modal layout: the current description (read-only), the description extracted from the image (read-only), and an editable Final Description pre-filled with an AI merge of the two. The Final Description is what gets saved.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-117 | ASSET | impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | A confidence badge shows how closely the AI thinks the uploaded image matches the asset's existing description.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-118 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | "Edit Image": the user types only what should change ("change suit to jumpsuit", "add scar on left cheek"). Generation uses the current image as reference plus that instruction, skipping the full description, which could confuse the AI.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-119 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | "Apply Visual Style" re-renders the uploaded image in the project capsule; e.g. a real photo of yourself becomes the Pixar-style you in a Pixar-style project. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-120 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | "Remove Background / make white" applies to character, prop, and extra uploads so the image works cleanly as a reference in later generation calls. It is hidden for locations.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-121 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | "Regenerate from Description" makes a fresh image from the final description plus capsule, without the upload as reference.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-122 | ASSET | impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | A "use current image as reference" toggle governs regeneration (see 08 for the other places it appears).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-123 | ASSET | voice | Uploads and image reconciliation | Canonical case: upload a photo of a character in a suit when the story needs an orange prisoner jumpsuit. Change the description to "orange jumpsuit" and regenerate with the upload as reference; the same character now wears the jumpsuit. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-124 | ASSET | impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | The preview updates after every action, with a carousel of all attempts produced in the session.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-125 | ASSET | impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Accept saves the Final Description plus whichever image is currently shown (the last edited, styled, background-removed, or regenerated one) as the asset's active image. Cancel discards everything and reverts to the pre-upload state.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-126 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Background removal starts as an image-generation redraw ("isolated on plain white background, no environment, no other characters or objects"). A dedicated segmentation model comes later (see 08). ‹src: 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-127 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Any regeneration of an isolated asset (edit-image and apply-style included) automatically produces a white background.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-128 | ASSET | voice,impl | Uploads and image reconciliation | Stage 5 (master assets) and Stage 8 (scene instances) share one upload modal. In Stage 8, "current description" is the scene's effective description, produced images become scene-instance attempts, the accepted description is saved as a scene override, and the master reference can serve as an extra identity anchor.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-129 | ASSET | rejected | Uploads and image reconciliation | Earlier behavior: Stage 5 offered only keep current / replace with extracted / use merged, with no iteration, and Stage 8 uploads became the scene image with no reconciliation at all. Replaced by the shared enhanced modal.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-130 | ASSET | rejected,voice | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: reconciliation design | Three-column Description Reconciliation modal (existing / extracted from image / editable merge built from both plus the user's own input), modeled on the merge-assets modal. Replaced by a simpler enhancement of the existing upload modal. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-131 | ASSET | planned | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: reconciliation design | Description-from-image choice: keep the existing description, replace it with the image-based one plus missing script details, or merge the two, previewed before confirming, mirroring cloned assets in asset matching.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-132 | ASSET | alt,rejected | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: reconciliation design | Agentic "Merge Descriptions" tool: the user specifies which parts of each description to keep, and AI merges them into one cohesive description.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-133 | ASSET | alt,rejected,voice | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: reconciliation design | Tag-based adjustment: keep a base description and add directional tags (jumpsuit, wet, bloody, sad, sweaty) for the AI to apply. Judged unnecessary because a free-text Edit Image box covers it. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-134 | ASSET | alt,voice | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: upload intent | Two explicit intents: Full Replacement (the image IS the asset; the description updates to match) vs Starting Point (the image is a reference to iterate toward the final vision). ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-135 | ASSET | impl,voice | Uploads and image reconciliation › Options: upload intent | Implicit intent replaced the explicit modes. Upload always opens the enhanced modal; the user accepts as-is or iterates with image actions. An explicit toggle is only worth it if the modal would differ meaningfully, since as-is uploads may still want background removal or styling. ‹src: 3.7-threads, streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-138 | ASSET | planned | Uploads and image reconciliation › Batch image intake (Streamline) | Heavy per-image actions (Edit Image, Apply Visual Style, Remove Background, Regenerate) are excluded from intake and done after commit through the normal Stage 5 reconciliation flow. Related: WD-IDEA-INPUT-106 (the batch grid).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-139 | ASSET | rejected | Uploads and image reconciliation › Batch image intake (Streamline) | Handing each Streamline image to the one-at-a-time upload modal (N modals in sequence) was replaced by the batch screen, since the assets to reconcile against don't exist yet at intake.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-140 | ASSET | - | Global asset library and promotion | The global asset library holds master assets (characters, props, locations) independent of any project, usable as templates for new projects.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-141 | ASSET | impl | Global asset library and promotion | Library browsing: search by name, grid/list views, All/Characters/Props/Locations tabs, and create/edit/delete.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-142 | ASSET | impl | Global asset library and promotion | Deleting a library asset checks for dependents before confirming.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-143 | ASSET | impl | Global asset library and promotion | "Promote to Global" clones a master asset or a scene instance (image plus description) into the user's library; also available from the Artifact Vault. It requires an image. The global copy remembers its source project, and the project asset links to it.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-144 | ASSET | impl | Global asset library and promotion | A global asset can be cloned into a project. The project asset remembers which version of the global asset it came from.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-145 | ASSET | impl | Global asset library and promotion | When reusing a global asset, the user can choose a merge strategy. The global description is the canonical base; the project description adds or modifies details without contradicting it, keeping every important visual detail from both. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-146 | ASSET | future | Global asset library and promotion | Richer promotion: promote scene instances with asset versioning, templates, and sharing between projects.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-149 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Stage 8 works with scene-specific instances of project assets. An instance can be inherited from the previous scene, created from a project asset, detected from shot/script dependencies, or added manually.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-150 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Scene 1 is bootstrapped from locked project assets matched by name to the scene's expected characters, location, and props. Later scenes inherit first (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-151 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | "Inherit from prior scene" copies the previous scene's asset states into the current scene as a starting point.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-152 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Stage 8 auto-suggests the scene's assets from the extraction scene map, grouped by type. Each is labeled by source (master asset, prior scene instance, new scene asset) and review status (unreviewed, edited, locked).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-154 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Relevance rules: include characters explicitly in the shot list, props mentioned or implied by the action, and every setting mentioned.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-155 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Each relevant asset shows where its starting state came from (master or previous instance), its starting description, whether it needs a visual update, its inherited status tags, and why the scene needs it.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-156 | ASSET | impl,voice | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | AI relevance detection suggests project assets that belong in the scene and discovers new assets the scene needs.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-157 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | New-asset proposals come with an inferred description and a justification.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-158 | ASSET | planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Suggestions persist across navigation and reload, show the AI's rationale, can be accepted or dismissed (dismissed ones don't reappear), aren't duplicated within a scene, and update when scene dependencies change.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-159 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Stage 8 always offers three ways to add assets: from project assets, create a new asset, or "Detect with AI". Detection adds newly found library assets and saves suggestions for ones not yet in the library.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-160 | ASSET | voice | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Stage 8 should be mostly deterministic thanks to good extraction. Manual add/delete remains the correction path and must be reliable. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-161 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Population and suggestions | Users must be able to re-add missed or accidentally deleted scene assets, add or delete after auto-population, and recategorize mislabeled assets (e.g. a prop extracted as a character).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-162 | ASSET | alt | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: does Stage 8 detect assets with AI? | Use only the extracted scene map for suggestions, with no fresh AI relevance pass. ‹src: MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-163 | ASSET | alt | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: does Stage 8 detect assets with AI? | Keep AI relevance detection running, with its "new assets required" suggestions persisted and dismissable. ‹src: 1c-scene-asset-suggestions›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-164 | ASSET | planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Adding and removing scene assets | Stage 8 has its own way to create a scene-specific asset from scratch, separate from pulling from the drawer. The button is labeled "Add New Assets", not "Create Scene Asset".
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-165 | ASSET | idea | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Adding and removing scene assets | Open questions: inline form vs drawer for scene-asset creation; job polling for bulk image generation. ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-166 | ASSET | impl,planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Adding and removing scene assets | The asset drawer (Stage 8): add assets extraction missed, create scene-only assets, switch between project and global library sources, search and filter, stay fast with large libraries, and promote to global.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-167 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Adding and removing scene assets | "Remove from scene" removes only the asset's link to that scene (e.g. fixing a wrong extraction), never the project or global asset, and asks for confirmation.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-168 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | "Use Master As-Is" is checked by default and copies the master image to the scene instance with no generation. Unchanged assets progress instantly, saving time and money.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-169 | ASSET | planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Bulk "Use Master As-Is" across many scene assets, plus smart default selection.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-170 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Stage 8 shows an asset's master reference next to its scene-instance image(s). The user can generate a scene-specific image, drop or upload their own, or use the master as-is.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-171 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | A scene-specific image never replaces the approved master image.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-172 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Scene visual-state evolution: the user types scene modifications ("add mud", "change clothing"). The AI generates a scene image that keeps the master identity and style and applies only those changes. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-017.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-174 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Reference history carousel: a scene's starting reference defaults to the previous scene's instance (Scene 4 starts from Scene 3). Arrows cycle back through the chain (master, Scene 1, Scene 2…), and "Use This Reference" confirms. The first scene defaults to the master, scenes without instances are skipped, and scene deletion or reordering is handled.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-176 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Generation attempts carousel. Every attempt is kept as a thumbnail with hover metadata (time, cost, prompt used), compared side by side, and any attempt (even the first) can be made active with "Select This One". Rationale: a regeneration can come out worse and the user must be able to revert. Old attempts follow a cleanup policy.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-177 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | The user can upload their own scene-instance image; it joins the attempts carousel alongside generated ones.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-178 | ASSET | - | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Scene images | Stage 8 flow: load suggested assets, toggle as-is, edit descriptions where needed, pick the reference from the history carousel, multi-select for bulk generation, compare attempts, apply condition tags with carry-forward (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-185 | ASSET | impl | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | Hard gate: every scene asset needs a visual reference before prompts. The block message says how many are missing.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-187 | ASSET | voice,planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | When the user proceeds with undecided assets, a modal offers to fall each back to its latest scene instance or master reference. Undecided deferred assets are auto-generated. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-188 | ASSET | alt,planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | Fallback order for untouched assets: (1) most recent previous scene instance, (2) master reference, (3) generate. ‹src: master-mvp-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-189 | ASSET | alt,planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | Tiered gating: primary characters must be generated; secondary props/locations may use master references; minor props get "Generate Later"; the user can override to force or skip generation.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-190 | ASSET | alt,planned | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | An imageless asset is raised as a visual-definition issue ("Asset X has no image — generate or upload one before proceeding") rather than silently excluded from the reference set. ‹src: 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-191 | ASSET | alt | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Options: proceeding with undecided scene assets | An imageless asset is simply excluded from the reference set. ‹src: 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-192 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Tag templates: predefined tag sets for common scenarios (e.g. "Fight Scene" = bloody, torn, sweaty). ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-193 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Tag history: when tags were added or removed across scenes.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-194 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Bulk tag operations across many assets at once.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-195 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Filter project assets by tag in the asset drawer.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-196 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Tag-based prompt enrichment: tags expand image prompts automatically ("muddy" becomes "covered in mud, dirty, realistic grime"). ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-197 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Tag severity levels ("light muddy" vs "heavy muddy").
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-198 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | Tag conflict detection warns about incompatible combinations ("clean" + "muddy").
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-199 | ASSET | future | Scene asset instances (Stage 8) › Status tag tooling | A tag analytics dashboard shows tag usage across the project.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-200 | ASSET | impl | Asset-to-shot assignment | Stage 8 initializes shot-asset assignments, recording which assets appear in which shots (see 07).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-201 | ASSET | planned,voice | Asset-to-shot assignment | Shot presence flags: each scene asset shows badges for the shot numbers it appears in, updated when the shot list changes.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-202 | ASSET | planned,voice | Asset-to-shot assignment | Scene-instance images carry camera-angle metadata taken from the shot list's camera directions. It is viewable, editable, and passed to frame generation, so an aerial top-down shot isn't built from front-on medium references.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-203 | ASSET | impl | Asset-to-shot assignment | The shot's camera description is parsed to pick the best-matching angle variant (front, side, three-quarter, back). That angle image is auto-selected and takes highest priority as the character's or prop's reference for the shot, followed by the scene-instance image, then the master image.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-204 | ASSET | alt,impl | Asset-to-shot assignment › Options: default shot-asset assignment | Auto-assign every scene asset to every shot as present "throughout": full coverage, but noisy semantics that need cleanup. ‹src: 4-15-location-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-205 | ASSET | alt,planned | Asset-to-shot assignment › Options: default shot-asset assignment | Confidence-scored preselection of assignments by default, keeping the all-assets fallback for safety. ‹src: 4-15-location-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-206 | ASSET | planned | Asset-to-shot assignment › Options: default shot-asset assignment | Smarter rule-based defaults: locations default to throughout; characters only if the shot names them in foreground/background; props only if the shot's action or setting mentions them. Assigning everything to every shot floods generation with irrelevant references.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-207 | ASSET | impl | Asset-to-shot assignment › Blocking stand-ins (CineBlock) | The blocking tool's scene assets are a flat pool of characters and props for the scene (name, type, one-line description such as "tall man, 30s, grey hoodie"). Shots draw from this pool (see 07).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-208 | ASSET | impl | Asset-to-shot assignment › Blocking stand-ins (CineBlock) | Each blocking asset is auto-assigned a distinct palette color on creation (color dot, changeable via picker), so its mannequin is identifiable during blocking.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-209 | ASSET | planned | Asset-to-shot assignment › Blocking stand-ins (CineBlock) | Removing an asset referenced in shots asks for confirmation and removes it from those shots.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-210 | ASSET | planned | Asset-to-shot assignment › Blocking stand-ins (CineBlock) | When integrated, blocking setup is auto-populated from the project: the scene's shots, its asset instances with scene-effective descriptions, and the location's directional views (see 06B). ‹src: prd-revised›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-212 | ASSET | - | Visual consistency in references and prompts | Describe a subject as if briefing a casting director and costume designer: age, gender presentation, ethnicity, hair (color/length/style/texture), eyes, distinctive facial features, build, skin details (scars, tattoos, freckles), garments with colors/materials/fit/condition, accessories, and held props. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-042, WD-IDEA-CONT-044 (same field set). ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-213 | ASSET | - | Visual consistency in references and prompts | Use 1–3 consistent reference images per character, with similar poses and clothing, and reuse the same set across every related clip in the project to reduce drift. Related: WD-IDEA-GEN-122.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-214 | ASSET | - | Visual consistency in references and prompts | Up to three reference images per generation, typed by purpose: character reference (lock appearance), object/product reference (prop consistency), and style reference (aesthetic continuity). Related: WD-IDEA-GEN-119.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-218 | ASSET | alt | Visual consistency in references and prompts › Options: carrying a character's identity text across shots | Define once, reference many: define a character, style, or setting once and reference it by name or alias in every shot. This keeps multi-shot sequences consistent while saving tokens. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-219 | ASSET | future,voice | Future and speculative | Agentic web scouring to build a style capsule. The system searches the web for images matching a desired look and shows about 5. The user picks favorites, the system finds more, and it repeats until the user is satisfied or gives more direction. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-220 | ASSET | future,voice | Future and speculative | Style transfer training: the user uploads an image dataset and a LoRA-style model learns their visual style, with testing, deployment, and versioning of style models. A proprietary style-tailoring model is a related idea.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-221 | ASSET | future | Future and speculative | Character consistency training: build a character image dataset, train a character embedding/model, use it in generation, and share character models.
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-222 | ASSET | future | Future and speculative | Film reference deconstruction: upload a reference clip or frame sequence and extract its visual style, shot types, camera-movement patterns, and color palette. The result is offered as a starting visual capsule or as location view references (see 06B).
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-223 | ASSET | future,voice | Future and speculative | "Anime mode" for anime lovers. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-ASSET-224 | ASSET | future,voice | Future and speculative | Sample library: a curated clip library supplied by the product, or better, a global library pooled from all clips users have submitted. ‹src: new-tasks›

## 06B Locations & Environments (06B-LOCATIONS-AND-ENVIRONMENTS.md)

- WD-IDEA-LOC-001 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Locations are space-centric, not object-centric. A character or prop reference shows the thing that should appear in frame (reference and output share a subject); a location reference shows one view of a space that frames must depict from many angles, distances, positions, and focal points ("same world, different view"). Locations therefore need different handling from characters and props (see 06).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-002 | LOC | idea | Why locations need their own model | The character/prop four-angle rotation (front/side/three-quarter/back) suits objects you look at, not environments you look out from. Locations need named spatial vantage points mapped to camera setups, not rotations of one subject. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning, 3.7-phase-3-EXPANDEDOUT-location-multi-shot›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-003 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Failure mode, angle mismatch: the reference is aerial, a facade, or one corner, but the shot is at eye level. The generator guesses the missing view; palette may carry over, but the feel of being inside the space does not.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-004 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Failure mode, partial space coverage: one image shows one part of a room (e.g. the fireplace wall). A reverse angle looking back has no reference, so the generator invents furniture, wall colors, and windows the user never chose or approved.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-005 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Failure mode, sub-location drift: one heading ("INT. APARTMENT") spans living room, kitchen, and hallway, or characters move to the couch, kitchen doorway, window, and hallway within it. Each position implies a different background; one image treats every shot as the same static backdrop and breaks realism.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-006 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Text prompting alone has a hard ceiling. Language is lossy for space; the same room prompted five times becomes five rooms (colors, proportions, architecture drift); the generator has no memory of prior frames. Visual references must do most of the work, with prompts supporting.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-007 | LOC | voice,idea,planned | Why locations need their own model | Goal: give the generator enough visual context that its inferences are constrained and logical rather than open-ended hallucination. The target is logical continuity, not perfection: backgrounds must make sense given what is already established about the place, even for angles never provided.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-008 | LOC | voice,planned | Why locations need their own model | A single location image is rarely enough for a dynamic story: angles shift, characters move through the space, and AI guesses about unseen angles are unreliable. An overhead shot of a kitchen should still read as the same kitchen as the eye-level shot.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-009 | LOC | voice,idea | Why locations need their own model | Locations must be "smarter" at frame time: a reference shows the place only in a general sense, so frame generation must reason about how it should logically look from this shot's viewpoint. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-010 | LOC | voice | Why locations need their own model | Generic asset framings bias shot framing. Locations made early are usually one wide image, which pulls generated shots toward that framing. For an over-the-shoulder shot the wanted background is the reverse-direction view (the window behind, a sliver of the register), not the establishing wide. The same applies to character assets, which are usually one generic medium-shot image each and bias the shot's framing toward that image. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-011 | LOC | planned,impl,voice | Why locations need their own model | Locations never get background isolation or the white-background treatment applied to characters, props, and extras, because the environment is the point; the remove-background control is hidden for locations. Related: WD-IDEA-ASSET-120 (the hidden control) and WD-IDEA-ASSET-087 (the isolation rule for characters, props, and extras).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-012 | LOC | impl | Why locations need their own model | Location images (masters and views) are generated in cinematic widescreen 16:9.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-013 | LOC | impl | Why locations need their own model | Locations are environmental constants that carry forward across scenes by default. Asset states follow the status-tag carry-forward toggle (WD-IDEA-CONT-065); continuation scenes inherit the location (WD-IDEA-LOC-076). ‹src: 3.8-phase4-location-multi-angle›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-014 | LOC | voice,rejected | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Options: what the location system is at its core | Every location is a multi-view reference library built upfront: each asset requires several named shots (aerial overview, room A medium, room B corner, hallway entrance), more extensive than character angles, with the coverage report and established views at the center of the workflow. Replaced by the layered model below, where views are opt-in and grow from production. ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8), 3.7-location-concern-notes›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-015 | LOC | rejected | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Options: what the location system is at its core | Finish the original advanced system exactly as first imagined and make it the main experience. Rejected when a product-shape problem surfaced during implementation. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-016 | LOC | rejected | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Options: what the location system is at its core | Abandon the structured location system and rely on prompts alone for background continuity. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-017 | LOC | idea,planned,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Options: what the location system is at its core | Layered model (the later canonical direction): a baseline layer that is always relevant (shot-to-location link, match confidence, baseline prompt context, reuse/edit of prior frames); an advanced layer that is progressive and opt-in (camera directions, views, shot assignment, coverage advisory, established-view feedback); and an optional blocking/composition layer.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-018 | LOC | idea,planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | The default tier optimizes for speed, clarity, and momentum: less setup, fewer generated assets, lower budget, fewer early decisions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-019 | LOC | idea,planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | The advanced tier optimizes for precision and repeatability: predictable spatial continuity, tight background control, reliable repeated and reverse angles, and reusable consistency anchors.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-020 | LOC | idea,planned,voice | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Default representation: one establishing/overview image, or a strong text description alone that is appended to frame prompts. The reference is a low-weight, flexible style and spatial cue, not a rigid blueprint, and prompt guidance adapts it to each shot.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-021 | LOC | voice | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Conceived: the AI interprets a location reference flexibly, e.g. turning a 3D or overhead reference into an eye-level background for an eye-level shot, so users need not pre-define views. Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-165 (the closest view is good enough). ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-022 | LOC | voice | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Creator rethink after using the multi-direction UI: it added too much friction and attention-to-detail burden. Keep the feature ("not nuke the feature"), but it must not be the default.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-023 | LOC | voice | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Multi-directional views become an opt-in toggle for power users: highly scrupulous, continuity-obsessed, with a high generation budget. ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-024 | LOC | idea | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | A later review judged the five-layer architecture directionally right for high-control work but found the default UX too heavy and too hidden: multi-view should be opt-in and frame reuse the ordinary path. ‹src: 5-1-location-system-intent-source›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-025 | LOC | voice,idea | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | User-settable "modes of location care" trade speed against precision and low cost against an uncapped budget. Flat views are the default and a toggle unlocks a more robust mode; even the minimal mode probably needs a couple of views. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-026 | LOC | voice,idea,planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Never trap users in setup. Support the user who submits only the minimum images and lets the AI infer each shot. Do not optimize only for meticulous power users; do not force multi-view setup, make camera direction mandatory per shot, or hard-block default-mode users.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-027 | LOC | idea | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Do not bury continuity control in setup flows that live only in asset definition (Stage 5).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-028 | LOC | planned,voice,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Reusing and editing approved frames is the long-term center of gravity for ordinary users and the main route to "good enough" background continuity with no multi-direction overhead and less setup than pre-building direction assets. It suits shot/reverse-shot, long scenes in a fixed environment, and recurring locations, and it matches user intuition. Start from one acceptable frame or location image and edit only the delta (new characters, props, a pose, an event, or room damage). Batch-generating many direction images upfront is no longer central. (Frame reuse mechanics: see 04.) ‹src: 5-1-location-system-intent-source, 5-2-location-product-synthesis, 5-3-location-implementation-plan, streamlined-input-process-consolidated, post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-029 | LOC | planned,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | The ordinary loop: define a location, generate, keep a good frame, reuse or edit it for later shots, and enter advanced controls only when continuity needs exceed the baseline. Related: WD-IDEA-LOC-030 states the same working-loop test.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-030 | LOC | impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | A working loop on a fresh project: add one location image, shots resolve to it, generate a frame, select it as the base for a later shot, get a result that reuses the background, optionally promote it to an established view with lineage recorded. ‹src: 7-19-location-stabilization-master-plan›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-031 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › The two tiers | Scenarios the system must serve: a new project with one simple location and no views; many repeated shots in one location; an advanced project with several directions and missing coverage; a reuse/edit loop that promotes a frame to an established view.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-032 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Canonical identity early, complexity late: every shot knows its location, but nobody is forced into heavy setup or multi-step repair to move forward. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-033 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Location truth (which space a shot belongs to) is distinct from location control (directions, coverage, view libraries). A shot knows its location without the user managing directions or coverage. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-034 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Prevent mismatch early instead of surviving it late; the earlier system was "resilient but not coherent". ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis, 4-15-location-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-035 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Show confidence wherever automation decides: users can always tell whether a location link is explicit, inferred, weak, or missing.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-036 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Advisories must be stronger than hidden fragility but lighter than premature blocking. Default users get warnings and recommendations; hard gates are reserved for advanced mode or explicitly strict project settings.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-037 | LOC | planned,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | The system learns from production: approved frames feed the advanced layer as established views, so the reference library grows from real output, not only upfront setup. Built for direction views (WD-IDEA-LOC-230); the wider learning loop across the library is planned. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis, location-continuity-phase-0-audit›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-038 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Stable vocabulary: location = the narrative space or room; location reference = its baseline image or description; location view / camera direction = an advanced-mode viewpoint; established view = an approved frame promoted to a continuity anchor; plus continuity base, generation package, reference manifest, attachment role.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-039 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Success for the default layer: users do not feel trapped, progress with imperfect structure, and see continuity improve through output reuse.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-040 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Principles | Success for the advanced layer: repeated and reverse angles become predictable, coverage can be repaired or extended with explicit tools, and quality scales with effort rather than collapsing under complexity.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-042 | LOC | planned,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Stage ownership | Shot list owns extraction and editing, setting text, the initial location-link suggestion and its visibility, a basic confidence display, light correction, and lock-time warnings. Promise: "make sure each shot basically belongs to the right space." It does not own view management, coverage diagnosis, heavy direction reassignment, or curation from frames.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-043 | LOC | planned,impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Stage ownership | Visual definition owns coverage analysis, direction/view assignment review, missing-view surfacing, one-step entry into advanced controls, and repair of repeated or high-risk problems. Promise: "decide whether your planned shots have enough spatial coverage before you spend generation effort." It does not own baseline shot editing, prompt/reference transparency, or frame-level curation.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-044 | LOC | impl | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Sequencing | Original build order: data model, sub-location precision upstream, asset-stage views UI, shot-list direction assignment, coverage advisory (highest user-facing value), prompt generation, established views, batch view generation (convenience, lowest priority). All eight phases (A–H) were reported done.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-045 | LOC | voice | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Sequencing | The creator wanted the A–H overhaul checked against the master spec for anything missed. ‹src: goated-prompts›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-046 | LOC | idea | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Sequencing | Counter-position: do not just finish every unchecked box in the master spec; first realign with the later correction (simpler default, advanced opt-in). ‹src: 5-1-location-system-intent-source›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-047 | LOC | idea | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Sequencing | Corrected priority: (1) clarify the default vs advanced model; (2) build frame reuse and edit-from-existing; (3) fix discoverability of the advanced system; (4) add light visibility in shot list and prompt review; (5) only then scene overrides, project dashboards, and smarter auto-assignment. ‹src: 5-1-location-system-intent-source›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-048 | LOC | planned | Product model: light default, opt-in advanced tier › Sequencing | Run the work as one coherent rebuild (establish canonical truth, expose lightly early, concentrate repair in one stage, make the preview honest, make frame generation the practical continuity engine), not a pile of unrelated tickets. ‹src: 5-3-location-implementation-plan›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-049 | LOC | impl | Advanced architecture | Five interlocking layers: (1) sub-location precision in script and extraction, (2) camera-direction reference views per location, (3) per-shot direction assignment, (4) an advisory coverage analysis before generation, (5) established ("hero frame") views fed back from approved frames.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-050 | LOC | impl | Advanced architecture | The establishing-plus-directions model (a spatial anchor plus directional detail) is understandable to filmmakers and bridges script semantics to image conditioning.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-051 | LOC | idea | Advanced architecture | Starting point the architecture replaced: locations as single-image assets (like characters, but widescreen), one master image plus an optional scene-level override; a shot's setting was free text matched to the location by name with no spatial awareness; continuity linking detected only same vs different location, not positions within a space. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-052 | LOC | voice,impl | Sub-locations | Precision is room-level. Script headings name the exact room or area ("INT. GINGERBREAD HOUSE - KITCHEN - DAY", "... - HALLWAY - CONTINUOUS"), not just the building, and script generation is instructed to produce this (see 05).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-053 | LOC | voice,idea | Sub-locations | Establishing images must be precise to the discrete room (kitchen, hallway, dining room), not one establishing shot of a whole house; one scene, or even shots within one scene, may need several sub-location establishing images. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-054 | LOC | voice,idea | Sub-locations | Each shot's location attribution must be specific, not a vague "interior - Hero's home" repeated for every shot, so the right references can be inferred. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-056 | LOC | voice,planned | Sub-locations | Long precise headings are split for display: "INT. GINGERBREAD HOUSE - KITCHEN - DAY" shows as location "GINGERBREAD HOUSE - KITCHEN" and time "DAY" in scene lists and the pipeline header. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-057 | LOC | voice | Sub-locations | Sub-locations are desirable but not mandatory; when a reference is only attached as a light cue, sub-areas may be combined into one. ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-058 | LOC | idea | Sub-locations | Direction views give angle coverage within a space; they must not compensate for sloppy extraction. When the story truly changes space, it gets a separate location asset.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-059 | LOC | idea | Sub-locations | Subtle position changes within one sub-location (walking to the corner) are not a new sub-location; they are a change of camera direction per shot, driven by spatially explicit action text.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-061 | LOC | idea | Sub-locations | Asset burden: 15 sub-locations with 3 direction views each is 45 images before a single frame; the burden must be communicated or managed upfront. ‹src: 3.7-location-system-enhance-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-062 | LOC | alt,impl | Sub-locations › Options: how sub-locations are modeled | A, granular locations: each room is its own location asset with its own references; views cover angles within the room. Clean and explicit but 3–5x more assets. Chosen as architecture Layer A; dependency extraction treats "Apartment - Kitchen" and "Apartment - Hallway" as distinct assets.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-063 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: how sub-locations are modeled | B, views as sub-locations: one general asset ("Gingerbread House") whose views cover both angles and sub-areas via zone labels (kitchen-wide, kitchen-reverse, hallway-looking-in); shot settings stay general and action text is parsed for zone hints. Fewer assets, longer view lists, less explicit matching. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-064 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: how sub-locations are modeled | C, parent/child hybrid: a parent location supplies shared identity (style, palette); each room is a child with its own views; shot settings auto-split into "GINGERBREAD HOUSE - KITCHEN". ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-065 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: view organization and hierarchy depth | Flat view list per location (chosen default first).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-066 | LOC | alt,future | Sub-locations › Options: view organization and hierarchy depth | Zone hierarchy: location, then zones, then views; possibly later just a grouping label on views or behind a toggle if flat struggles.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-067 | LOC | rejected,future | Sub-locations › Options: view organization and hierarchy depth | Infinite hierarchy or nested scene graph: rejected; precision stays at room/area level and complex zone hierarchies are deferred.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-068 | LOC | idea | Sub-locations › Options: view organization and hierarchy depth | Open question: at what threshold does flat break down (a mansion, a ship, a school with many areas), even if the fix is deferred?
- WD-IDEA-LOC-069 | LOC | alt,voice | Sub-locations › Options: where sub-locations are inferred | In the script (Stage 3) or dependency extraction (Stage 4), which the creator leaned toward, so asset definition knows exactly which sub-location establishing shots and recommended (not required) direction views to make. Related: WD-IDEA-FLOW-122 and WD-IDEA-FLOW-123 (the two stage placements). ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-070 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: where sub-locations are inferred | In shot-list generation (Stage 7), the questionnaire's selection; the creator also felt the shot list might need to make these distinctions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-072 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: characters moving between sub-locations within a scene | Per-shot assignment: the shot list assigns a sub-location and direction to each shot (selected in the questionnaire).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-073 | LOC | alt | Sub-locations › Options: characters moving between sub-locations within a scene | Hybrid: prefer split headings, fall back to per-shot assignment.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-075 | LOC | voice,idea | Sub-locations › Sub-location detection and continuation scenes | Story-aware sub-location gap detection: parse action and direction text (dependency extraction or shot list) for spatial movement cues, tag shots with inferred zones, and feed the coverage report, e.g. "Characters move to 3 distinct areas within 'Kitchen'; consider adding zone references." Bridges narrative to spatial awareness without manual zone tagging.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-076 | LOC | idea | Sub-locations › Sub-location detection and continuation scenes | Continuation scenes ("INT. LAB - CONTINUOUS") must still surface location context and direction controls; the gap between the logical story location and the scene's linked asset must be resolved. Options: auto-inherit the previous scene's location and coverage in continuous sequences, show coverage for any scene with a recognized expected location even without a linked asset, or a prominent "inherit assets from previous scene" action. Because continuation scenes share the same space, the previous scene's location asset and coverage analysis are the natural inheritance source. ‹src: 3.7-location-rd-2-UIUX-improove›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-077 | LOC | rejected | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | "Location shots" / named shots (earliest): named reference images for vantage points (aerial overview, corner booth, hallway entrance), each with a description, one marked primary as default, modeled on character angle variants. Shots were fuzzy-matched to them; unmatched shots used the primary image. Replaced by formalized camera directions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-078 | LOC | planned | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | Named shots were free-text and user-defined rather than a fixed angle set; each had a name, optional description of the angle/area, image, generation prompt, generation status, and user-controlled order. ‹src: 3.8-phase4-location-multi-angle, 3.8-threads›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-079 | LOC | planned | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | Named-shots UI: a "Location Coverage" grid / "Named Shots" dialog opened from location cards in the asset stage; add a named shot, upload an image, or generate one from the primary location image plus its name/description; generate all pending or one at a time.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-080 | LOC | planned | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | The user could upload a custom image for any named shot instead of generating it.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-081 | LOC | alt,planned | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | MVP option: ship named shots with manual upload/generation and manual selection of which named shot each scene shot uses, before any automatic camera-to-shot matching.
