- WD-IDEA-LOC-082 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | Camera directions (replacement): each location holds a set of named reference views (an establishing view plus wall/direction views) that the system selects from per shot; the location is a library drawn from intelligently per frame, not a single image.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-083 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | "Camera direction" is deliberately universal: interiors ("stove wall", "window wall") and exteriors ("facing the house", "facing the forest").
- WD-IDEA-LOC-084 | LOC | voice,idea | Location views (camera directions) › Evolution of the view concept | The creator's "walls" model: a room's walls are its directions. Start with shot and reverse shot (angle 1, angle 2), add walls 3 and 4 only when a scene needs them; the shot list tags each shot with its key wall, and a shot facing one way receives only its sub-location's establishing image and that wall.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-085 | LOC | rejected | Location views (camera directions) › Options: angle paradigm for locations | Fixed four-angle rotation borrowed from characters; does not fit spaces (see above).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-086 | LOC | alt | Location views (camera directions) › Options: angle paradigm for locations | Free-text named shots defined by the user in their own dialog, kept separate from character angle variants.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-087 | LOC | alt,impl | Location views (camera directions) › Options: angle paradigm for locations | Camera directions: default Establishing + Direction 1 + Direction 2 with a primary fallback, auto-inferred and assigned during shot extraction.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-088 | LOC | voice,idea | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | Proposed minimum taxonomy: establishing (wide/aerial, full layout and scale); eye-level primary (default medium straight-on); eye-level secondary (reverse or 90° rotation); detail focal points (fireplace, desk, door); named sub-location views for zones characters move between.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-089 | LOC | voice,idea | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | Each location image is tagged with view type, sub-location label (kitchen_doorway, couch_area), and shot alignment (distance wide/medium/close; height eye/high/low), so frame prompting picks the most relevant image instead of the first upload.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-090 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | View metadata: system name (establishing, direction 1, direction 2…), optional user alias ("stove and counter wall"), description of what it shows, view type (establishing, eye level/directional, detail, overhead, approach), camera distance (default wide), camera height (eye level/high/low/overhead/ground, default eye level), image, primary flag, source, and established-from provenance. One view per name per location; user-visible display order.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-091 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | View source, shown as a badge: User (uploaded or generated), Established (promoted from an approved frame, with "Scene X, Shot Y"), or Inferred (named and described by shot extraction, awaiting an image).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-092 | LOC | planned | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | When creating a direction, prompt for a semantic name or alias ("entry-facing", "north wall"); numbered names are only an internal fallback.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-093 | LOC | idea,impl | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | Primary view = fallback for shots with no match. It must be the main eye-level direction (Direction 1), never the overhead establishing view, because an aerial reference actively misleads a ground-level shot. Only one primary per location, toggled with a star.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-094 | LOC | idea,impl | Location views (camera directions) › View taxonomy and metadata | The establishing view has a different job: a spatial-context anchor always sent as secondary context alongside the matched direction, never the main reference.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-095 | LOC | voice,idea | Location views (camera directions) › Default view sets | Keep default proposals minimal: wide views of each relevant wall (not necessarily four) plus one aerial establishing view of the room is probably enough; users choose which views they want and never need dozens.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-096 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › Default view sets | Interior vs exterior is detected from the INT./EXT. prefix of the headings where the location appears. Related: WD-IDEA-STORY-059 (the heading parse).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-097 | LOC | alt | Location views (camera directions) › Options: default views proposed for a location | Brainstorm set: interior = establishing, eye-level primary, eye-level reverse, 2 detail focal points; exterior = establishing, street level, approach, aerial, detail.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-098 | LOC | alt | Location views (camera directions) › Options: default views proposed for a location | Vantage-point proposals by type (named-shots era): interior = wide establishing, detail corners, overhead, through-doorway; exterior = wide establishing, street-level, aerial, approach, detail; user edits, adds, removes. Expected 5–10 references per location.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-099 | LOC | alt | Location views (camera directions) › Options: default views proposed for a location | Establishing overview plus 2 walls (recommended); or plus 3–4 walls; or just establishing plus primary. Detail views and extra walls (e.g. an L-shaped room) are optional.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-100 | LOC | alt,impl | Location views (camera directions) › Options: default views proposed for a location | Built "Suggest Default Views": never more than 3. Interior = establishing (overhead, wide, whole room from above) + Direction 1 (eye level, one wall, primary) + Direction 2 (eye level, opposite wall). Exterior = establishing (eye-level wide) + Direction 1 (approach, facing the location) + Direction 2 (reverse, looking outward). Establishing plus shot/reverse covers most narrative angles; users add Direction 3, 4… manually.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-101 | LOC | planned | Location views (camera directions) › Suggesting and requiring views | Suggestion is additive ("suggest missing defaults") so partially set-up locations improve incrementally.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-102 | LOC | rejected | Location views (camera directions) › Suggesting and requiring views | All-or-nothing suggestion that refuses once any view exists; replaced by additive suggestion.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-103 | LOC | idea | Location views (camera directions) › Suggesting and requiring views | Open question: how many views are required? Too many is friction, too few defeats the purpose. One early answer: require one establishing plus one eye-level view, the rest recommended from shot-list analysis. ‹src: 3.7-location-concern-notes›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-104 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › Managing views (asset stage) | A "Camera Directions" manager (locations only) opens from the location card menu: empty state with a compass and "Suggest Default Views"; view cards with image upload, inline alias/description editing, primary star, source badges, delete (cleans up the image); "add custom direction" with alias and description; a count such as "2/3 views have images".
- WD-IDEA-LOC-105 | LOC | impl | Location views (camera directions) › Managing views (asset stage) | Asset definition is the primary place to build views but not a gate: users can define all views, only the establishing view, or nothing, and add or edit later from visual definition; the system expects lazy users and compensates with advisories.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-106 | LOC | idea | Location views (camera directions) › Managing views (asset stage) | Asset-stage view creation is more upfront and less shot-informed than visual-definition gap filling; any difference between the two batch review flows should be explicit.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-107 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: who creates the views | The user uploads all views: high quality, high friction.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-108 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: who creates the views | The user uploads one and the system generates the rest: lower friction, quality risk.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-109 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: who creates the views | Hybrid: the user uploads what they have; the system fills gaps with views flagged as AI-inferred.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-110 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: when views are generated for a text-only location | Generate the full multi-view set upfront.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-111 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: when views are generated for a text-only location | Generate views on demand as shots require them.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-112 | LOC | voice,planned,impl | Creating view images › Generation workflow | Coverage workflow: provide or generate one good establishing/first shot, iterate a few times until the look is right, then generate the remaining views from it as reference plus each view's description and the location description; the user approves or regenerates each and approved views join the library.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-113 | LOC | planned | Creating view images › Generation workflow | Start from the most representative angle: a consistent interior generated from an exterior front view is unreliable.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-114 | LOC | idea | Creating view images › Generation workflow | AI is unreliable at alternate angles of complex rooms (wrong proportions, spatial inconsistency, invented furniture), though fine for simple objects. Keep generation optional and framed as "a starting point, expect to curate", make uploading easy, and favor uploads for critical views. Test single-reference spatial consistency before building the full batch UI.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-115 | LOC | impl | Creating view images › Generation workflow | View generation sends an existing location image as style reference plus a prompt describing the desired angle and demanding the same palette, architecture, and lighting ("same Victorian kitchen seen from the opposite wall, eye-level, looking back toward the stove"), plus the location description.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-116 | LOC | impl | Creating view images › Generation workflow | Style-anchor cascade for view generation: establishing view, else primary direction, else the location's master image. With no image anywhere, generation is disabled ("Upload or generate at least one reference image first"). Alternative considered: make the establishing view a soft requirement before direction generation.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-117 | LOC | impl | Creating view images › Generation workflow | Generating a view image only fills that view; it never overwrites the location's master image.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-118 | LOC | voice,future | Creating view images › Generation workflow | Per-direction generation and "Generate N Missing Views" could get a toggle to pick which existing view is the style reference, or opt out; default off and low priority since the legacy flow already picks the establishing/primary view. ‹src: ._1-scratchpad›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-119 | LOC | alt,impl | Creating view images › Options: batch generation of missing views | One-click "Generate N Missing Views" per location plus per-direction generate buttons; sequential, immediate, no review step; shown only when directions lack images and a reference exists, with per-view loading state. Directions already exist with names, aliases, and descriptions (often created by the shot list), so there is nothing left to propose and one click has less friction. Recommended over a confirmation dialog (WD-IDEA-LOC-120) or the full propose/review/confirm flow (WD-IDEA-LOC-121).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-120 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: batch generation of missing views | Confirmation dialog listing the views to generate and their style reference.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-121 | LOC | alt,planned | Creating view images › Options: batch generation of missing views | Full propose → review → confirm (original spec): analyze uncovered shot clusters, propose views prefilled with name/alias/description, user edits/removes/confirms, generate, regenerate misses; proposals capped at what the shot list needs.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-122 | LOC | alt,voice | Creating view images › Options: how coverage gaps get filled with new views | Auto-propose from the shot list: each gap gets a proposed name and description with Generate or Skip (recommended; the creator chose this with a limited default). The user can edit, remove or confirm each proposal before generation; misses can be regenerated; proposals are capped at what the shot list needs.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-123 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: how coverage gaps get filled with new views | Manual definition of each missing view.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-124 | LOC | alt | Creating view images › Options: how coverage gaps get filled with new views | A template of standard views plus manual additions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-125 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Canonical shot-level location identity is foundational infrastructure: every shot carries a reliable link to its location asset, and all stages consume one shared truth instead of recomputing text matches. It is plumbing, not the product experience by itself. ‹src: 5-2-location-product-synthesis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-126 | LOC | planned,impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | A shot's free-text setting stays editable descriptive copy; its location identity is anchored to the linked asset, not the text. Text is supporting metadata; IDs are the join key.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-127 | LOC | idea | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Text identity drift ("Kitchen Hallway" vs "hallway kitchen" vs "kitchen") grows non-linearly with project size; operationally robust fallback is not semantically robust state.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-128 | LOC | idea | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Past problem: setting text could be edited freely with no check against known locations, so drift surfaced late, in visual definition, as "silent mismatch".
- WD-IDEA-LOC-129 | LOC | impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | A scene's expected location comes deterministically from the structured screenplay heading (with INT/EXT and time of day feeding frame context); LLM extraction remains only a fallback for legacy scripts.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-130 | LOC | planned,impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Every shot carries a baseline location link whenever reasonably inferable, with confidence, source, and an optional reason. Sources: manual, exact name, alias, fuzzy, inferred during extraction, legacy backfill. Shot state: resolved, suggested, ambiguous, or unresolved.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-131 | LOC | impl,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Locations carry multiple aliases ("kitchen", "kitchen hall", "main kitchen hallway") for deterministic matching.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-132 | LOC | impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Resolution cascade: an assigned camera direction maps to its parent location at full confidence; exact normalized name ≈0.95; exact alias ≈0.90; token-overlap fuzzy match on name plus aliases ≈0.60–0.89; otherwise the same cascade against the scene's expected location. Output: chosen location, confidence, source, reason, candidates, ambiguity flag.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-133 | LOC | impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Normalization lowercases, strips punctuation, collapses whitespace, optionally drops "the/a/an", and removes INT./EXT. before matching.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-134 | LOC | impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | When the top two candidates are within ≈0.08, the result is ambiguous: the link stays unresolved and candidates are shown for the user to pick, never auto-picked.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-135 | LOC | planned,impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | One shared resolver is the only matching source for extraction, edits, lock validation, coverage, and asset relevance, replacing scattered exact-equality and substring heuristics.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-136 | LOC | impl,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Resolution runs when shots are extracted, when setting text or direction changes (debounced), and on bulk updates.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-137 | LOC | impl,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | A manual pick is marked manual at full confidence and locked against automatic re-resolution; the UI distinguishes "system suggestion changed" from "manual choice retained".
- WD-IDEA-LOC-138 | LOC | impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Batch "resolve all shot locations" for a scene: dry-run and apply modes, a minimum confidence for auto-apply, and counts of updated, unresolved, and ambiguous shots with per-shot reasons and candidates.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-139 | LOC | impl,voice | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Backfill for existing projects: direction parent first, then setting text, then the scene's expected location. Dry-run report by confidence first; only high-confidence (≈0.85–0.90), non-ambiguous, non-manual links apply automatically; medium or ambiguous ones stay visibly "system-assigned" in a review queue, never silently promoted to trusted truth. The creator wanted existing projects backfilled and the continuity system brought to a stable state.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-140 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Legacy or unresolved shots degrade to "unresolved" advisories, never errors, so the system does not look broken when it isn't.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-141 | LOC | idea,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Main risk: wrong auto-links from low-confidence fuzzy matches lead users to trust wrong assignments. Mitigate with confidence gating and an unresolved-review flow.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-142 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Quality targets: ≥70% of shots linked in active projects, fewer manual reassignments in visual definition (e.g. 40% fewer), <5% unresolved at shot-list lock on the median project.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-143 | LOC | planned,future,impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Telemetry: unmatched rate, manual reassignment rate, primary-fallback rate, plus a logged record of each resolution decision (for diagnosis, not runtime). Calibrate thresholds from real usage before enforcing anything; tune badge language, reduce warning fatigue, fix copy users misread.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-144 | LOC | planned,future | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) | Alias management UX: author, curate, type, dedupe, and normalize aliases.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-145 | LOC | impl,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | Each shot shows a searchable "Linked Location" selector beside its setting with a confidence badge (High ≥0.90 green, Medium 0.75–0.89 amber, Low <0.75 or unresolved red/neutral), the match source ("Alias match", "Fuzzy / ambiguous"), and candidates when ambiguous (e.g. "Kitchen Hallway, Main Hallway"). Missing links show as warnings.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-146 | LOC | impl,planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | Per-shot actions stay light: Accept Suggestion, Choose Different, Clear, and Open Coverage Tools as an exit to deeper tools, not the center of the view.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-147 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | A scene banner ("2 shots have weak or unresolved location links") offers Review now or Continue; stage actions include Resolve Visible Suggestions and Review Location Warnings; a validation section counts unresolved, ambiguous, and mismatched-with-expected shots, summarizing rather than launching coverage editing.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-148 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | Unresolved states are obvious but not alarming; the shot list should feel like shot editing with better context, not a spatial-planning dashboard, and stay usable with many unresolved shots.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-149 | LOC | planned,impl | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | Lock validation counts unresolved links, ambiguous matches, and high-confidence links that diverge from the scene's expected location; it warns by default.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-150 | LOC | planned,alt | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | Option: hard-block shot-list lock when unresolved links exceed a threshold (enforcement phase). ‹src: 4-15-location-resolver-spec›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-151 | LOC | planned | Shot-to-location identity (which location a shot is in) › Location link in the shot list | An unresolved location is a data-quality concern; an unresolved camera direction is only an advanced continuity concern. The default flow does not treat them as equally severe.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-152 | LOC | impl,planned | Assigning shots to camera directions | Each shot can be assigned one of its location's directions, which decides its reference image. This is advanced-mode precision, useful when views exist but not required on the default path.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-153 | LOC | impl | Assigning shots to camera directions | View reuse is the core consistency mechanism: one direction serves many shots, so every shot facing "Direction 1" shares the same reference.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-154 | LOC | impl | Assigning shots to camera directions | The shot list both consumes and produces directions. It receives the scene's expected location and existing directions (alias + description) and assigns each shot by where the camera would face given the action, filling structured camera metadata (distance, height, movement, e.g. "MS - Eye Level - Slow Dolly In"). With no existing directions, it makes educated direction decisions (see WD-IDEA-LOC-156 for the bootstrapping rule).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-155 | LOC | voice,idea | Assigning shots to camera directions | Assignment should intelligently use directions already defined for the location; with none, the AI makes educated direction decisions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-156 | LOC | impl | Assigning shots to camera directions | Bootstrap: when a location has no directions (or the story needs undefined ones), shot extraction creates inferred directions (name, AI-written alias and description, no image) and assigns shots to them, so assignment always happens whether or not views were set up; downstream they read as "needs image", not "no directions". Asset-stage setup is never a prerequisite.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-158 | LOC | impl | Assigning shots to camera directions | Assigning a direction can set or update the shot's linked location to the direction's parent unless a manual location conflicts; clearing a direction re-runs resolution unless manually locked; only directions of project locations are assignable.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-159 | LOC | voice,idea | Assigning shots to camera directions | Between story and shot list, let users control how many views shots should use (many different views vs only 1–2), i.e. how creative extraction is with direction assignment. ‹src: jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-160 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: how shots are matched to views | Deterministic metadata matching: fast, no LLM (recommended); build and evaluate first.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-161 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: how shots are matched to views | LLM classification: more accurate, costly.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-162 | LOC | alt,voice,future | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: how shots are matched to views | Hybrid metadata with LLM fallback, which the creator asked to consider; add LLM matching only if metadata proves too coarse.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-163 | LOC | rejected | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: how shots are matched to views | Fuzzy/keyword matching of the shot's camera and setting text against named-shot names and descriptions, falling back to the primary image (named-shots era). Replaced by canonical location links plus explicit direction assignment.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-164 | LOC | impl | Assigning shots to camera directions › Matching behavior | Matching heuristic when no direction is assigned: score directions (not establishing) by camera height match (+2), distance match (+1), and action/setting text containing alias keywords (+3, "stove" → "stove wall"); highest wins; zero falls back to primary (or first) direction; the result is stored and user-overridable. An explicit assignment always wins.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-165 | LOC | voice,idea | Assigning shots to camera directions › Matching behavior | The closest view is good enough; not every variation needs its own image. A high-angle close-up whose closest view is an eye-level medium of the same wall attaches that image, and the prompt describes the actual shot so the model adapts the framing.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-166 | LOC | voice,planned | Assigning shots to camera directions › Visibility and editing of assignments | The assigned direction must be clearly visible on each shot; the creator could not tell which direction a shot used.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-167 | LOC | voice,idea | Assigning shots to camera directions › Visibility and editing of assignments | Assignments are AI-suggested and user-confirmable: the creator expected users to verify each shot's wall in the shot list (a modal or an assignment table with overrides); the assignment persists per shot and drives generation.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-168 | LOC | planned | Assigning shots to camera directions › Visibility and editing of assignments | A shot's direction is one shared value, editable in shot list, visual definition, and prompt review and visible in frame generation; a change anywhere shows everywhere. Stage 7 editing becomes available after shot-list generation; Stage 8 edits it through the coverage table; Stage 9 is the last chance to change it before generation; Stage 10 shows it. (Shot-list and prompt-review editing were reported missing earlier.)
- WD-IDEA-LOC-169 | LOC | idea | Assigning shots to camera directions › Visibility and editing of assignments | Open: when an assignment is edited in a later stage, does it persist immediately or on save, and what happens to frames already made with the old assignment? Changing a direction invalidates the shot's prompt (different references, different delta) and requires regeneration (see 08). ‹src: jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-170 | LOC | idea | Assigning shots to camera directions › Visibility and editing of assignments | An "Auto-Assign Shots" action in the coverage panel pre-fills assignments by metadata matching and shows proposals for review before committing.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-171 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in the shot list | Read-only badge in the shot inspector with lightweight dropdown reassignment; visual definition stays the main editor (recommended compromise). Related: WD-IDEA-UX-185 and WD-IDEA-UX-186 restate this compromise; the tool split by stage is WD-IDEA-FLOW-064, WD-IDEA-LOC-042 and WD-IDEA-LOC-043. ‹src: 5-1-location-system-intent-source, jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-172 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in the shot list | Full direction editing with thumbnails and coverage hints, as the original spec said.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-173 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in the shot list | Read-only label only.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-174 | LOC | rejected | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in the shot list | Any direction-editing surface in the shot list; visual definition owns direction management.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-175 | LOC | alt,planned | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in prompt review | Read-only transparency (recommended): reference thumbnails labeled by role (MAIN REFERENCE vs SPATIAL CONTEXT), direction alias, delta warnings, and weak-fallback warnings. ‹src: jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-176 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in prompt review | Visible and editable: reassigning marks the shot's prompts stale and requires regeneration (literal spec); users may override the matched view here.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-177 | LOC | alt | Assigning shots to camera directions › Options: direction UI in prompt review | No structured UI; rely on prompt text.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-178 | LOC | alt,impl | How a shot picks and uses location references › Options: references attached per shot | Best match plus establishing (chosen): exactly two location references, the assigned direction as MAIN REFERENCE and the establishing view as SPATIAL CONTEXT, plus all character and prop references; no dropping cascade needed (diminishing returns beyond 3–4 location refs; model limit ≈14). The location text includes the direction's alias and description. When no views exist, the master location image is the fallback.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-179 | LOC | alt | How a shot picks and uses location references › Options: references attached per shot | Best match only.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-180 | LOC | alt,impl | How a shot picks and uses location references › Options: references attached per shot | Up to 3 role-labeled references: best view as main background, establishing as spatial and palette context, and a detail view for close-ups (e.g. eye-level primary plus window detail for a medium shot near the window); the prompt says how to use each.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-181 | LOC | rejected | How a shot picks and uses location references › Options: references attached per shot | Named-shots priority: matched named shot, then the scene-instance location image, then the master image. Replaced by the direction-based fallback chain below.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-182 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Recorded reference strategy per shot: matched direction; fallback to primary direction; fallback to establishing; fallback to base location image; direction assigned but image missing; or text-only.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-183 | LOC | planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Direction assigned and imaged: direction image first as main background, establishing second, and the prompt states angle, distance, height, and intended view.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-184 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Location linked, no direction: fall back to primary direction, else establishing, else base image, with an adaptation note rather than implying an exact match; shown as advisory or risky depending on mode.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-185 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Direction assigned but imageless: use establishing or base image, describe the wanted direction from its metadata, attach a risk notice and adaptation note, and offer generate/upload/establish repairs.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-186 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | No usable location image: generate from text-only location context, warn of weak continuity before generation, and never silently substitute an unrelated location's image.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-187 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Candidates are scoped to the shot's own linked location, then refined by direction; other locations in the same scene never bleed into its prompt, and not all views are attached.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-188 | LOC | impl | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Manifest order: matched direction first, establishing second, then characters, then props. Each attached reference gets a numbered label stating what it is, so the prompt writer knows the role of each image. The numbered order is shown to the user in WD-IDEA-LOC-201.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-189 | LOC | voice | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | When a specific background/view is set for a shot, that image must be the one attached for its frame generation. ‹src: claudeprompt-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-190 | LOC | idea | How a shot picks and uses location references › Fallback chain and honesty | Open: when many characters and props exceed the reference budget, are location references dropped, and in what order?
- WD-IDEA-LOC-191 | LOC | impl | How a shot picks and uses location references › Reference role and prompt support | Location references are attached as style/environment references, not identity/subject references (image models offer subject types for people, animals, products, not places); they carry palette, materials, architectural logic, and atmosphere, and must not overclaim unseen geometry. Characters, props, and a continuity base frame go as identity references.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-192 | LOC | impl,voice | How a shot picks and uses location references › Reference role and prompt support | Delta descriptions: when the reference angle differs from the shot, the prompt says to keep the space but reframe ("reference shows the kitchen at eye level; frame this from a HIGH ANGLE looking down; keep palette, architecture, layout"), and the shot's camera-angle details are always described. The imperfect match is also flagged in the coverage advisory.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-193 | LOC | idea | How a shot picks and uses location references › Reference role and prompt support | Ceiling: style references capture tone, not structure, so delta reframing asks a style reference to do structural work; set user expectations.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-194 | LOC | impl | How a shot picks and uses location references › Reference role and prompt support | Scene bible: lock structured location descriptors (lighting, color grade, architecture, materials, time of day fixed per scene, the direction's alias and description) and repeat them identically in every prompt, so environment language stays stable even when images are imperfect; research calls it the most reliable environment-consistency technique. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-035 (the sequence-wide lighting rule).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-197 | LOC | future | How a shot picks and uses location references › Reference role and prompt support | Structure- and style-conditioning tools (ControlNet / IP-Adapter style) for maximum environment control; needs separate image-model infrastructure.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-200 | LOC | planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Generation package and transparency | One continuity "generation package" per shot/frame is the single source of truth for prompt text and attached images; prompt review previews exactly that package and frame generation consumes it. Contents: chosen location, chosen direction or fallback view, background instructions, camera framing instructions, start- and end-frame reference sets, continuity base when reusing a frame, optional blocking reference, fallback chain and risk notices, and each attachment's reason and role. ‹src: 5-3-location-implementation-plan›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-201 | LOC | impl,planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Generation package and transparency | Prompt review shows a continuity panel per shot: location and direction, reference stack in generation order with thumbnails and roles (identity vs style) and per-reference reasons (matched direction, establishing, base, blocking, continuity base, fallback), fallback/delta notes, risk badges, and an overall continuity-strength indicator.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-202 | LOC | planned | How a shot picks and uses location references › Generation package and transparency | Plain wording in the preview: "using matched direction view", "using establishing view as spatial support", "adapting from similar angle", "falling back to baseline location reference". Example wording also includes "No exact height match" (a plain-language risk when camera height differs) and "Using reverse-direction image plus spatial context. Prompt will adapt framing for a slightly higher camera angle."
- WD-IDEA-LOC-203 | LOC | impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Before generation, a proactive coverage report analyzes every shot's camera and setting/action cues, clusters shots into view groups, and checks each for a matching view: "8 shots eye-level medium: covered", "3 shots high-angle wide: no reference (quality risk)", "2 shots near the window: no zone reference". Users create views or accept risk before spending credits.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-204 | LOC | voice,idea | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | If the shot list includes a reverse angle and no secondary view exists, warn: "No secondary angle reference found for this location; generation quality may be reduced."
- WD-IDEA-LOC-205 | LOC | impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | The advisory is non-blocking and corrective ("Generate Missing Views" / "Proceed Anyway"): it surfaces risk so users make pragmatic tradeoffs. Before generation it lists each location's shots grouped by assigned direction with counts, whether each direction has an image, and unmatched shots with their closest match (see WD-IDEA-LOC-203 and WD-IDEA-LOC-208).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-206 | LOC | impl,planned | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Coverage states: covered (direction has image); fallback/imperfect match (no good direction, primary used); direction without image (e.g. inferred, needs upload or generation); unassigned (never got a direction, distinct from imageless); mismatch (direction belongs to another location); fully covered. Per shot: good, partial, fallback, risky, or unassigned.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-207 | LOC | impl,planned | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Grouping uses canonical shot-location links, one section per location plus an explicit "No linked location" section, replacing setting-text containment.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-208 | LOC | impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Panel: per-location collapsible sections with status ("Partial Coverage", "X/Y shots covered"), shot count, view inventory with image state (attached / none / inferred only), direction groups with alias, source badge, shot count, thumbnail, and covered/gap status; unmatched shots get direction dropdowns (the shot-to-direction table in WD-IDEA-LOC-210 has thumbnails and dropdowns for reassigning any shot) and counts update immediately; per-direction generate and "Generate N Missing Views". Hidden for scenes with no location asset.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-209 | LOC | impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Established views appear distinctly with provenance, e.g. "Direction 1 'stove wall' (ESTABLISHED — Scene 2, Shot 3)". They sit alongside user-uploaded and missing views in the panel.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-210 | LOC | planned,impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | A risk list states specific problems ("Shot 13 needs reverse coverage", "Shot 15 is using weak fallback from primary", "Shot 16 has no direction assignment"); an assignment table shows shot, setting, linked location, assigned view, Good/Risky/Fallback verdict, and quick change. Layout: summary strip (mode, risk counts), then per-location view inventory, risk list, table, action row.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-211 | LOC | planned | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Baseline problems (shot not linked to a location) are visually separate from advanced direction-coverage problems: related but not equivalent.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-212 | LOC | planned,impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Gaps must be actionable in one step, not only visible. Repairs: create view, add direction, create default views, assign shots, generate a missing image (from establishing or primary), batch-generate, use an approved frame as a view, create inferred view shells.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-213 | LOC | planned,impl | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Severity follows continuity mode: an unassigned direction or a fallback is advisory in default mode and a stronger warning in advanced mode.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-214 | LOC | planned | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Clear empty states: no views, only an establishing reference, all shots on primary fallback. With zero directions show a call to action ("No camera directions defined for this location. Set up directions to enable shot assignment") instead of a dropdown holding only "Unassigned"; options: hide the dropdown, disable it with a tooltip, or offer inline "Create direction".
- WD-IDEA-LOC-215 | LOC | idea | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | A thin progress bar beside "X/Y shots covered" (red/amber at 0%, amber partial, emerald at 100%).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-216 | LOC | idea | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) | Coverage work should feel like creative progression, not diagnosis and repair; direction naming and default-view tools should feel intent-driven, not technical. ‹src: 4-15-location-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-217 | LOC | planned | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) › Discoverability | Deferred location assets must not hide direction controls; warnings need direct actions ("you have a gap" → "fill this gap" in one step); direction setup is reachable from visual definition. Options: menu entry on deferred rows, a "Set Up Camera Directions" entry in the coverage panel, or auto-activate/surface locations whenever scenes depend on them. Stage 8 notifies when a scene's location is deferred ("This scene's location X is deferred. Activate it to enable camera direction assignment") and may auto-activate locations in scene dependencies. Related: WD-IDEA-ASSET-068 (deferred-asset menus make direction controls unreachable today).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-218 | LOC | idea | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) › Discoverability | Bidirectional navigation between the direction manager and the coverage panel ("Define directions" / "See shot assignments").
- WD-IDEA-LOC-219 | LOC | idea | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) › Discoverability | A guided quick-start ("Set Up Location Directions") from asset definition or visual definition collapses five scattered steps (activate, open directions, define, open coverage, assign) into pick location → suggest default views → assign shots.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-220 | LOC | future | Coverage advisory (visual definition stage) › Discoverability | Project-wide coverage summary and per-scene gap indicators show which scenes still carry risk and where to spend effort; richer dashboards and smarter auto-assignment and review later. Proposed form: a red/amber/green dot per scene row in the Script Hub for unresolved coverage gaps, plus a project-wide coverage dashboard.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-221 | LOC | impl,planned | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | A Basic/Advanced continuity mode, persisted per project and defaulting to Basic, visible in the coverage panel and the frame-generation mode bar; also considered per scene or as a session preference. The mode is visibly framed in the relevant stages; per-scene mode overrides on top of the project setting are a future extension (WD-IDEA-UX-265).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-222 | LOC | planned | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | Enter the advanced tier through intentional, clearly labeled entry points ("Advanced Location Continuity", "Camera Directions", "Precision Background Control") when the user asks, drift repeats, a scene has many repeated or reverse angles, or the user opts in; never mandatory for basic setup.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-223 | LOC | planned | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | The system proactively suggests advanced mode on many shots in one location, repeated reverse angles, repeated fallbacks, or many manual direction corrections, with nudges like "Need stronger background consistency?" or "This location would benefit from advanced view coverage." ‹src: 5-3-location-implementation-plan›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-224 | LOC | impl | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | In Basic mode, visual definition shows a dismissible strip of continuity suggestions drawn from project health; it is the progressive-disclosure door to advanced controls.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-225 | LOC | impl,planned | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | Continuity health metrics per project or scene: unresolved and ambiguous locations, direction gaps, shots on fallback or weak references, shots with a selected base, frames generated from a base, and frame-reuse rate, with improvement suggestions.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-226 | LOC | planned,future | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure | Optional strict mode strengthens warnings or blocks progression when advanced continuity is on or for high-value projects; advisory behavior ships first. A strict gate already exists in legacy Advanced mode for Stage 10 Lock and Proceed (WD-IDEA-UX-273); this bullet covers extending strictness beyond that.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-227 | LOC | alt | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure › Options: how the advanced tier is enabled | A toggle.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-228 | LOC | alt | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure › Options: how the advanced tier is enabled | An entry point from an empty state.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-229 | LOC | alt | Continuity modes and progressive disclosure › Options: how the advanced tier is enabled | Progressive disclosure.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-230 | LOC | impl,voice | Established views (hero frames) | An approved frame can be locked as the established view for its direction, so later shots facing that way use the generated frame instead of the original asset image. The library grows organically during production: the AI's first invented details become the consistency reference, no extra uploads. Elevated to a core feature as the "hero frame" pattern, the single most impactful consistency technique. After approval the user is offered "Lock this as an established view for [Location] from [angle]?". ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning, 3.7-location-concern-notes, 5-1-location-system-intent-source›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-231 | LOC | impl,idea,planned | Established views (hero frames) | The loop bridges the light default and the advanced library: established views feed the direction library so later frames for that direction converge, and default-mode generation turns into advanced structure without upfront authoring.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-232 | LOC | impl | Established views (hero frames) | After approval, if the frame's direction has no image, a prominent "This fills a coverage gap!" banner names location and direction and offers "Lock as Established View" / "Skip". If the direction already has an image, only a subtle "Lock as established view for [direction]" link appears.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-233 | LOC | impl,alt | Established views (hero frames) | No establish prompt appears for shots without an assigned direction. The prompt is proactive only when it fills a gap (the direction has no image); otherwise only a subtle link appears (WD-IDEA-LOC-232). The alternative (prompt also for unassigned shots) is WD-IDEA-LOC-234. ‹src: 3.7-location-system-enhancement-(master-doc)›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-234 | LOC | alt | Established views (hero frames) | Alternative: show the prompt even without a direction, with a dropdown to choose which direction to establish, turning a rare passive feature into an active step. ‹src: 3.7-location-rd-2-UIUX-improove›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-235 | LOC | idea | Established views (hero frames) | Make it visible that established views improve future generations.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-236 | LOC | future | Established views (hero frames) | Auto-lock the first approved frame per direction as a preference toggle; manual by default to keep user control.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-237 | LOC | impl | Established views (hero frames) | Provenance: the view records source scene, shot, and frame, and the frame records which view it became; users can navigate back to the source frame and query which scenes produced established views; the view inherits camera distance and height from its source shot.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-238 | LOC | impl | Established views (hero frames) | Established views can be removed or replaced anytime from the direction manager (reverting to no image or swapping another frame/upload) with no unlock ceremony; a re-establish flow covers a later, better frame or a changed story.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-239 | LOC | alt,impl | Established views (hero frames) › Options: what locking does when a reference already exists | Replace after a side-by-side comparison of current reference and new frame; replacing archives the old reference for rollback; keeping cancels the lock.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-240 | LOC | alt | Established views (hero frames) › Options: what locking does when a reference already exists | Overwrite the existing direction's image and source in place.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-241 | LOC | alt | Established views (hero frames) › Options: what locking does when a reference already exists | Coexist as an alternative reference, or let the user choose.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-242 | LOC | alt,planned | Established views (hero frames) › Options: what locking does when a reference already exists | Promotion choices: add the frame as a new direction view, replace the current direction image ("Replace Existing Reverse View"), or attach it to the location as a stronger baseline reference, recording origin and promotion reason.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-243 | LOC | voice | Continuity across shots and scenes | Continuity anchor problem: two shots from the same angle in the same room (shots 3 and 7) should have identical backgrounds (wall color, furniture placement, lighting), yet independent generation drifts even from the same reference; a locked view of camera position A becomes the reference for every later position-A shot. A non-adjacent frame link to an earlier shot in the same room (WD-IDEA-CONT-181 to WD-IDEA-CONT-184) is a complementary way to hold the same background. ‹src: 3.7-location-concern-notes›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-245 | LOC | planned | Continuity across shots and scenes | Views live at the project level, shared by every scene's instance of the location, with no per-scene duplication, so a location's views stay consistent across scenes.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-246 | LOC | planned | Continuity across shots and scenes | Location continuity with modification: a recurring location (the protagonist's bedroom in the first and final scenes) must stay recognizably the same place while reflecting story change (a diploma now on the wall). Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-052 (assets evolve in both directions while the chain stays intact).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-248 | LOC | alt,future | Continuity across shots and scenes › Options: project-level vs scene-level views and scene overrides | Defer scene-specific view overrides (recommended): no data model or UX existed, assignments are project-global, and the feature adds asset state and complexity.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-271 | LOC | alt | Continuity across shots and scenes › Options: project-level vs scene-level views and scene overrides | Named-shots era: whether location shots live at the project level (asset definition, Stage 5, global to the project) or per scene (visual definition, Stage 8) was left unresolved; the spec recommended project-level shots with scene-specific overrides in Stage 8, mirroring how character descriptions work. Later location planning instead recommended deferring scene overrides (WD-IDEA-LOC-248). ‹src: 3.7-phase-3-EXPANDEDOUT-location-multi-shot, 3.7-threads›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-249 | LOC | alt | Continuity across shots and scenes › Options: project-level vs scene-level views and scene overrides | Per-scene text description override only.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-250 | LOC | alt,planned | Continuity across shots and scenes › Options: project-level vs scene-level views and scene overrides | Full per-scene image and description variants added in visual definition (this scene's kitchen has smoke damage or a broken window), following the scene-specific character description pattern while other scenes keep normal views; scene location shots appear in the master reference carousel. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-078 and WD-IDEA-CONT-079 (the reference-history carousel).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-251 | LOC | alt,future | Continuity across shots and scenes › Options: project-level vs scene-level views and scene overrides | For a location transformed by the story (a room destroyed): post-transformation variants of its views, or the transformed scene instance gets its own views. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-104 to WD-IDEA-CONT-109 (the asset transformation model).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-252 | LOC | idea | Time of day, weather, and state variants | An environment description in a frame prompt covers location type, architecture/materials, furniture and surfaces with condition, props with rough positions, depth layers (fore/mid/background), atmospheric visuals (dust motes, steam, fog, rain on glass), time of day as visible light (not a narrative beat), and weather only as visible. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-253 | LOC | planned | Time of day, weather, and state variants | Each shot's setting describes the specific part of the location visible in that shot, its lighting, time-of-day cues, and atmosphere.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-254 | LOC | idea | Time of day, weather, and state variants | A location may need more than one establishing view (a day and a night version), so view naming must allow variants like establishing_day / establishing_night. ‹src: 3.7-location-system-enhance-analysis›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-255 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | Film-studio model: worlds are generated per unique location, not per scene; a location used in scenes 3, 7, and 11 gets one world, revisited to capture angles for each (blocking: see 07).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-256 | LOC | rejected | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | Regenerating the world per frame or per scene: one world per location.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-257 | LOC | future | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | 3D previsualization: 360° panoramas of environments in a game engine, style-transferred into backgrounds that are consistent by construction; full previs tooling later.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-258 | LOC | voice | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | A world built from a few environment references from different directions is sloppy (noise, artifacts, not photoreal) but gives a working semblance of the scene's 3D space, which is enough. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-LOC-259 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | Studio Lobby: a lot overview with a card per unique location showing name, scenes that use it, saved-angle count, and world status (not generated / generating / ready), each with "Enter Studio".
- WD-IDEA-LOC-260 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | World generation is lazy: it starts on first "Enter Studio", avoiding spend on locations never blocked.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-261 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | Seed images come from the location's existing references, optionally plus 1–2 extra angles; a light "World Gen Images" subsection on the location tags which references to use and accepts drag-ins, feeling like a minor addition, not a new flow.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-262 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") | Ideal input is 3–5 images: establishing wide/aerial (required, layout and scale), eye-level hero (required, main dramatic position), opposite wall/reverse (required, depth both ways), detail/texture close-up (optional), light-source direction (optional). Three is minimum viable; more than five has diminishing returns.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-263 | LOC | alt,impl | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Options: world input modes | Guided: four azimuth drop slots (Front 0°, Right 90°, Back 180°, Left 270°) with previews, remove, and hints (eye-level hero, side, opposite wall/reverse, window wall).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-264 | LOC | alt,impl | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Options: world input modes | Free: 2–8 images in any arrangement; spatial layout inferred.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-265 | LOC | alt,impl | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Options: world input modes | Text only, single video, or single image (optionally flagged panoramic).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-266 | LOC | alt | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Options: world input modes | Earlier plain multi-image uploader (drag and drop) recommending establishing wide/aerial, eye-level hero, and reverse, with removable thumbnails.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-267 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Angle capture | Integrated, the directional slots map to the location's existing views (establishing = front; other directions = right/back/left).
- WD-IDEA-LOC-268 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Angle capture | Angle Library per location: every capture is saved with location, timestamp, optional label ("over-shoulder reverse"), and the scene/shot selected at capture; visible in the studio for labeling, deleting, and seeing assignments.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-269 | LOC | planned | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Angle capture | A scene selector inside a location's studio lists every scene using it and their shots; selecting a target shot auto-tags captures; unassigned angles can be captured for later sorting.
- WD-IDEA-LOC-270 | LOC | impl,future | 3D worlds per location ("film studio") › Angle capture | Worlds can be swapped at runtime within a session, loaded pre-built to skip generation, or produced by a different provider without changing blocking; a generated world links back to its provider's editor.

## 07 Shots, Cinematography & Previs (07-SHOTS-CINEMATOGRAPHY-PREVIS.md)

- WD-IDEA-SHOT-001 | SHOT | idea | The shot list's role | The shot list is the most critical production control point: it defines exactly what happens in the scene (mechanical breakdown into discrete, time-bounded shots), not how assets look. It must be locked before scene asset work begins.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-002 | SHOT | impl | The shot list's role | When a scene has no shot list, one is extracted automatically on entry; the workspace is a shot table/list beside a detail editor for the selected shot.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-004 | SHOT | rejected | The shot list's role | "Rearview Mirror": a persistent panel in the shot list stage showing the previous scene's final action, dialogue and final frame, so the new scene's first shots hand off seamlessly. Judged possibly not useful enough and replaced by the multi-tab content panel; its rearview tab is WD-IDEA-CONT-215. ‹src: project-overview, user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-005 | SHOT | idea | The shot list's role | Each scene's shot list carries continuity notes explaining how it connects to the prior and next scenes; its first shot must match the prior scene's ending positions and states, and the setting must match or transition explicitly (see 04). Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-073 (explicit setting-transition rule) and WD-IDEA-CONT-135.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-008 | SHOT | idea | The shot list's role | Expected quality of a generated shot list: medium shots for dialogue alternating with close-ups on emotional beats; technically specific camera language (MS, CU, tracking, POV, Dutch, handheld); dialogue spread across shots; correct character prominence; fast sequences split into more shots; total duration matching the target length. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-009 | SHOT | idea | The shot list's role | For audio-driven projects, shots line up with the audio's timing, the dialogue field carries transcript excerpts, and action describes visuals that complement the audio (see 09). Related: WD-IDEA-GEN-067 (audio synchronization first) and WD-IDEA-INPUT-008. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-010 | SHOT | future,alt | The shot list's role | With a fully supplied script or storyboard package, the shot list stage imports or extracts shot lists instead of inventing them; storyboard panel labels (1A, 1B, 2A) are matched to script/outline labels, and strong storyboard data is distilled and categorized rather than reinterpreted (see 02B). Related: WD-IDEA-INPUT-109 (storyboard Stage 1 input, same status). ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-011 | SHOT | idea | The shot unit and duration | A shot is the atomic video unit: one primary action or one dialogue exchange.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-012 | SHOT | impl | The shot unit and duration | Shots default to 8 seconds because that matches the video generator's clip length; duration is adjustable per shot, and other lengths should carry a justification (4–6 s suggested for complex action).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-013 | SHOT | idea | The shot unit and duration | If an action doesn't finish within the clip, shorten the action or lengthen the duration. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-014 | SHOT | alt | The shot unit and duration › Options: shot duration model | Fixed: every shot is exactly 8 seconds unless explicitly justified ("8-second shot definitions"; golden tests expect exactly 8 s). ‹src: project-overview, golden-test-evaluation-framework, AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-015 | SHOT | alt | The shot unit and duration › Options: shot duration model | Discrete: 4, 6, or 8 seconds; 4–6 s for complex or action-heavy beats, 8 s for atmospheric shots. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-016 | SHOT | alt | The shot unit and duration › Options: shot duration model | Flexible: any duration from 1 to 30 s is valid, 4–12 s is recommended, 8 s is only the default (see WD-IDEA-SHOT-064). ‹src: current-app-user-flow, architecture-and-rules›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-017 | SHOT | alt | The shot unit and duration › Options: how much can happen inside one clip | One major action per shot: multi-action clips fragment into soft or smeared motion, so a chain of actions is split across shots. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-018 | SHOT | alt | The shot unit and duration › Options: how much can happen inside one clip | Timestamp prompting: one video prompt choreographs timed beats inside one clip in roughly 2-second segments, each with its own action, camera change (even a cut to a reverse shot) and audio cue; the start frame still anchors the visuals. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-019 | SHOT | idea | Shot fields | Each shot records: shot ID and order, duration, verbatim dialogue, an atomic physical action, characters present with prominence (e.g. "John (foreground, profile), Mary (background, blurry)"), a specific setting with lighting detail, a camera spec (e.g. "CU – Dolly In (Slow) – High Angle"), continuity flags, and an optional link to the story beat it serves.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-020 | SHOT | impl | Shot fields | Shot IDs are human-readable: scene number plus letter (3A, 3B); shot order can be changed.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-021 | SHOT | idea | Shot fields | Split shots take suffixed IDs (3A → 3A-1, 3A-2); a merged shot keeps the first shot's ID with an "-M" suffix. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-022 | SHOT | impl | Shot fields | Every character in a shot is marked foreground, background, or off-screen.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-023 | SHOT | planned | Shot fields | Foreground/background characters carry relative position (left/center/right), distance from camera, and facing direction. ‹src: 3.8-phase2-shot-list-action-iteration›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-024 | SHOT | idea | Shot fields | Dialogue extensions carry visual meaning: O.S. means present in the scene but off-screen (voice only, no render needed), V.O. is voiceover; asset and frame stages use this to decide whether a character must be rendered in a shot. Related: WD-IDEA-CONT-009 (off-screen dependency rule). ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-025 | SHOT | idea | Shot fields | Dialogue structure informs shot choices (two-shots, over-the-shoulder, close-ups), and who is speaking determines camera focus when composing frames. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-026 | SHOT | planned | Shot fields | The setting field describes the specific part of the location visible in this shot, its lighting, time-of-day cues and atmosphere.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-027 | SHOT | voice | Shot fields | Location attribution must be specific per shot, not a vague "interior – Hero's home" repeated for every shot in the scene, so the right references can be inferred (see 06B). ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-029 | SHOT | impl | Shot fields | Each shot has a toggle for whether it needs an end frame: start+end is the default; start-only suits models that don't use end frames (see 08).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-030 | SHOT | impl | Camera language | A camera spec has three parts, shot type – angle – movement, drawn from a standard vocabulary: EWS/WS/MS/MCU/CU/ECU; eye level, low, high, bird's-eye, Dutch, worm's-eye; static, dolly, pan, truck, crane, handheld, steadicam. Optional framing notes ("subject frame-left, looking frame-right").
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-031 | SHOT | planned | Camera language | The camera field also states lens feel (telephoto compression vs wide-angle distortion) alongside shot type, angle and planned movement. ‹src: 3.8-phase2-shot-list-action-iteration›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-032 | SHOT | idea | Camera language | The vocabulary has canonical meanings: EWS establishes scale, WS gives spatial context, MS for dialogue, MCU for emotion with context, CU for intimacy, ECU for intense detail; low angle = power, high = vulnerability, Dutch = unease, bird's-eye = omniscience, worm's-eye = towering; lens options include shallow/deep focus, wide-angle, telephoto compression, anamorphic, fisheye.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-033 | SHOT | idea | Camera language | Camera movements have intended effects: dolly-in for intimacy/focus, dolly-out to reveal context, tracking for dynamic participation, crane for dramatic reveals, subtle handheld for documentary realism, shaky handheld for chaos, gimbal/steadicam for polish, dolly zoom for disorientation.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-034 | SHOT | idea | Camera language | Every video prompt states camera movement explicitly or says "static", using exact terms ("slow dolly in") from a fixed vocabulary (pan, tilt, dolly, truck, crane, arc, handheld, steadicam, rack focus, zoom, push-in, pull-out), never vague words like "cinematic".
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-035 | SHOT | impl | Camera language | Static/moving split: a frame prompt gets only where the camera *is* (shot type, angle, lens character, focal plane); movement belongs only to the video prompt; rack focus is a video element because it changes over time. One rich camera description therefore feeds the frame prompt, the video prompt, and character-angle matching (see 08). The legacy prompt-assembly rules implement this split.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-036 | SHOT | impl | Camera language | The shot's camera angle and movement must actually shape how generated frames are composed, and the frame prompt must describe it.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-037 | SHOT | idea | Camera language | Controlled spatial vocabulary for placement: frame left/right, foreground/midground/background, center frame, facing camera/profile/three-quarter/back to camera, above/below frame line, overlapping.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-038 | SHOT | impl | Camera language | Structured camera metadata per shot (camera distance, camera height, movement, plus the assigned location direction) exists alongside the free-text camera spec, is generated at shot-list time, and needs user-facing exposure.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-039 | SHOT | impl,voice | Camera language | Two-track camera metadata: the AI emits structured fields when it can; otherwise a deterministic parser derives them from free text ("WS – High Angle – Slow Pan Right" → wide / high / slow pan right), mapping shot sizes to wide/medium/close, angle words to heights (bird's-eye→overhead, worm's-eye→ground, Dutch→eye level) and movement words with slow/fast; this also covers legacy and hand-edited shots. Both tracks were chosen in the questionnaire.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-041 | SHOT | future | Camera language | Richer per-shot cinematography (real lens selection, film stock, color grade reference) exported as a cinematography block for generation. ‹src: CineBlock-system-overview›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-042 | SHOT | voice,planned | Descriptive, directional shots | Legacy notes call shot descriptions "lazy" (often a line of dialogue plus buzzwords) and must become richly descriptive and directional, especially if frames come out incoherent: they are the preamble to the frame and video prompts, so improvements propagate.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-043 | SHOT | planned | Descriptive, directional shots | Crew-note style: written like a director's notes to a cinematographer, covering camera angle and motion, lighting, mise-en-scène framing, character positioning and movement, and environment changes (e.g. "WIDE — Kitchen, morning light camera-left. SPONGEBOB at stove, back to camera, spatula raised theatrically. PATRICK enters camera-right, sleepy slouch, dragging blanket.").
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-044 | SHOT | impl | Descriptive, directional shots | Action describes what a viewer sees, not the story: blocking and position in frame, physical choreography and spatial relationships ("A leans forward across the table, pointing at B, who recoils with arms crossed"), environmental details and implied lighting. Vague actions ("they talk") are forbidden.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-046 | SHOT | voice,planned | Descriptive, directional shots | A character's signature performance style travels into prompts (e.g. SpongeBob's Chaplin-esque, over-expressive facial acting). ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-2(3.8)›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-049 | SHOT | voice,planned | Descriptive, directional shots | Improve shot detail by iterating on the existing extraction, not rebuilding it. ‹src: 3.8-threads›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-050 | SHOT | planned | Descriptive, directional shots | Guardrail: richer descriptions must not regress shot count, dialogue accuracy or character assignment.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-051 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Every field is directly editable; the shot inspector shows the selected shot expanded (duration, action, dialogue, characters, setting, camera, beat reference) with the beat reference and the AI's rationale.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-052 | SHOT | idea | Editing the shot list | Field coherence: editing one field (e.g. Wide → Close Up) near-instantly regenerates dependent fields (action, dialogue) so the shot stays coherent. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-053 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Split: AI divides one shot at a natural action or dialogue break into two (or up to three) shots that together cover the same narrative content, with adjusted camera specs, kept or refined continuity flags, and a rationale for the split point; no new story content is invented.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-054 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Split and merge accept optional user guidance (e.g. "split at the moment John stands up").
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-055 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Split uses "stretch" semantics: each new shot keeps the original's full duration rather than a fraction, so every resulting clip can use the generator's full time limit.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-056 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Merge: two adjacent shots (with the next or the previous) become one continuous beat; dialogue, action, characters, setting, camera and continuity flags are combined without invention, and durations are summed.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-057 | SHOT | voice,idea | Editing the shot list | Split and merge handle dialogue intelligently: on split each line goes to the shot where it belongs, on merge lines combine in order; the user can still edit.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-058 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | The merge dialog leads with a clear "Merge with: Next (preselected) / Previous" toggle at the top.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-059 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | Shots can be deleted; edits autosave after a short pause.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-060 | SHOT | impl | Editing the shot list | The last shot in a scene can't be deleted; the warning instead offers to cancel or to defer/sideline the whole scene and return to the Script Hub (see 02).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-061 | SHOT | idea | Editing the shot list | Optional tempo agent offers non-destructive pacing suggestions.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-062 | SHOT | voice,idea | Editing the shot list | The shot list stage needs more care: a collapsible right sidebar or pull-up panel shows the exact script and beat-sheet passages relevant to the selected shot. ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-063 | SHOT | impl | Validation, locking and invalidation | Blocking errors at lock: empty shot list, duplicate shot IDs, missing action, setting or camera, duration under 1 s or over 30 s.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-064 | SHOT | impl | Validation, locking and invalidation | Overridable warnings ("Lock Anyway" / proceed anyway): duration outside 4–12 s, unusually short or long total scene duration, character names missing from the scene's dependencies with "Did you mean?" typo suggestions, and missing location links.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-065 | SHOT | impl | Validation, locking and invalidation | Locking validates locally then on the server; one dialog lists every issue with a "Fix Issues" path.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-069 | SHOT | voice,idea | Directing style and creative shot design | Shot/"Auteur" style capsule, separate from the visual style capsule (color, medium): it sets how scenes are shot the way different directors would (number of shots, shot types, how shots follow each other, framing) and mainly affects shot list creation and frame/video prompts (see 06 for capsules). ‹src: 3.7-tasks-part-3›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-070 | SHOT | voice,idea,future | Directing style and creative shot design | Shot-planning complexity modes: a "shot director style" from super-dynamic coverage down to minimal single-wall shooting, e.g. a simple South Park-style 2D mode with minimal camera direction versus full cinematic coverage. ‹src: jump-a-gun-discrepancies, 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-071 | SHOT | voice | Directing style and creative shot design | AI suggests experimental shooting techniques for chosen moments, at a user-set frequency, drawing on a compiled memory of legendary shots from film and anime history, beyond basic CU/MS coverage. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-072 | SHOT | voice | Directing style and creative shot design | More stylized shots and deliberately planned transitions such as match cuts. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-073 | SHOT | voice,future | Directing style and creative shot design | "Film sampling": like hip-hop sampling or Tarantino's borrowing, the user supplies a clip from an existing film to emulate its editing, blocking, choreography, pacing or stylized sequence, re-expressed in their own story, setting and visual style (e.g. a car chase re-staged in a Pixar-style alien story in the Rocky Mountains). Related: WD-IDEA-ASSET-222 (film reference deconstruction for style) and WD-IDEA-FLOW-130 (film-sample intake item). ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-074 | SHOT | voice,future | Directing style and creative shot design | The sampled clip is a key reference for the scene's shot list, frame prompts/generation and/or video generation. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-075 | SHOT | voice,future | Directing style and creative shot design | Sampling input: upload the actual video, or more conveniently just describe the clip in text. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-076 | SHOT | impl | Shots and locations | Each shot links to a location asset: assign manually, clear, or let the system resolve automatically; missing links surface as warnings (see 06B).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-077 | SHOT | impl | Shots and locations | Every shot resolves to one of its location's camera directions ("wall"/view), which determines the background reference image for its frame: an explicitly assigned direction, otherwise the scored match, otherwise the primary direction (see WD-IDEA-LOC-152 and LOC-164).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-081 | SHOT | impl | Shots and locations | Metadata matching is preferred over AI matching for direction choice: deterministic, fast and free; AI adds cost and latency for marginal gain, and user override covers edge cases.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-082 | SHOT | voice,idea | Shots and locations | Between scenes and shots, a control for how many location views shots should use (many varied views vs only one or two), i.e. how creative extraction is with view assignment. ‹src: jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-083 | SHOT | voice,idea | Shots and locations | Smart detection of in-scene location movement from the script, with shot extraction tuned to notice setting changes within a scene. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-084 | SHOT | idea | Shots and locations | Directions are AI-suggested but user-confirmable; the assignment persists per shot and is used at generation time. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-085 | SHOT | alt | Shots and locations › Options: characters moving between sub-locations within one scene | Stage 7 assigns a sub-location and direction per shot (selected). ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-088 | SHOT | alt | Shots and locations › Options: how much direction editing the shot list stage exposes | Full direction editing in Stage 7 right after generation (the original spec): a modal or assignment table with overrides where users pick or verify each shot's wall, with thumbnails and coverage hints. ‹src: 3.7-location-pre-planning, jump-a-gun-discrepancies›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-090 | SHOT | alt | Shots and locations › Options: how much direction editing the shot list stage exposes | Read-only direction label only.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-091 | SHOT | impl | Shots and assets | Each asset's presence in a shot has a type that decides frame references: "throughout" (start and end frame), "enters" (end frame only), "exits" (start frame only), "passes through" (video only, no frame reference).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-092 | SHOT | idea | Shots and assets | Shot-level asset relevance: attaching every scene asset to every shot's prompt is too blunt, and imported storyboards or blocking data must not attach every asset to every shot (see 06 for default assignment rules). Related: the relevance step in WD-IDEA-ASSET-154 and WD-IDEA-CONT-016. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-093 | SHOT | idea | Shots and assets | If AI shot-asset assignment (including presence type) is poor, a user-facing correction UI in Stages 8 or 9; presence correctness is critical for continuity.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-095 | SHOT | idea | Shots into frames and video | Prompt segmentation produces a separate frame (still) prompt and video (motion) prompt per shot (see 08).
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-096 | SHOT | future,voice | Shots into frames and video | "Extend Shot": lengthen a generated shot with the video provider's extend capability, with cost tracking; treated as a research spike before any build. Extension parameters: initial clips of 4, 6 or 8 s, each extension about 7 s, chained up to 20 times (about 148 s) while keeping visual consistency. Related: WD-IDEA-AV-040 (choose-the-best-take-first workflow). ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-097 | SHOT | voice,future | Shots into frames and video | Users submit a rough sketch to guide a shot's visuals. ‹src: Issues›
