- WD-IDEA-SHOT-098 | SHOT | idea | Shots into frames and video | Sketches and doodles are composition/blocking guides, never final identity or visual style unless explicitly tagged; they combine with text and asset references to generate real frames.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-099 | SHOT | planned,future | Blocking references | Shots may carry an optional blocking/composition reference (previs capture, storyboard frame, sketch, rough layout, other guide) that controls camera framing, subject placement, rough pose, screen direction and visible/occluded relationships. Location references supply environment identity and material detail only, never angle, crop or staging (see WD-IDEA-SHOT-102); the composition rules are WD-IDEA-GEN-173.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-100 | SHOT | planned | Blocking references | A blocking reference is never treated as character identity, prop identity, location appearance, texture, lighting or art direction unless explicitly marked so.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-101 | SHOT | planned,voice | Blocking references | With a blocking reference, the prompt states that mannequins, primitives, sketch marks and placeholder backgrounds are layout guides only, to be replaced by the canonical character, prop and location assets. The prompt also asks the model to produce the frame as the user framed it.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-102 | SHOT | planned,voice | Blocking references | Division of authority: the blocking still owns framing, camera angle and crop; location references supply environment identity and material detail only; character/prop references supply appearance only, not screen placement. A wide, high-angle location reference must not pull a tight shot (a bedroom corner seen from the bed) back toward the wide view. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-103 | SHOT | planned | Blocking references | Blocking is enabled per shot or per scene and can include separate start and end blocking frames.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-104 | SHOT | planned | Blocking references | Conflicts between blocking metadata and the shot's text, assigned assets or location state are surfaced at the prompt stage, before frame generation.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-105 | SHOT | future | Blocking references | The core rollout reserves room for blocking references (composition, start and end roles) without building a specific previs tool, so later sketch/previs inputs need no new prompt or attachment path. The blocking roles (composition reference, blocking start and end frames) are reserved in the reference-role model but not built.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-106 | SHOT | future | Blocking references | Full previs tooling. ‹src: 7-19-location-stabilization-master-plan›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-107 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | Conceived as a crude shot-listing and blocking tool feeding AI video: block each shot in a rough 3D world, capture the still as the reference frame, then send still + text prompt + relevant asset images (characters, props, location) to the image/video model. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-108 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | The 3D world comes from a few reference images of the environment taken in different directions; it is sloppy (noise, artifacts, not photoreal) but gives a workable semblance of the scene's 3D space, which is enough (see 06B for world-generation inputs). ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-109 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | Characters are only mannequins and props are basic primitives (cube, cone) that can be placed and moved; the real characters populate the frame at generation time. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-110 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | The user lists a scene's shots with per-shot info, then goes shot by shot inside the environment getting framing and blocking right for each shot's start and end frame. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-111 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | Key insight: current models given a rough layout doodle plus a prompt plus asset attachments can produce a precise frame; the CineBlock still replaces the hand-made doodle. The test case combined the rough layout and prompt with a person, a large prop and a background as attachments. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-112 | SHOT | voice | CineBlock: concept | Core bet: world generation takes about a minute and moving mannequins is easy, so an ~8-shot scene can be blocked in under a minute; if blocking stills reliably steer generation, the whole pipeline becomes viable. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-113 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | The world never needs to be photorealistic: rough spatial reference plus detailed asset descriptions should yield strong results. ‹src: prd-1, voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-114 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | Three-input division of labor at frame generation: the frame prompt (text), the asset visual states (appearance, wardrobe, condition), and the blocking capture (composition, camera angle, subject positions); the model matches the capture's arrangement while rendering characters as defined.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-115 | SHOT | impl | CineBlock: concept | Defined as a browser-based previs tool: generate a world, block with mannequins and primitive props, light it, frame and capture through a virtual camera, and export a shot package (start/end frames per shot plus structured shot metadata) designed to become the structured brief for AI generation in the main product.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-116 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | "Virtual Production Studio": a navigable 3D world built from the project's location assets lets the user physically block the film before committing to expensive generation; snapshots become spatial reference frames.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-117 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | Core loop: generate the world once, walk around, place mannequins, take screenshots, pick the best angle per shot, then generate frames with spatial context locked in.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-118 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | Output routing: captures go to frame generation as composition references, blocking camera metadata (distance, height, movement) fills the shot's camera fields, and asset placement informs shot-level asset assignment. This routing applies once the blocking tool is integrated; until then the camera fields are not filled from blocking.
- WD-IDEA-SHOT-120 | SHOT | planned | CineBlock: concept | Angle Library per location: every capture is saved to the location's library, tagged with location, timestamp, optional label ("over-shoulder reverse") and the scene/shot selected at capture; viewable in the studio with labelling, deletion and shot assignments (see 06B for one world per location). ‹src: prd-1›

## 08 Generation & Prompting (08-GENERATION-AND-PROMPTING.md)

- WD-IDEA-GEN-001 | GEN | impl | Core model | Every shot gets purpose-built prompts: a frame prompt (sent to an image model for the start frame and, optionally, the end frame, each with its own prompt) and a video prompt (sent to the video model, which already has the frame(s) as its visual anchor). Stage 9 produces them; frame and video prompts follow different rules.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-002 | GEN | - | Core model | Three canonical prompt types that must never be merged: frame prompts (image only; visually verbose, asset-heavy, spatially explicit), video prompts (video only; action, dialogue, accents, timing, SFX, minimal visuals), and hidden system scaffold prompts (never editable; encode intent such as "this image is a video anchor frame" plus dependency rules). ‹src: project-overview, README›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-003 | GEN | rejected | Core model | One monolithic prompt per shot mixing visuals, action and audio. Replaced by the frame + video pair because mixed prompts dilute action clarity, cause character drift and make lighting and composition inconsistent. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-004 | GEN | - | Core model | Governing rule: the frame prompt defines what the world LOOKS like; the video prompt defines what HAPPENS in it. If a detail is visible in a still, it goes in the frame prompt; if it only shows up when you press play, it goes in the video prompt.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-005 | GEN | idea | Core model | Verbosity alignment: frame prompts as descriptive as possible, video prompts as lean as possible. Visual description in a video prompt is weight in the wrong place: it dilutes action adherence and tends to be ignored; stripping it also makes audio and dialogue direction clearer.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-006 | GEN | - | Core model | Because prompts are split, look and performance iterate independently: change the look without touching the action, or the action without regenerating the look.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-007 | GEN | - | Core model | Model economics: a cheap image model handles high-volume anchor frames; the expensive video model (with audio) is used only for final video and must accept two anchor frames plus a combined video/audio prompt (see 01, "the Inversion").
- WD-IDEA-GEN-008 | GEN | voice | Core model | Video generation is image-to-video: a start frame, an optional end frame and a video prompt; the model generates the in-between frames, and the provider produces audio together with the picture.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-009 | GEN | - | Core model | "Ingredients to video" order: generate reference images first, then the start frame from the frame prompt plus references, then the video from the anchor frame plus a lean video prompt.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-010 | GEN | - | Core model | Distinct jobs: reference images give loose identity/style/object guidance across many shots; a start frame gives exact pixel-level truth for one shot; the video prompt deals only with motion and sound, never with references. Related: the video prompt as motion and sound only is WD-IDEA-GEN-187. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-011 | GEN | idea | Core model | Video models that take a first frame cannot also take reference images, so the generated start frame is the sole environment anchor for video; frame quality and location matching directly determine video background quality.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-012 | GEN | impl | Prompt assembly | The system assembles and injects the right global or local context before every AI call; the user never pastes context (see 04 for global-to-local layering).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-014 | GEN | planned | Prompt assembly | Frame and video prompts are structured and deterministic, built from a template rather than free-form paragraphs; the same inputs always give the same structure, and they draw on all Stage 1–8 work. Treated as the keystone that shot chaining and adaptive descriptions build on.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-015 | GEN | planned | Prompt assembly | Assembly inputs: the scene's script excerpt (narrative context), master asset descriptions and images, the shot list (action, dialogue, camera, duration, characters, setting), scene asset instances (descriptions, status tags, scene images), style capsule directives, and transformation events for within-shot visual change.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-016 | GEN | - | Prompt assembly | Conceived: the prompt stage receives prompt-ready context: final asset references, shot-level asset relevance, storyboard/reference image notes, and frame- and video-prompt inputs. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-018 | GEN | idea | Prompt assembly | Open concern: avoid feeding too much irrelevant intake context into generation prompts. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-019 | GEN | alt | Prompt assembly › Options: who writes the prompt | Deterministic template interpolation builds the prompt's structural scaffold; the LLM only fills creative gaps. ‹src: 3.8-phase3-structured-prompts-and-limits›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-020 | GEN | alt,voice | Prompt assembly › Options: who writes the prompt | A reasoning step interprets intent, because users phrase requests in many ways and deterministic rules cannot reliably turn unstructured input into structured output. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-021 | GEN | idea | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | A prompt engine turns the user's rough, "sloppy" request into a precise, structured, token-efficient generation request; its goal is to elevate the intent with expert cinematic, stylistic and audio language, not merely translate it. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-022 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | The engine classifies each request: text-to-video (default), image-to-video (valid only if an image is actually supplied), or multi-shot sequence (detected from cues like "scene 1", "shot 2", "then cut to", numbered lists); multi-shot requests automatically get a define-once character/style structure.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-023 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Define once, reference many: a character, style or setting is defined a single time and referenced by name/alias in every shot, enforcing consistency across a sequence and saving tokens.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-024 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | The engine extracts from free text: subject, action (including micro-expressions), context, style, cinematography, ambiance/lighting, audio (split into dialogue, soundscape, SFX, music) and negative directives ("no…", "without…").
- WD-IDEA-GEN-025 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Abstract emotions and vague requests become concrete physical and sensory cues, inferred from the shot's action and dialogue: "sad man" → slumped shoulders, downcast eyes, cool desaturated palette, diffuse overcast light; "grief" → slumped shoulders, red-rimmed eyes, a loosely held crumpled photograph; "a nice video of a watch" → product hero shot with studio lighting and an arc move; "fly through this building" → a specific camera path.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-026 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | When the user omits a component (e.g. camera angle), the AI applies a sensible neutral default and records the assumption visibly; no AI assumption is silently inserted.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-027 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Factual grounding: the engine never invents model parameters or capabilities; it works only from a maintained reference of documented capabilities and limits, kept current as models change.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-028 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Ask vs act: a fatally ambiguous, too-vague or impossible request (unsupported ratio or duration, image-to-video without an image) gets a helpful reply asking for subject/action/context detail or offering a workaround (generate 16:9 for later cropping; split into several shots).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-029 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Where seed and guidance controls aren't exposed, a request for "more control" ("make it more realistic") is met by more precise cinematic and lighting language, not parameter tuning.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-030 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Two engine modes: interactive/debug, where the generated prompt carries comments explaining reasoning and assumptions, and production, which strips comments to save tokens; each transformation can show its reasoning beside the result.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-031 | GEN | - | Prompt engine: from rough intent to precise request | Plain, concrete language: replace poetic metaphor with visual specifics; prefer one precise technical term (e.g. "chiaroscuro") over a long paraphrase.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-032 | GEN | impl | Frame prompts | A frame prompt describes a frozen photograph: no action verbs, movement, dialogue or sound; one dense paragraph.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-033 | GEN | - | Frame prompts | Frame-prompt-only content: character appearance, environment/set design, props, spatial relationships, camera position/framing/angle/lens, lighting setup, color palette, film style, depth of field/bokeh, weather and atmospheric visuals.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-036 | GEN | - | Frame prompts | Conceived: frame prompts are broken into labeled components (camera, spatial layout, character descriptions, setting, style cues) and list the frames and assets they reference for continuity.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-037 | GEN | alt,impl | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Five-part formula: (1) camera position and framing — shot type, angle, lens/depth of field, no movement; (2) subject appearance; (3) placement in frame and static pose, eye-line, weight; (4) environment and props with depth layers, atmosphere, time of day; (5) lighting, palette, contrast, film stock, mood.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-038 | GEN | alt | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Camera spec first, then spatial layout of characters and setting, then asset descriptions verbatim, then action only where it matters to a still frame, then style capsule cues. ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-040 | GEN | planned | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Subject appearance comes from each asset's scene-specific effective description (not just the master), with status tags as visual condition modifiers; conditions such as damage or dirt are described in detail (see 04).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-041 | GEN | impl | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | When a character has an attached reference image, the frame prompt describes them with extra precision and ties them to the image by a short trait summary ("the red-haired woman"); prompt text never contains image-slot labels like "Image #1".
- WD-IDEA-GEN-042 | GEN | planned,voice | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Body language for every visible character: posture, hand position/gesture, a specific facial expression ("jaw clenched, brow furrowed", not "angry"), gaze direction and physical state markers (sweating, trembling). The same rule governs shot action descriptions written upstream.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-043 | GEN | planned,voice | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Positioning and choreography: where characters and props sit relative to each other (thirds, depth planes, scale, interaction geometry), not just their presence; multi-character shots need explicit blocking ("A stands frame-left facing right, B sits frame-center looking up at A"). Blocking detail also covers body orientation to camera, hands, head tilt, weight and eyeline.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-045 | GEN | voice | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | The model must infer what a framing implies (an over-the-shoulder shows only the foreground character's back, never his face; the other character faces forward); where it can't, the system states those implications explicitly in the prompt. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-046 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | A frame prompt describes the complete lighting state (key light direction, hardness, color temperature; fill; rim/back; visible practicals; ambient base) and style (palette, contrast, film stock, aesthetic, grade).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-047 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Lighting vocabulary maps to mood: three-point (clean), high-key (upbeat), low-key (mystery/drama), Rembrandt, chiaroscuro (noir), golden hour (romantic/nostalgic), blue hour (serene/melancholic).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-048 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Lighting continuity: standardize 2–3 lighting/color descriptors across all frame prompts in a sequence so lighting doesn't shift between clips.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-049 | GEN | planned | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Style capsule directives (design pillars, descriptor strings, negative constraints) supply the lighting/color/style part, and master image descriptions are referenced when available (see 06 for capsules).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-050 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | For non-photoreal styles (felt, yarn, stop-motion, claymation, anime), material and style words saturate subject, environment, motion quality and even soundscape.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-051 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Diegetic on-screen text (e.g. a phone screen in the scene) can be specified as part of the composition, distinct from unwanted subtitle overlays.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-052 | GEN | - | Frame prompts › Options: frame prompt order | Frame prompt checklist: appearance fully described; camera/angle/lens specified; spatial layout explicit; environment detailed; lighting and palette defined; no action, dialogue or sound; character block identical across shots; aspect ratio matches the platform; reference images prepared.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-055 | GEN | impl | End frames | End frames have their own prompts, generated and regenerated separately; the end frame can be reviewed and edited, and both frames go to video generation.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-058 | GEN | impl | Video prompts | The video prompt assumes the anchor frame(s) already carry all visual truth: it never describes appearance, environment, lighting, color or film aesthetic, only what happens when you press play, and stays lean (at most ~500 characters). The original spec is softer: video prompts prioritize motion, dialogue, performance and sound, and avoid restating visual attributes unless required for action clarity, an exception to the rule above.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-059 | GEN | - | Video prompts | Video-prompt-only content: action/movement, dialogue and voice performance, camera movement, sound effects, ambient audio, behavior and emotional performance, timing/pacing, interactions between subjects.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-060 | GEN | alt,impl | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Four parts: (1) camera movement only, "Static camera" when none; (2) action and performance as verbs, one major action per shot; (3) exact dialogue in quotes with speaker and voice direction; (4) sound design. Nothing else. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory, Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide, 3.8-phase3-structured-prompts-and-limits›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-061 | GEN | alt | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Opens with an "Audio:" section (dialogue plus SFX), then movement dynamics (how characters move, not how they look), then timing cues ("pause for 2 seconds"). ‹src: AI-agent-registry-context-flow-architecture›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-062 | GEN | alt | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Two parts: a visual section (camera, character, action, plus style injection) and an audio section ("Audio: [SFX]. Character [Name] speaks: '[line]'"). ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-063 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Exception: a short visual detail is allowed only to identify which subject or object the action applies to ("the red folder" when several folders are visible). Test: if removing it would not make the action ambiguous, remove it.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-064 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Self-test: a video prompt should read like a silent-movie stage direction plus audio cues.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-065 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Action is written as verbs and behaviors, never appearances; camera movement and subject action are described together so their relative timing is clear ("dolly in as she leans forward and whispers").
- WD-IDEA-GEN-067 | GEN | idea | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | For audio-driven projects (e.g. podcast-to-video), video prompts put audio synchronization first, carry timing cues and treat visuals as secondary to the audio narrative. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-068 | GEN | voice | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Video prompts must amply describe facial expression, blocking, or at least framing. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-069 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | When animating a supplied image, the prompt focuses on the desired motion and tells the model to maintain the image's appearance, style and lighting. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-070 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Each video prompt is tagged with the video models it is compatible with and an estimated length.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-071 | GEN | - | Video prompts › Options: video prompt structure | Video prompt checklist: one clear primary action; movement or "static" stated; dialogue quoted with speaker, accent, tone, delivery; SFX for key sounds; ambient audio defined; duration fits complexity; no appearance/environment/lighting/color text; negative prompt prepared.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-072 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Structured per-shot prompt fields: subject, action, context, style; cinematography (shot type, angle, movement, lighting); audio (dialogue, soundscape, SFX, music); config (negative prompt, aspect ratio, duration, resolution, audio on/off, person-generation safety level); optional reference image for image-to-video. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-073 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Negative prompts list unwanted elements descriptively ("wall, frame"), not as instructions ("no walls"). Standard quality negatives: subtitles, captions, watermark, text overlays, logo, blur, low resolution, artifacts, distorted hands, compression noise, camera shake.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-074 | GEN | idea | Model-specific technique | Automatically add a "no subtitles" negative to any generation containing dialogue, because video models hallucinate burned-in subtitles when dialogue is present. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-075 | GEN | idea | Model-specific technique | Audio generation can be switched on or off per request. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-076 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | A person-generation safety setting (disallow, adults only, all), which differs between image-to-video and text-to-video and by region.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-077 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | When the model can't natively output a deliverable ratio (9:16, 1:1), generate in a supported ratio with a centered composition planned for cropping, and flag the crop requirement.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-078 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Looping clips: describe identical start and end states and continuous cyclical motion so the clip edits into a seamless loop.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-079 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Slow motion comes from temporal keywords ("high-speed camera", "1000 fps effect") and granular physics description, including time-stretched audio.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-080 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Over-constrained prompts that keep producing the same artifacts: reduce adjectives in the frame prompt or change the seed; seeds also give reproducibility where the model exposes them.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-081 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Reproducibility limit: without a seed, regenerating from the same prompt won't give an identical result; consistency comes from specific, unambiguous prompts, and users should expect variance on regeneration.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-082 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Known model weaknesses to plan around: multi-action fragmentation, identity drift without frame anchors or references, unreliable hands in complex scenes, imperfect lip-sync, native audio unfit for precise work.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-083 | GEN | - | Model-specific technique | Troubleshooting map (symptom → cause → fix): smeared motion → too many actions (simplify, shorten); identity drift → inconsistent character blocks (copy identical blocks, use refs); lighting shifts → differing lighting text (standardize); camera mismatch → vague terms (be exact); visual details ignored → too much visual text in the video prompt (strip it).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-084 | GEN | impl | Prompt length and validation | Frame and video prompts have separate length thresholds, each with a warning and an error level (legacy-implemented: frame paragraph near 1,200 characters, video near 500). The planned direction is warn-only with the limit raised (WD-IDEA-GEN-091).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-085 | GEN | - | Prompt length and validation | Validation before sending: length checks, forbidden-character checks, a preview, and schema validation of allowed values (aspect ratio, resolution, model) plus a dialogue-format check.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-086 | GEN | - | Prompt length and validation | The system warns, without blocking, when prompt verbosity doesn't suit the target model; a sanity checker flags prompt/model mismatches.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-087 | GEN | impl | Prompt length and validation | Video provider compatibility is filtered by prompt length (a provider offered only for short-enough prompts), so changing length limits means revisiting which providers a prompt fits.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-088 | GEN | idea | Prompt length and validation | Unresolved: whether longer prompts actually degrade image quality with current providers; test empirically before committing to higher limits.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-089 | GEN | rejected | Prompt length and validation › Options: prompt length handling | Hard cap that silently truncates prompts mid-sentence. Replaced by warn-only thresholds.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-090 | GEN | alt,voice | Prompt length and validation › Options: prompt length handling | Remove the length limit entirely.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-091 | GEN | alt,voice,planned | Prompt length and validation › Options: prompt length handling | Raise the limit and keep thresholds only as non-blocking amber warnings ("Prompt exceeds recommended length (X/1200 chars) — longer prompts may reduce generation quality"); prompts go through in full and the user decides whether to shorten. The creator's pick.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-092 | GEN | alt | Prompt length and validation › Options: redundancy when a reference image is attached | Trim an asset's prompt description to scene-specific differences ("torn, soaking wet") plus spatial info, dropping what the attached image already shows (the blue dress). ‹src: 3.7-phase-4-frame-prompt-quality›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-093 | GEN | alt | Prompt length and validation › Options: redundancy when a reference image is attached | Raise length limits and accept the redundancy.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-094 | GEN | future,voice | Prompt length and validation › Options: redundancy when a reference image is attached | Dynamic trimming: detect visual details already conveyed by attached references and remove them from the text automatically for leaner, context-aware prompts.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-097 | GEN | planned | Shot chaining and prompt staleness | Chaining amplifies whatever prompt quality exists, so frame prompt quality is improved first.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-098 | GEN | planned | Shot chaining and prompt staleness | When a shot's start frame is regenerated, edited, approved or swapped, its video prompt is flagged "Frame has changed since this prompt was generated". Per-shot "Update prompt for new frame" and bulk "Update all stale prompts" regenerate it from the new frame's content, showing a diff to accept, reject or edit; the user may keep the original.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-100 | GEN | - | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Conceived: Stage 9 labels clearly which prompt drives the image, which drives the video, and which logic is system-owned and non-editable, to prevent wrong edits.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-101 | GEN | - | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Conceived: frame prompts are read-only by default with an optional manual-edit toggle; video prompts are always editable. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-102 | GEN | impl | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Prompts can be generated for all shots, regenerated for all, or regenerated for one shot; partial failures report how many succeeded and failed.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-103 | GEN | impl | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Frame prompt correction: the user writes a targeted correction and the AI applies it surgically, leaving the rest of the prompt identical.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-104 | GEN | - | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Conceived: per-shot model compatibility tags show whether a shot needs a start frame only or start and end frames, and which video models fit.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-107 | GEN | voice | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | A frame tool with all of the shot's assets beside the prompt so the user can "just roll": e.g. a Mexican standoff composed from two pre-generated characters (one facing left, one right, ten feet apart, each with a distinct gun) plus background and gun assets. ‹src: new-tasks›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-108 | GEN | planned | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | Per-shot reference image upload (drag and drop, several per shot, each captioned, storyboard sheets supported) passed to frame and video generation as added context.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-109 | GEN | impl | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | No dedicated AI instructions exist for Stage 1, Stage 6 or Stages 10–12; they reuse earlier AI services or simpler prompts. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-110 | GEN | future,voice | Stage 9: prompt review and editing | A "Custom System Prompts" sidebar tab to view and change the system prompts behind generated content; the creator thinks it probably unneeded. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-114 | GEN | voice | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Asset images must actually be fed into frame generation, or there is no point to them; continuity between shots comes from using asset images for later frames. The creator observed frames that looked nothing like the scene-instance images, "generic slop" with no master influence. ‹src: new-tasks, 3.8-phase1-asset-inheritance-fix›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-115 | GEN | voice,impl | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | The reference sent for a character is its scene-specific instance image (e.g. the gnome variant), not the original master; Stage 8 scene images flow through to Stage 10 so frames resemble the instances.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-119 | GEN | planned,impl | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Every attached reference has an explicit semantic role plus a human-readable reason and source. Roles: location direction main, location establishing context, location fallback, continuity base frame, blocking composition, blocking start frame, blocking end frame, character identity, prop identity, style reference. Related: the reference stack panel and per-shot card layout are WD-IDEA-LOC-201.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-122 | GEN | - | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Research finding: 2–3 reference images is the sweet spot; quality degrades beyond 3–4.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-124 | GEN | voice | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Observed failure, information overload: given many asset and reference images, the model tries to satisfy every input instead of discarding irrelevant ones (an OTS of SpongeBob grabbing Squidward's shirt came out as a generic medium shot, both facing camera, oversized register, whole restaurant visible). ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-125 | GEN | voice | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Generation must prioritize inputs intelligently, discard reference information not valuable for the specific shot, balance competing inputs and "make the logical jumps" rather than reproducing each reference literally. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-127 | GEN | planned | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Reference-image failures are surfaced ("X of Y asset references failed to load — frames may not reflect your assets") instead of silently generating text-only.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-128 | GEN | planned | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Warn about the silent mismatch where prompt text mentions an asset but no image accompanies it (a scene asset with a description but no image).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-130 | GEN | planned | Reference images: roles, priority, budget | Output is checked against references for every asset type: characters look like their master/instance, location settings match, key props are visible.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-136 | GEN | impl | Generation package and preview parity | Parity contract: the references Stage 9 previews are exactly those Stage 10 sends, in the same order with the same roles, including a selected continuity base in first position; treated as mandatory. Related: the generation-side rule behind the Stage 10 check in WD-IDEA-UX-239, which warns when the reference package differs from the Stage 9 preview.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-137 | GEN | planned | Generation package and preview parity | Risk: the UI showing one background while generation sends a different image destroys user trust immediately.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-138 | GEN | planned | Generation package and preview parity | Regenerating a frame reuses the same package unless the user explicitly overrides references; manual overrides keep their roles and stay intentional and visible.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-139 | GEN | impl | Generation package and preview parity | The reference set and roles chosen at prompt-generation time are stored per shot and reused at frame generation.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-142 | GEN | impl | Location references at frame time | Delta descriptions: when the reference angle or distance differs from the shot, the prompt says how the needed view differs and asks the model to keep the same space but reframe ("reference is eye-level kitchen; shoot from above"). The delta keeps palette, architecture and layout; camera-angle details are always described in the prompt, even when the reference matches. An imperfect match is also flagged in the coverage advisory (WD-IDEA-LOC-203).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-150 | GEN | impl | Location references at frame time | Graceful degradation: when no view matches the shot's angle, use the closest direction, state the delta explicitly ("reference shows the kitchen at eye level; frame this from a HIGH ANGLE looking down; keep palette, architecture, layout"), and flag it in the coverage advisory so the user can create the missing view before spending credits.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-154 | GEN | impl | Continuity generation modes | Start-frame continuity modes: match (copies the prior frame's pixels), camera change (recomposes the prior frame for a new camera using continuity references), reuse/edit from a selected base, and fresh (see 04). Related: the match, camera change and none classification with AI recommendation and user override is WD-IDEA-CONT-139; reuse or edit from a base is WD-IDEA-CONT-199 and WD-IDEA-CONT-203; the per-shot mode badge is WD-IDEA-UX-222.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-157 | GEN | impl | Continuity generation modes | If the base shares the shot's direction, its framing is preserved exactly; if not, framing adapts while location identity and lighting are kept.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-160 | GEN | voice | Continuity generation modes | Drag-and-drop frame reuse: drag a previously generated frame from any shot, earlier or later, in this or another scene, into the frame being worked on as a background/composition base, even if that asset was never designated in Stages 7–9; used as-is (expected rare) or, mostly, as a starting point. ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink, ._1-scratchpad›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-161 | GEN | voice | Continuity generation modes | From a pulled-in frame, the target is reached by prompt-editing that image (character now holds the object, now standing, new character added, blood on the wall, room on fire), swapping assets, rather than generating from blank; only the delta changes. ‹src: post-clean-location-rethink›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-162 | GEN | voice,planned | Continuity generation modes | Frame panels get an "edit image" action and a regenerate-from-prompt that can use the selected image; the full frame prompt remains the key generation prompt, unlike edit image's short "change it like this" instruction. ‹src: ._1-scratchpad›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-164 | GEN | - | Continuity generation modes | Cost rationale for analyzing the reference before a camera change: one vision call (~$0.01–0.03, ~2–3 s) against 10–30 s+ for the frame itself, so users won't notice.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-168 | GEN | future | Continuity generation modes › Options: when to analyze the reference frame | Region inpainting: select a region of a frame (e.g. a face) and give a local instruction ("change expression from surprise to neutral") to fix a continuity break without regenerating the whole frame. One plan deferred it to the final nice-to-have phase. Related: WD-IDEA-GEN-195 lists a mask-plus-prompt inpaint as a current frame action; this entry is the deferred region-editing plan, with region selection tools in WD-IDEA-GEN-169.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-169 | GEN | future | Continuity generation modes › Options: when to analyze the reference frame | Inpainting region tools: brush, rectangle and ellipse for quick selection, lasso plus brush for fine work (a "character's face" workflow), and a tool picker.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-170 | GEN | future | Continuity generation modes › Options: when to analyze the reference frame | Continuity drift detector: after frames are approved, an AI compares consecutive frames for character appearance (clothing, hair, face), setting elements (furniture, lighting, background) and props (presence, position).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-173 | GEN | - | Blocking and composition references | Blocking references control camera framing, subject placement, pose, screen direction and visible/occluded relationships, but never override character identity, prop identity, lighting, texture, final location appearance or art direction unless explicitly marked.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-174 | GEN | voice | Blocking and composition references | Composition authority: the blocking still defines framing, angle and crop; the location image only informs the background's look and content. A wide, high-angle location reference must not pull a tight, intimate shot back toward the wide view. ‹src: voice-message-5-2›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-176 | GEN | - | Blocking and composition references | Conceived: the frame stage accepts visual anchors: uploaded storyboard frames, user-made stills, blocking captures, rough sketches, and prior approved frames to reuse and edit. Sketch anchors are the frame-stage side of WD-IDEA-SHOT-097 and WD-IDEA-SHOT-098.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-179 | GEN | voice | Blocking and composition references | Models may have improved enough to use blocking references correctly; re-test the core bet against current models.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-182 | GEN | planned | Blocking and composition references › Options: how a blocking capture enters frame generation | 3D world generation for blocking is lazy: it runs on first entering the studio for a location, never pre-emptively, to avoid spending on locations never blocked. Related: the film-studio model in WD-IDEA-LOC-255.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-183 | GEN | impl | Blocking and composition references › Options: how a blocking capture enters frame generation | World generation offers a quality/cost choice: fast cheap model (~30–45 s, ~$0.15) vs high quality (~5–10 min, ~$1.50); density presets (quick preview / balanced / full); optional seed for reproducible worlds.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-184 | GEN | idea | Blocking and composition references › Options: how a blocking capture enters frame generation | Iterate with the cheap world model, use the high-quality one only for hero/demo worlds, and reuse generated worlds rather than regenerating.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-186 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Conceived: the start-only path is first-class: end frames are skipped and hidden in the frame UI, frame dependencies adapt, and costs exclude them. Related: the generation side of WD-IDEA-SHOT-029 and WD-IDEA-FLOW-057; the end-frame reference rules in WD-IDEA-CONT-115 and WD-IDEA-CONT-116 do not apply in this mode.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-187 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | The video prompt works in either mode; with start + end frames it describes only the movement, action and audio between them (e.g. a 180° arc from in front of a singer to behind her, looking at the crowd).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-188 | GEN | impl | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Generation scope: all needed frames at once, one shot's frames, start frames only, or end frames.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-189 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Frame generation starts with an explicit mode choice: Quick Mode [future] (bulk-generate all frames in a grid with progress and bulk review; faster, higher risk of wasted credits) or Control Mode (sequential; a start frame must be approved before its end frame; approve-before-advance).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-190 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Control Mode is the default recommendation for cost-sensitive users; the chosen mode feeds frame dependencies and cost estimates.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-191 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Conceived: a frame dependency manager enforces correct frame chaining.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-192 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Frames are reviewed and approved shot by shot; frame regeneration guidance is optional and a plain re-roll stays available.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-193 | GEN | impl | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Three ways to regenerate a start or end frame: plain re-roll, regenerate with a text correction, or regenerate from a manually edited prompt.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-194 | GEN | impl | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Users can upload their own image as a start or end frame instead of generating.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-195 | GEN | impl | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Frame actions: approve, compare frames side by side, inpaint a region with a mask plus a prompt; frame and video prompt text remain editable here.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-196 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Conceived: frames and videos are approved separately per shot; each frame tracks how many times it was regenerated.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-198 | GEN | impl | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | The frame panel includes a warning dialog with a "Don't show again" option.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-199 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | Expected frame quality: start and end frames keep characters and setting consistent; in action shots positions change realistically and props show state changes; continuity holds across shot and scene boundaries; complex camera angles are faithful.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-200 | GEN | - | Stage 10: frame generation workflow | For audio-driven projects, frames show hosts in natural speaking positions and visual aids at the right timestamps.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-201 | GEN | impl,voice | "Use as reference" on regeneration | When an image is regenerated, a "Use as reference" toggle sends the current image as a style and composition reference so the result stays close to what the user has; off means a fresh generation from the description only. Related: the toggle's UI treatment is WD-IDEA-UX-281; the default question is settled differently in WD-IDEA-UX-296 and WD-IDEA-GEN-207.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-202 | GEN | impl,voice | "Use as reference" on regeneration | The toggle appears only when an image already exists in that component (regeneration, never first generation).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-203 | GEN | impl,voice | "Use as reference" on regeneration | Available at four regeneration points: the asset image upload review (Stages 5/8), Stage 8 scene-asset image, Stage 8 transformation post-image, and Stage 10 start/end frames; extending it to frames (regenerate from a previously uploaded image) and other fitting places was the creator's ask.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-204 | GEN | impl | "Use as reference" on regeneration | In Stage 10 the toggle applies to all three regeneration modes (re-roll, text correction, edited prompt).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-205 | GEN | impl | "Use as reference" on regeneration | The user-supplied current image is treated as a style/composition guide, separate from the identity references for characters and props, and gets the highest priority among images sent.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-206 | GEN | impl | "Use as reference" on regeneration | Transformation post-image generation already uses a default reference image; turning the toggle on replaces it with the current post-image.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-207 | GEN | alt,impl,voice | "Use as reference" on regeneration › Options: reference toggle default | ON in the upload review modal, OFF everywhere else (Stage 8 scene asset, Stage 8 transformation, Stage 10 frames).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-208 | GEN | alt | "Use as reference" on regeneration › Options: reference toggle default | One consistent default everywhere, recommended ON since the user presumably likes the current image.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-209 | GEN | future | "Use as reference" on regeneration › Options: reference toggle default | A session-wide toggle at the top of frame generation, "Use existing frames as reference for regenerations", setting the default for every regeneration.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-210 | GEN | idea | Asset and location image generation | Edit Image is image-to-image with text guidance; its quality depends on provider support for reference plus instructions, so verify before committing to the UX (see 06 for the image actions).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-211 | GEN | future | Asset and location image generation | If automatic white background produces artifacts for some asset types, make it user-toggleable.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-212 | GEN | future,voice | Asset and location image generation | Dedicated AI background removal (segmentation, possibly in-browser) replacing generation-based removal: pixel-perfect, preserves the original, costs no credits, takes seconds instead of 30–60 s, cannot reinterpret the subject; alpha converted to white when needed.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-213 | GEN | future | Asset and location image generation | Build the dedicated remover after users validate generation-based removal, or sooner if they report quality problems.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-214 | GEN | impl | Asset and location image generation | Bulk generation for selected scene assets shows a cost confirmation, then progress; partial failures are reported and failed items can be regenerated one by one.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-224 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | "Confirm & Render" queues video generation. After it, queued jobs are not edited in place; later changes go back through the shot list or frames and invalidate the affected work.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-225 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | A shot can have several video attempts, kept as versions.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-226 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Conceived: video renders show status (queued, rendering, complete, failed) and an error message on failure.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-227 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Conceived: long renders tolerate completion delays of 30–45 minutes; only final outcomes (succeeded, failed, cancelled, timeout) are acted on, not partial progress.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-228 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Each provider has a timeout window (e.g. ~20 min, ~45 min for video, ~2 min for audio); a render that misses it is marked timed out and treated as failed.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-229 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Conceived: when a render completes or fails, one normalized event triggers the next pipeline step and notifies the user. Related: completion notification is WD-IDEA-UX-354 and the toast and email in WD-IDEA-AV-052.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-230 | GEN | idea | Video generation and rendering | Audio renders (e.g. synthesized voice) are render jobs like video, with "audio" as the output type, and follow the same completion contract. ‹src: API-integration-async-contract›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-231 | GEN | future | Video generation and rendering | Push-style completion reporting instead of the client polling.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-232 | GEN | future | Video generation and rendering | Background handling for long generations: status, retry, cancellation, prioritization, batching. Related: the generation side of the background rendering item WD-IDEA-AV-052.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-233 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Latency targets: text iterations under 5 s, frame pairs under 15 s, video in the background with notifications.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-234 | GEN | impl | Video generation and rendering | The UI enforces each provider's limits (e.g. shot duration only 4, 6 or 8 seconds for one provider; other values fail).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-236 | GEN | future | Video generation and rendering | Object-level video editing: add or remove an object in a generated video (the available tool loses audio when doing so).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-237 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Draft vs final quality tiers: iterate at lower resolution (720p), render finals at 1080p.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-238 | GEN | - | Video generation and rendering | Model tier choice: a faster, cheaper video model vs the standard one (fast for casual/social output).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-239 | GEN | future | Video generation and rendering | Scene preview: fast low-res animatic-style previews with quick frame interpolation, later upgraded to the final render.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-240 | GEN | impl | Generation jobs and reliability | Image generation runs as tracked async jobs of several kinds: master asset images, scene asset images, start frames, end frames, and inpainting.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-241 | GEN | impl | Generation jobs and reliability | A job moves through queued, processing, generating, uploading, then completed or failed; a failure records the step where it happened.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-242 | GEN | impl | Generation jobs and reliability | Automatic retries (default max 3) are counted separately from user-initiated retries; errors are classified as temporary, permanent, rate limit or authorization.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-243 | GEN | impl | Generation jobs and reliability | Generation requests are idempotent: a double click or duplicate submission never creates or charges a second job.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-244 | GEN | - | Generation jobs and reliability | When a provider returns several output variants, one is chosen as primary by project rules.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-245 | GEN | - | Providers and models | Multiple LLM providers with cost-aware routing, no hard-coded assumptions about any provider, and graceful fallback when a service is down.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-246 | GEN | impl | Providers and models | All image and video generation goes through a provider-neutral interface with no fixed assumptions about prompt format or model behavior; image providers are swappable and text generation supports several vendors.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-247 | GEN | impl | Providers and models | The video pipeline is built around frame-to-video generation with one named provider; another is noted as having a clip time limit. ‹src: system-prompts-inventory›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-248 | GEN | future,voice | Providers and models | Users choose among video providers; MVP ships with one provider (Google Veo 3) but the architecture stays ready for more; the options named for later are Sora and Runway.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-249 | GEN | planned | Providers and models | Provider-specific prompt formatting: one format per video provider, a generic default, extensible as providers are added (see also provider-tailored pipeline settings in 02). Veo 3 was the first target format; Sora, Runway and Kling are named as future extensions.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-250 | GEN | alt | Providers and models | An external automation layer may run expensive async pipelines (trigger render, wait, handle callback, notify) but must never hold creative or pipeline logic.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-295 | GEN | future | Providers and models | Orchestration is explicit and code-driven, deliberately structured so it can later migrate to a durable workflow engine (Temporal.io) if workflows become long-running or need high fault tolerance. ‹src: tech-stack, inheritance_contract›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-252 | GEN | future | Providers and models | Multi-model routing: capability matrix, cost/quality optimizer, automatic model selection with manual override, model performance tracking.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-253 | GEN | future | Providers and models | Enterprise custom models: register private models, route generation to them, benchmark, configure costs, control access. Related: WD-IDEA-ALT-016 (enterprise administration).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-255 | GEN | alt | Providers and models › Options: prompt serialization format | YAML (preferred: readable, comments, define-once anchors).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-256 | GEN | alt | Providers and models › Options: prompt serialization format | JSON, for validation.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-257 | GEN | alt | Providers and models › Options: prompt serialization format | XML-tagged sections, for enterprise endpoints.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-258 | GEN | alt | Providers and models › Options: prompt serialization format | Labeled structured natural language ("Subject: … Action: … Style: …") as a simple fallback.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-259 | GEN | - | Cost of generation | Conceived: generation is paid in credits per second of video, per image and per LLM token; every frame, video and image job records what it cost.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-260 | GEN | - | Cost of generation | Cost rules (what should happen) are kept separate from cost outcomes (what did happen); rules give a base cost plus a per-unit cost (scene, shot, frame, video, token) per stage and model, so estimates are predictable and auditable.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-262 | GEN | - | Cost of generation | Conceived: each generation stores its estimated cost next to its actual cost.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-263 | GEN | - | Cost of generation | Conceived: cost awareness everywhere: frame generation, regeneration and bulk operations always show cost; every AI call is estimated and tracked; high-cost operations need explicit confirmation; Stage 11 totals by mode, frames per shot, and start-only vs start+end path. Related: the principle is stated in WD-IDEA-PHIL-054 and WD-IDEA-PHIL-055 and applied to invalidation in WD-IDEA-UX-070.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-264 | GEN | planned | Cost of generation | Estimated generation cost is shown on the review screen before a commit spends credits (see WD-IDEA-UX-116); a gateway before every generation action is WD-IDEA-GEN-265.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-265 | GEN | future,voice | Cost of generation | Show estimated credits before any generation action, with optional confirmation above a user-set threshold or by preference; deferred to a late nice-to-have phase.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-267 | GEN | impl,planned | Cost of generation | Credits are deducted per generation. Alerts on excessive spend are planned, not verified as shipped (see WD-IDEA-PHIL-057).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-268 | GEN | future | Cost of generation | Cost analytics: per-operation tracking, dashboard, per-user reports, budget alerts, anomaly detection. Related: budget alerts overlap WD-IDEA-PHIL-057 (planned) and per-user cost reporting overlaps WD-IDEA-UX-348 (future).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-271 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Conceived: a render plan states shot count, max variations per shot, max retries, and a mode (quality vs a speed/cost choice), derived from a speed-versus-cost setting chosen earlier in the pipeline. ‹src: security-credit-rate-limiting-strategy›
- WD-IDEA-GEN-272 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | No paid video render starts without three things: a credit reservation, a render budget, and a hard execution ceiling.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-273 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | A render is blocked if the user lacks credits for its estimated cost.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-274 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Reserve, then settle: estimated credits are held (~30 min) when a render starts and reconciled to actual cost afterward, refunding any overestimate; prevents double spending when completions arrive late.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-275 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | A failed or aborted render costs nothing if no output was produced; partial output is charged proportionally.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-276 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Each render has a ceiling on provider calls (e.g. 25) and wall-clock time (e.g. 12 min); hitting either aborts gracefully.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-277 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | An agentic shot generator has a per-shot cap on variations and retries; at the cap the shot is marked "accepted best effort" and may not be retried by the agent again.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-278 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Loop breaker: an identical prompt, model and parameters requested more than N times aborts the render and flags an agent loop.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-279 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Default rate limits, checked before any provider call: one active final-video render per user, two renders per project per hour, 60 calls per minute per provider.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-280 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Configuration kill switches: global disable of video rendering, per-provider disable (outage or cost spike), per-user freeze (abuse, suspicious activity, payment problems). Related: the provider-isolation rule in WD-IDEA-PHIL-063 and the provider-switching and maintenance items in WD-IDEA-ALT-017.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-281 | GEN | - | Spend safeguards | Conceived: each render keeps a permanent record of estimated, reserved and spent credits, provider call count and any abort reason, for cost analysis, billing disputes and debugging agent behavior.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-282 | GEN | impl | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Every output stores the exact final prompt sent, the prompt-template version and the user's regeneration guidance, so results are reproducible and auditable; a template becomes immutable once a generation has used it. Related: output provenance extends the lineage rule in WD-IDEA-PHIL-048; prompt templates are versioned artifacts per WD-IDEA-PHIL-003, and the guidance stored per version is WD-IDEA-STORY-074.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-283 | GEN | impl | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Writing-stage prompt templates are editable at runtime without shipping code, so live AI instructions may differ from shipped defaults.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-285 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Conceived: end-to-end traces show each stage's exact inputs and outputs; if a late video prompt is too verbose, the trace shows whether the shot list or the script caused it. Related: the product-side form of the AI tracing item in WD-IDEA-ALT-018.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-286 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | A failing generation can be replayed against identical data with a modified prompt (e.g. tighter style constraints), for fast iteration on style locks.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-287 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Conceived: style-injection consistency is evaluated automatically with LLM-as-judge metrics (faithfulness, relevance).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-288 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Evaluate each stage's AI agent alone against per-stage success criteria first, then run full pipeline runs to check context propagation, branching and invalidation (see 01; golden test projects are WD-IDEA-PHIL-061, success metrics are WD-IDEA-PHIL-060).
- WD-IDEA-GEN-289 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | When output misses criteria: identify the failing stage agent, review its instructions and input context, check for context propagation problems, compare with golden expectations, then update the agent.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-290 | GEN | - | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Post-generation QC for each clip: does the first video frame match the supplied start frame; is the primary action readable and complete; does camera movement match the prompt; are dialogue and SFX present and timed; has the character drifted from the frame anchor.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-291 | GEN | future | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Automatic quality scoring: image sharpness and artifacts, video quality, dialogue clarity, threshold alerts, automated retake suggestions.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-292 | GEN | future | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Smart regeneration: map detected quality issues to generation parameters, adjust prompts automatically, refine progressively, retry with variation, detect convergence.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-293 | GEN | future | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Automated QA before approval and before expensive generation: pre-flight checks, configurable quality gates, QA reports, exception approval.
- WD-IDEA-GEN-294 | GEN | future | Provenance, evaluation and quality control | Smart prompt optimization: an analysis agent suggests improvements, A/B tests prompts, offers a prompt library with examples, and scores prompt effectiveness. The planned evaluation of prompting strategies (A/B tests) is WD-IDEA-STORY-088; this item is the later in-product form.

## 09 Performance, Audio & Editing (09-PERFORMANCE-AUDIO-EDITING.md)

- WD-IDEA-AV-001 | AV | impl | Dialogue and performance direction | Dialogue in a video prompt carries the exact words in quotes, the speaker's identity, voice qualities (accent, tone, pitch, pace, emotion), delivery style (whispered, shouted, muttered, deadpan, sing-song, trembling), and pause/timing cues.
- WD-IDEA-AV-002 | AV | idea | Dialogue and performance direction | Speaker-attributed format ("<Character> says: '…'") improves speaker attribution and lip-sync in multi-character shots. Each speaker in a dialogue shot needs a distinct description and a label. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide, Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-003 | AV | idea | Dialogue and performance direction | Narration/voiceover is its own dialogue type: a narrator voice described by accent and tone, with the spoken line quoted. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-005 | AV | idea | Dialogue and performance direction | Performance direction covers pace, intensity, emotional shifts ("shifts from X to Y", "suddenly realizes"), body language (tenses, slumps, freezes), and eye behavior (locks eyes, glances away). One shot can carry a small emotional arc. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-008 | AV | alt,planned | Dialogue and performance direction › Options: where the spoken words come from | Script verbatim: the video prompt uses the exact words from the shot's dialogue. ‹src: 3.8-phase3-structured-prompts-and-limits›
- WD-IDEA-AV-009 | AV | alt | Dialogue and performance direction › Options: where the spoken words come from | Implicit dialogue: describe the speech act ("a man tells us his name") and let the model generate the words. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-010 | AV | alt,voice,future | Dialogue and performance direction › Options: where the spoken words come from | The recording wins: when the user's recorded performance goes off-script (for example, an ad-lib that is funnier), the video follows the recording, and the recorded line may override or update the scripted dialogue (see 05). ‹src: Issues, streamlined-input-process-consolidated, streamline-master-plan-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-AV-011 | AV | idea | Audio in the video prompt | Every video prompt in the target model's format carries an audio part: the shot's dialogue with delivery direction plus sound design for the action.
- WD-IDEA-AV-012 | AV | impl,planned | Audio in the video prompt | When the shot does not specify sound, sound design (SFX, ambience, and music) is inferred from scene context. ‹src: 3.8-phase3-structured-prompts-and-limits, 3.7-Do-last-maybe-redundant-structured-image-task›
- WD-IDEA-AV-017 | AV | impl | Sound design and native audio | Sound design has layers, each specified separately: SFX tied to specific actions (door slam, footsteps on gravel, ice clinking), the ambient soundscape (rain, traffic, ship hum), and musical cues where relevant. Music can be score or a diegetic source, such as a radio playing off-screen.
- WD-IDEA-AV-018 | AV | impl | Sound design and native audio | When a quiet beat matters, the prompt calls for silence explicitly instead of leaving it to chance.
- WD-IDEA-AV-019 | AV | idea | Sound design and native audio | Treat native generated audio as a starting point. There are three treatments, chosen per situation: / Keep it when the ambient sound fits the scene. / Refine it, for example by crossfading between clips so the audio does not jump at cuts. / Disable and replace it for brand work, precise dialogue, or music-driven pieces, and whenever the ambient sound or levels distract. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-020 | AV | idea | Sound design and native audio | Exact lip-sync is not guaranteed. When precise lip movement matters, plan to align the voiceover and retime it in post-production. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-021 | AV | future | Character voices | Voice gallery: each master character is linked to a voice profile (from an external voice service) in the asset editor during global asset definition, with a voice preview. This fills a gap: native video audio does not keep a character's voice consistent across shots (see 06). ‹src: project-overview, Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-AV-022 | AV | future | Character voices | The character's voice profile acts as a "voice seed": it goes into the audio section of every video prompt so the character sounds identical in every clip. ‹src: project-overview, Post-MVP-tasklist›
- WD-IDEA-AV-023 | AV | future | Character voices | A voice sample supplied at intake attaches to a character only when the user explicitly maps it. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-AV-024 | AV | idea,future | Character voices | Dialogue extracted from the script can build per-character voice profiles and feed audio generation and audio continuity checks. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-026 | AV | voice,future | Recorded performance and voice acting | A cloned voice or the user's own recorded dialogue drives each video generation, and the characters' mouths are lip-synced to the supplied audio. ‹src: Issues, Totally-New-Features›
- WD-IDEA-AV-027 | AV | voice,future | Recorded performance and voice acting | Lip-sync also carries the recording's underlying emotion (excited, neutral, bored, happy, overjoyed, terrified, and so on) into the on-screen performance. ‹src: Issues›
- WD-IDEA-AV-028 | AV | future | Recorded performance and voice acting | Voice-print transfer keeps the emotional performance and inflection of the recorded line when a different voice print is applied (voice cloning or transformation). ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated, streamline-master-plan-8-1›
- WD-IDEA-AV-029 | AV | future | Recorded performance and voice acting | A long recorded line can be split across several clips or shots without breaking audio continuity (see 07). ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated›
- WD-IDEA-AV-030 | AV | voice,future | Recorded performance and voice acting | "Voice-Actinator": the user shows a start frame, enters the action, picks which character speaks, and submits a voice-acting recording. The result is an animated clip matched exactly to the performance. It could be a standalone product or part of Aiuteur. ‹src: truly strech›
- WD-IDEA-AV-034 | AV | future | Audio-driven projects | Full audio-first projects (podcasts, audiobooks, lectures) generate visuals to match a transcript or narration timeline. This inverts the pipeline, so the script matters less than the audio's cadence, which makes it a fundamentally different product mode. ‹src: streamlined-input-process-consolidated, streamlined-input-analysis-5-10›
- WD-IDEA-AV-039 | AV | idea | Clip length, sequencing, and the audio stage | Clip length is capped (about 8 seconds per generation), so a longer narrative is assembled by sequencing short clips. A request longer than the cap is broken into several shots. Related: this cap is the constraint behind the 8-second shot default in WD-IDEA-SHOT-012. ‹src: veo-3-prompt-guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-040 | AV | idea | Clip length, sequencing, and the audio stage | Clip-extension workflow: generate a short base clip, review it and pick the strongest take, extend only the best clips using consistent prompt language, then stitch the clips together in an editor. The extend-shot feature itself belongs to shot and generation (see 07, 08); its extension parameters are in WD-IDEA-SHOT-096. ‹src: Frame_Video_Prompt_Format_Guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-042 | AV | idea | Clip length, sequencing, and the audio stage | One stage map put audio/music at Stage 9, with script dialogue as the source text for voice generation. Elsewhere Stage 9 is prompt review (see 02). Related: WD-IDEA-FLOW-005 and WD-IDEA-FLOW-010 hold conflicting stage maps; stage-map variants belong to 02. ‹src: tiptap-json-extraction-guide›
- WD-IDEA-AV-043 | AV | idea | Scene review player | Conceived: Stage 12 plays the whole scene, cut together from its individual clips, in a timeline player with shot markers. ‹src: project-overview, user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-AV-044 | AV | planned,voice | Scene review player | Side-by-side review layout: the scene player on the left and the shot list with start-frame thumbnails on the right. The timeline shows shot boundaries, supports scrubbing across the whole scene, highlights the current shot, and auto-advances between shots; clicking a shot jumps to it. The creator called this upgrade "huge" and said the thumbnail shot list is key. ‹src: Tickets, injecting-org-tickets-into-plan›
- WD-IDEA-AV-045 | AV | impl | Scene review player | The review stage tracks render jobs live and plays finished shots with play/pause, timeline seek, shot selection from a list, skip back/forward, mute, and fullscreen.
- WD-IDEA-AV-046 | AV | impl | Scene review player | Render jobs that fail can be retried. ‹src: current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-AV-047 | AV | impl | Scene review player | Empty and error states: if no video jobs were queued, review says so and offers a route back to the confirmation stage; if jobs cannot be loaded, it shows the error and a retry. ‹src: current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-AV-048 | AV | idea | Routing issues found in review | Review sorts issues into visual continuity, timing, dialogue/audio, or narrative structure. A failure-attribution agent maps each issue to the upstream stage most likely to fix it. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-049 | AV | impl | Routing issues found in review | Review offers three resolution paths (see 02): / Complete Scene: return to the Script Hub. / Minor visual issue: return to frames and regenerate the anchors. The video is re-queued and credits are charged again. / Major narrative or audio issue: return to the shot list. The changes may cascade through the asset, prompt, and frame stages.
- WD-IDEA-AV-051 | AV | impl | Routing issues found in review | Completing a scene returns the user to the Script Hub. If some render jobs failed, the scene still completes, with a warning. Related: WD-IDEA-FLOW-059 (Stage 12 completion rule). ‹src: current-app-user-flow›
- WD-IDEA-AV-052 | AV | future | Background rendering | Background rendering: after "Confirm & Render" the user can go straight back to the hub and work on other scenes. A progress bar stays in the sidebar, and completion or failure triggers an in-app toast and an email. Related: WD-IDEA-UX-354 (notification item), WD-IDEA-GEN-232 (long-job handling). ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-053 | AV | future | Background rendering | Render re-entry: clicking the completion notification, or the scene's "Complete" status in the hub, opens that scene's review player directly. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-054 | AV | idea | Quality bar for assembled output | Expected final video: duration matches the target (for audio-driven projects, exactly the audio's length); dialogue is audible and matches the script; movement is natural and continuous, with no jumps or artifacts; sound design supports the action; and the visuals complement the audio rather than distracting from it. ‹src: golden-test-evaluation-framework›
- WD-IDEA-AV-055 | AV | planned | Scene and project video export | Scene video export from the review stage: MP4 (H.264) at 720p, 1080p, or 4K. A settings dialog sets format, quality, and filename. Metadata (project, scene, timestamp) is embedded. The export runs as a background job with progress and cancel, and download links expire. Related: 4K depends on upscaling (WD-IDEA-AV-081, future); the MP4-only MVP scope is WD-IDEA-PHIL-097; expiring links relate to access control in WD-IDEA-ALT-013.
- WD-IDEA-AV-056 | AV | planned | Scene and project video export | Export history in project settings: the settings and timestamp of each export, re-download within 7 days, and export credits or costs tracked separately.
- WD-IDEA-AV-057 | AV | planned,future | Scene and project video export | Full-project export (optional for MVP): concatenate all scene videos with transition handling into a master timeline with scene markers, or export only selected scenes.
- WD-IDEA-AV-059 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff | Pro export includes every individual shot clip, encoded at high bitrate. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-060 | AV | future | Professional export and NLE handoff | Timeline descriptor (EDL/XML) for one-click import into DaVinci Resolve, Premiere Pro, or CapCut. It keeps the scene order from the hub, carries timecodes and markers at shot boundaries, and ships in an NLE-ready folder structure. Treated as a post-MVP professional feature. Related: WD-IDEA-PHIL-097 defers it to post-launch; the Project Wrap bullets WD-IDEA-AV-065 to WD-IDEA-AV-076 build on it.
- WD-IDEA-AV-061 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff | Bulk archive: a single zip of all approved artifacts from the active branch. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-062 | AV | future | Professional export and NLE handoff | Asset package: a ZIP of videos, frames, scripts, and a project metadata file, where the user picks which artifacts to include. Related: deferred to post-launch by WD-IDEA-PHIL-097. ‹src: missing_todos-not-sure, MVP-tasklist-v1.1›
- WD-IDEA-AV-063 | AV | alt | Professional export and NLE handoff › Options: clip naming convention | `[Project]_[Scene#]_[ShotID]_[Version#]` (e.g. Aiuteur_Sc01_Shot3A_v02.mp4), so clips stay organized inside editors. ‹src: project-overview›
- WD-IDEA-AV-064 | AV | alt | Professional export and NLE handoff › Options: clip naming convention | `PROJECT_SC###_SH###_TK##_ROLE_VERSION` (e.g. ALPHA_SC003_SH014_TK02_PRIMARY_v1), with zero-padded IDs. The filename is the single source of truth for NLE import. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-065 | AV | idea,future | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | "Project Wrap": a single export package of all Approved artifacts for editorial. It imports into DaVinci Resolve or Premiere Pro in one click, with correct clip names, metadata, bins, and an assembled timeline. Related: WD-IDEA-AV-060, WD-IDEA-PHIL-097. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-066 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | Success test: an editor can unzip the package and start cutting within minutes, with no relinking, renaming, or reorganizing. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-067 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | The export is deterministic: the same approved artifacts always produce the same folder layout, filenames, and timeline descriptors. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-068 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | Wrap contents: approved video clips; timeline descriptors for Resolve and Premiere, with an EDL as a frame-accurate fallback; a machine-readable manifest that is the authoritative record of the export (project, version, timestamp, approved artifacts, scene/shot/take mapping, timeline order), plus a mirror copy for programmatic use; a checksum per file for integrity checks; and a human-readable README. Media paths are relative, so the package is self-contained. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-069 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | Left out of the wrap: drafts, rejected artifacts, raw originals unless explicitly approved, and any color grade, VFX, or audio mix beyond reference tracks. ‹src: project-wrap-export-spec›
- WD-IDEA-AV-070 | AV | idea | Professional export and NLE handoff › Project Wrap | A clip is eligible only if it is Approved, has fixed scene and shot IDs, has locked in/out timecodes, and has a media file that resolves.
